The claim is the claim that the commentators recognized as the claim that is the sin: the sin of Iblīs is not the refusal of the prostration alone, but the claim that is the reason of the refusal. The refusal is the act, and the claim is the state, and the state is the state that is the form of the arrogance, and the arrogance is the state that the hadith names as the state that prevents the entry of the Garden:

> *"Whoever has in his heart the weight of an atom of arrogance (kibr) will not enter Paradise."* (Bukhari)

The hadith is the form of the teaching: the state of the arrogance is the state that is the form of the claim of the superiority, and the claim of the superiority is the claim of Iblīs, and the claim of Iblīs is the claim that is the form of the refusal. The two verses of this sūrah (7:12, 7:13) are the two forms of the same teaching: the claim of the superiority is the form of the fall, and the fall is the fall that is the form of the claim.

**The Argument of the Element**

The argument of Iblīs is the argument of the element: Iblīs says that God created him from fire, and God created Adam from clay, and the fire is better than the clay, and therefore Iblīs is better than Adam. The argument is the argument of the material cause, and the argument is the argument that the commentators analyzed with care. The element of Iblīs is the element of the fire, and the element of Adam is the element of the clay, and the two elements are the two elements that the Qur'an names in the same form in the verse of the creation of the jinn and the creation of the man:

> *"And He created the jinn from a smokeless fire."* (55:15)

> *"And We created man from clay, from an inert lump of clay."* (15:26)

The two verses are the two forms of the same statement, and the statement is the statement that Iblīs uses as the argument, and the argument is the argument that is the form of the claim. The commentators drew out the properties of the two elements with care, and the properties are the properties that are the form of the teaching. The fire is the element that is luminous, subtle, and characterized by the lightness and the levity, and the fire is the element that is associated with the fickleness, the recklessness, the restlessness, and the destructiveness, and the fire is the element that has the grandeur and the haughtiness, and the haughtiness is the quality that is consistent with the arrogance that leads to the perdition of Iblīs. The clay is the element that is base, heavy, dark, and low, and the clay is the element that has the properties of the gravity, the forbearance, the humility, and the stability, and the stability is the quality that is the quality that leads Adam, after the disobedience, to seek and receive the forgiveness of God.

**The Blindness of the Argument**

The argument of Iblīs is the argument that is blind to the other ways in which God ennobled Adam. The argument is the argument of the element, and the element is not the whole of the creation of Adam. The Qur'an names the other ways in which God ennobled Adam, and the other ways are the ways that Iblīs is blind to. God created Adam with His two Hands (38:75), and the creation with the two Hands is the creation that is the form of the special attention. God breathed into Adam of His Spirit (15:29, 38:72, 32:9), and the breathing of the Spirit is the breathing that is the form of the special gift. God taught Adam the names, all of them (2:31), and the teaching of the names is the teaching that is the form of the special knowledge. The three ways are the three ways that Iblīs is blind to, and the blindness is the blindness that is the form of the argument of the element: the argument of the element is the argument that sees the element and does not see the Spirit, and the Spirit is the Spirit that is the form of the ennoblement.

The argument of Iblīs is, in the form of the logic, an argument of the analogy, and the analogy is the form of the reasoning that is the form of the human reasoning. The later scholars of the law discussed the argument of Iblīs in the form of the discussion of the analogy as a source of the law, and some of the schools, notably the Jaʿfarī school, cited the argument of Iblīs as the reason for the rejection of the analogy, in the form of the saying: "The first to use analogical reasoning (qiyās) was Iblīs!" The response of the scholars who accept the analogy is the response that is worth stating: the problem of the argument of Iblīs is not the use of the analogy as such, but the use of the analogy in the form of the opposition to the direct command of God. The analogy, in the form of the source of the law, is always subordinate to the Quran and the Prophetic practice, and the argument of Iblīs is the argument that is the form of the analogy that is used to oppose the command, and the use of the analogy to oppose the command is the use that is not the use of the analogy in the form of the source of the law.

**The Two Elements and the Two Purifications**

The commentators drew out a detail of the argument that is worth stating, because it is the detail that makes the contrast of the two elements concrete in the form of the practice of the religion. In the context of the Islam, the elements of the clay — the water and the earth — are the means of the ritual purification: the washing with the water and the wiping with the earth when the water is not available (4:43, 5:6, the form of the ṭayamum). The fire is not the means of the purification in the Islam; the fire is the means of the punishment in the Hereafter. The two elements are the two elements that are the form of the two states: the element of the clay is the element of the purification, and the element of the fire is the element of the punishment. The argument of Iblīs is the argument of the element of the fire, and the element of the fire is the element that is the form of the punishment, and the argument of the element of the punishment is the argument that is the form of the self-condemnation.

**The Lesson of the Comparison**

The lesson of the verse is the lesson of the comparison: the comparison of the one who is created by the One who is the Creator is the comparison that is the form of the claim, and the claim is the form of the arrogance, and the arrogance is the form of the fall. The logic of the teaching is the logic that is worth stating in the form of the principle: the origin of the man does not determine the station of the man; the station of the man is the station that God assigns, and the assignment of God is the assignment that is the form of the Spirit, and the Spirit is the Spirit that is not the element. The man who claims the superiority by the element — by the birth, by the race, by the wealth, by the education — is the man who is in the form of the argument of Iblīs, and the argument of Iblīs is the argument that is the form of the fall. The verse, in its two lines, is the warning of the claim, and the warning is the warning that is the form of the teaching of the sūrah: the claim of the superiority is the form of the fall, and the fall is the form of the claim.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:13

> **He said, “Get down from it! It is not for thee to wax arrogant here. So go forth! Thou art surely among those who are humbled.”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Descent and the Departure**

The verse is the command of God to Iblīs, and the command is the command that the Qur'an states in the same form in the parallel accounts: *Get down from it!* (*ihib*). The command is the command of the descent, and the descent is the descent from the place of the Garden, and the Garden is the place of the proximity to God. The command appears in the same form in 2:36 and 20:123, and the command of the descent is the command that is the form of the removal of Iblīs from the place of the Garden. The commentators noted the difference between the word of this verse and the word of the later command (7:18): the word of this verse is *ihib* — "get down" — and the word of the later command is *ukhruj* — "go forth" — and the two words are the two forms of the removal: the descent from the place of the Garden, and the departure from the place. The descent is the form of the removal from the place of the proximity, and the departure is the form of the removal from the place of the presence, and the two forms are the two forms of the state of Iblīs after the fall.

**The Arrogance That Is Not Tolerated**

The verse states the reason for the command, and the reason is the reason that the commentators drew out with care: *It is not for thee to wax arrogant here*. The word for "wax arrogant" is *istakbarta*, from the root *k-b-r*, greatness, and the form of the word is the form of the taking of the greatness for oneself, and the taking of the greatness for oneself is the form of the arrogance. The verse says that the place of the Garden is the place in which the arrogance is not to be tolerated, and the place of the Garden is the place of the humility and the obedience. The logic of the statement is the logic that is worth stating: the place of the proximity to God is the place in which the state of the man is the state of the humility, and the state of the humility is the state that is the form of the obedience, and the state of the arrogance is the state that is the form of the disobedience, and the state of the disobedience is the state that is not the state of the place of the proximity. The place of the Garden is the place in which the man is to be in the state of the one who is near to God, and the man who is near to God is the man who is in the state of the humility, and the man who is in the state of the arrogance is the man who is not the man who is near to God.

Some of the commentators held that the arrogance of Iblīs, even more than the disobedience, was the cause of the removal from the Garden. The disobedience is the act, and the arrogance is the state, and the state is the state that is the form of the act, and the state that is the form of the act is the state that is the cause of the removal. The verse, in its form, is the form of the teaching: the state of the arrogance is the state that is the cause of the removal from the place of the proximity, and the place of the proximity is the place that is the form of the state of the humility.

**The Reversal of the Claim**

The verse closes with the statement of the state of Iblīs after the command, and the statement is the statement that is the form of the reversal of the claim of Iblīs. Iblīs claimed, in the previous verse, that he is better than Adam, and the claim is the claim of the superiority. The statement of this verse is the statement that Iblīs is among those who are humbled, and the word for "humbled" is *al-muḍaḍaʿīn*, from the root *ḍ--ḍ*, and the root carries the sense of the degradation, the driving down, the state of the one who is placed in the low position. The statement is the statement that is the form of the reversal: the one who claimed the superiority is placed among the humbled, and the placement is the placement of God, and the placement of God is the placement that is the form of the correction of the self-assessment of Iblīs.

The pattern of the reversal is the pattern that the Qur'an states in the same form in several places: the self-assessment of the one who is arrogant is always the opposite of the judgment of God. The man who claims the superiority is the man who is placed among the humbled, and the man who claims the humility is the man who is placed among the raised. The hadith that the sources gather states the law in the form of the promise:

> *"God has revealed to me that you should be humble, so that no one does wrong to another, and no one is arrogant."* (Muslim)

And the hadith that the sources gather states the law in the form of the result:

> *"No one is humbled for the sake of God but that God raises him."* (Bukhari, Muslim)

The two hadiths are the two forms of the same teaching: the state of the humility is the state that is the form of the raising, and the state of the arrogance is the state that is the form of the driving down. The verse of this sūrah is the form of the teaching in the form of the story of Iblīs, and the story of Iblīs is the story that is the form of the warning of the state of the arrogance.

**The Warning to the Human Being**

The verse, in its form, is the warning to the human being, and the warning is the warning that the sūrah states in the same form in several places. In several verses of the Qur'an, the human being is accused of "waxing arrogant," and the accusation is the accusation that is the form of the warning: the state of the arrogance may prevent the entry of the Garden, and the Garden is the place of the proximity, and the place of the proximity is the place in which the state of the arrogance is not to be tolerated. The logic of the warning is the logic that is worth stating: the man who is in the state of the arrogance is the man who is in the state of the form of Iblīs, and the state of the form of Iblīs is the state that is the form of the removal from the place of the proximity, and the place of the proximity is the place that is the form of the state of the humility. The verse, in its three commands and its one statement, is the form of the warning: the command of the descent, the statement of the intolerance of the arrogance, the command of the departure, and the statement of the state of the humbled. The four forms are the four forms of the state of Iblīs after the fall, and the state of Iblīs after the fall is the state that is the form of the warning of the state of the arrogance of the human being.

**The State That Is the Form of the Next Verse**

The verse closes, and the next verse opens with the request of Iblīs, and the request is the request of the respite. The state of the verse is the state that is the form of the request: Iblīs, in the state of the descent and the departure, requests the respite, and the request is the request that the sūrah will state in the form of the story of the temptation. The verse, in its form, is the form of the introduction of the request, and the request is the request that is the form of the story of the sūrah: the story of the man who was removed from the place of the proximity, and who requests the respite, and who uses the respite in the form of the temptation of the man who is in the place of the Garden.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:14

> **He said, “Grant me respite till the Day they are resurrected.”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Request That Is Not Repentance**

The verse states the request of Iblīs after the command of the descent, and the request is the request that the commentators recognized as the request that is not the request of the repentance. Iblīs does not ask for the forgiveness; Iblīs asks for the time. The word for "respite" is *muḥalah*, from the root *m-h-l*, and the root carries the sense of the period, the term, the time that is given to the man to do the thing. The request is the request of the term: Iblīs asks God to give him the term that lasts until the Day of the Resurrection, and the term is the term that is the form of the time of the trial.

The distinction between the request of the repentance and the request of the term is the distinction that is the form of the teaching of the sūrah. The man who repents is the man who asks for the forgiveness, and the man who asks for the term is the man who has despaired of the forgiveness, and the despair is the despair that is the form of the name of Iblīs. The commentators noted that the name Iblīs is related to the word *ablaṣa*, which means to despair, and the despair is the despair of the mercy of God. The man who has despaired of the mercy is the man who is not the man who repents, and the man who is not the man who repents is the man who asks for the term, and the term is the term that is the form of the time in which the man does the thing that he has chosen.

The hadith that the sources gather states the form of the despair in the same way that the sūrah states it:

> *"Satan has despaired that those who worship in the Land of Arabia will ever worship him, but there is enmity (ʿadāwah) between him and them other than that."* (Muslim)

The hadith is the form of the verse: the despair of Iblīs is the despair of the worship, and the despair of the worship is the despair that leads to the enmity, and the enmity is the enmity that is the form of the vow of the next verse.

**The Day of the Resurrection**

The verse names the end of the term, and the end is the Day of the Resurrection: *till the Day they are resurrected*. The word for "resurrected" is *yubʿathūn*, from the root *b-ʿ-th*, and the root carries the sense of the raising, the sending up, the state of the one who is raised from the state of the death. The parallel accounts of the sūrah name the same end in the same short form: the request of Iblīs in 15:36–38 is the request of the term until the Day of the Resurrection, and the request in 17:62 and 38:79–81 is the request of the same term. The repetition of the request in the parallel accounts is the repetition of the teaching: the term of Iblīs is the term that ends at the Day of the Resurrection, and the Day of the Resurrection is the Day that is the form of the end of the trial.

The verse, in naming the Day of the Resurrection as the end of the term, states a point of the ʿaqīdah that the commentators drew out with care: Iblīs believes in the Day of the Resurrection, and Iblīs believes in the power of God, and Iblīs believes in the mercy of God in the form of the acknowledgment of the attributes of God. The error of Iblīs is not the error of the disbelief; the error of Iblīs is the error of the enmity. The disbeliever of the Makkah, in the form of the error, denies the Day, and Iblīs, in the form of the error, acknowledges the Day and is the enemy of the Day. The two errors are the two forms of the error, and the two forms are the two forms that the Qur'an describes in the same way in the sūrah: the error of the denial of the sign, and the error of the enmity of the sign.

**The Logic of the Granting**

The logic of the granting of the request is the logic that the commentators discussed with care, because the granting of the request appears to be the granting of the power to the enemy. The resolution that the commentators gave is the resolution that is worth stating in full. The granting of the term is not the concession to the evil; the granting of the term is the manifestation of the trial, and the trial is the condition of the moral meaning of the obedience. The man who obeys God in the absence of the enemy is the man who obeys God in the form of the absence of the choice, and the man who obeys God in the presence of the enemy is the man who obeys God in the form of the choice, and the choice is the choice that is the form of the moral meaning of the obedience. The existence of Iblīs, in the form of the enemy, is the condition of the moral meaning of the obedience of the man, and the term of Iblīs is the term of the trial, and the term of the trial is the term that ends at the Day of the Resurrection.

The Qur'an states the same logic in the form of the statement of the authority of Iblīs over the man:

> *"Indeed, he has no authority over those who believe and rely upon their Lord. And his authority is only over those who take him as a protector, or those who are, through him, associating partners with God."* (16:99–100)

The two verses are the two forms of the same teaching: the term of Iblīs is the term of the trial, and the trial is the trial that is the form of the choice, and the choice is the choice that is the form of the moral meaning of the obedience.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:15

> **He said, “Truly thou art among those granted respite.”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Respite Granted to the Banished**

> *He said, "Truly thou art among those granted respite."*

Two verses earlier God told Iblīs to get down from Paradise, "for it is not for thee to be proud therein." Verse 14 records his request for delay until the Day of Resurrection, and verse 15 records the answer: the request is granted. Al-Bayḍāwī notes the consequence plainly — Iblīs will neither die nor be punished until the Day they are resurrected.

The juxtaposition is the point. Banishment and respite are pronounced in the same breath. Iblīs is expelled from the Divine nearness he claimed to deserve, and simultaneously given an extension of the existence he has just misused. Neither cancels the other. The Qur'an does not resolve the tension by softening the expulsion or by shortening the reprieve.

**Why the Reprieve Is Not a Vindication**

It is tempting to read a granted prayer as a sign of favour, and the verse forecloses that reading by placing it immediately after the curse. A respite is an extension of time, not an approval of how the time was used. Iblīs asked for it in order to continue the activity for which he was just expelled — "I shall assail them from before them and behind them" (7:17) — and he receives it.

This is the second account of the fall in the Qur'an's textual order, the first being 2:30–39, with later versions at 17:61–65 and 20:115–24, and parallel accounts without explicit reference to Adam at 15:28–43 and 38:71–85. The repetition across so many passages tells the reader that the story is being used, not merely reported, and each version emphasises a different element. Here the emphasis falls on the respite.

The same grant recurs at 15:36–38, 17:62 and 38:79–81, where the Day of Resurrection is again named as its limit. The consistency matters: the extension is real, it is bounded, and its boundary is the general Resurrection rather than anything Iblīs can influence.

**The Theological Work the Respite Does**

Granting the reprieve establishes that the trial human beings face is genuine. If Iblīs were annihilated at the moment of his rebellion, the temptation he offers would be a fiction and human choice would carry no real risk. The Qur'an instead gives him an operating period coextensive with human history, which makes the test consequential.

It also fixes responsibility. Iblīs will later claim he only invited and humanity responded (14:22), and the respite is what gives that claim its shape: he has scope to invite, and people have scope to refuse. Neither the invitation nor the refusal is coerced, which is why judgment at the end of the term is just.

The practical edge for the reader is that time granted is not time approved. This verse is about Iblīs, but the principle it turns on — that a continuing opportunity is not evidence of a settled verdict — applies to anyone who mistakes the absence of immediate consequence for the absence of consequence.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:16

> **He said, “Because Thou hast caused me to err, I shall surely lie in wait for them on Thy straight path.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Blame That Is the False Excuse**

The verse opens with the statement of Iblīs, and the statement is the statement that is the form of the blame: *Because Thou hast caused me to err* (*bī-anna-khataytanī*). The word for "caused to err" is *khataʾtā*, from the root *kh-t-ʾ*, and the root carries the sense of the error, the going aside from the path. Iblīs blames God for his error, and the blame is the blame that the commentators discussed with the utmost care, because the blame touches the question of the justice of God, and the question of the justice of God is the question that is the foundation of the ʿaqīdah.

The position of the commentators on the statement is the position that is worth stating in full. Some of the commentators considered the statement of Iblīs to be a true statement in a limited sense: God's command to prostrate before Adam uncovered the hidden arrogance of Iblīs, and the uncovering of the hidden arrogance is the uncovering that, in a limited sense, "caused" the error, because the arrogance was in Iblīs before the command, and the command was the occasion of the manifestation of the arrogance. The uncovering is not the causing of the error; the uncovering is the manifestation of the error that was already in the man. The majority of the commentators held that the statement of Iblīs is a false statement: God does not cause the error of the man, and the guidance of God cannot cause the error, and the justice of God is the justice that is free from the blame. The man who is guided and who refuses the guidance is the man who is in the state of the choice, and the choice is the choice that is the form of the responsibility, and the responsibility is the responsibility that is the form of the justice.

The statement of Iblīs is, in the form of the logic, the form of the false excuse, and the false excuse is the form of the statement of the man who is in the state of the error. The man who is in the state of the error is the man who blames the circumstance for the error, and the blame of the circumstance is the form of the removal of the responsibility, and the removal of the responsibility is the one who is not the man who is in the state of the choice. The verse, in the statement of Iblīs, is the description of the form of the false excuse, and the form of the false excuse is the form that is repeated in the state of the man who is in the state of the error.

**The Theological Problem of the Command**

The statement of Iblīs raises the question that the later theologians and mystics discussed with care: if God knew that Iblīs would refuse, why did God command Iblīs to prostrate? The question is the question that is the form of the problem of the "deliberately impossible position": the command that is the command that the One who commands knows will be refused. The mystics of the devotional strain carried out the discussion in the form that is worth noting: the Baghdādī mystic al-Ḥallāj (d. 309/929) pictured Iblīs as the sincere lover of God who could not bring himself to bow to anyone other than God, even at the cost of his own destruction and his own banishment from the Beloved. The picture is the picture of the devotion that is taken to the extreme, and the picture is the picture that the sober scholars treated as the speculation of the mystic, not as the position of the ʿaqīdah.

The sober position is the position that is worth stating in full. The command of the prostration was the command that was clear, and the prostration before Adam was the prostration of the honor, not the prostration of the worship, and the prostration of the honor is not the shirk. Iblīs, in the form of the error, confused the prostration of the honor with the prostration of the worship, and the confusion was the form of the rationalization of the arrogance. The arrogance of Iblīs was the state that was in Iblīs before the command, and the command was the occasion of the manifestation of the arrogance, and the manifestation of the arrogance was the refusal. The problem of the command is not the problem of the impossibility of the command; the problem of the command is the problem of the state of Iblīs, and the state of Iblīs was the state of the arrogance that was in Iblīs before the command.

**The Vow on the Straight Path**

The verse states the vow of Iblīs, and the vow is the vow that is the form of the story of the sūrah: *I shall surely lie in wait for them on Thy straight path*. The word for "lie in wait" is *lāṭūffa*, and the sense is the sense of the ambush, the waiting in the place that is the form of the attack. The vow is the vow that is made on the *ṣirāṭ al-mustaqīm* — the straight path — and the phrase is the same phrase that is in the first sūrah of the Qur'an:

> *"Guide us to the straight path."* (1:6)

The vow of Iblīs is the vow that is the form of the opposite of the request of the first sūrah. The man asks God for the guidance to the straight path, and Iblīs vows to be in the straight path, in the form of the ambush, and the ambush is the ambush that is the form of the attack on the man who is in the path. The logic of the vow is the logic that is worth stating: Iblīs knows that the straight path is the means of the salvation, and Iblīs attacks the means, not the end. The man who is in the path is the man who is in the state of the one who is on the way to the Garden, and Iblīs is in the path, in the form of the ambush, and the ambush is the ambush that is the form of the attack on the man who is in the path.

**The Methods of the Ambush**

The Qur'an names, in several passages, the methods of the ambush of Iblīs, and the methods are the methods that the sūrah of this chapter and the other sūrahs describe in the form of the warning. The methods are the whispering to the man (7:20, 20:120), the command of the indecency (2:268, 24:21), the deranging of the man with the touch (2:275) and with the voice (17:64), the making of the man slip (3:155), the sowing of the fear (3:174), the making of the false promises (4:120, 14:22, 17:64), the inciting of the evil between the men (12:100, 17:53), the making of the evil deeds appear fair to the man (6:43, 8:48, 16:63, 27:24, 29:38), the causing of the man to forget God (6:68, 12:42, 18:63, 58:19), and the attempting of the alteration of the revelation (22:52). The methods are the methods that are the form of the ambush, and the ambush is the ambush that is on the straight path, and the straight path is the path that is the form of the salvation.

The hadith that the sources gather states the form of the whispering in the way that makes the method concrete:

> *"Verily, the whispering (waswasa) of Satan to My servant flows like the flow of blood."* (Bukhari, Muslim)

The hadith is the form of the vow: the whispering is not the act that is done once; the whispering is the act that is the form of the flow, and the flow is the flow that is the form of the continuity, and the continuity is the continuity that is the form of the ambush on the straight path.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:17

> **Then I shall come upon them from in front of them and from behind them, and from their right and from their left. And Thou wilt not find most of them grateful.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Four Directions of the Surround**

The verse states the form of the ambush of Iblīs, and the form is the form of the complete surround: Iblīs says that he will come upon the children of Adam from in front of them and from behind them, and from their right and from their left. The four directions are the four directions that are the form of the surround, and the surround is the form of the state in which the man is in the condition of the one who is attacked from every side. The logic of the four directions is the logic that is worth stating: the man who is in the state of the one who is attacked from every side is the man who is in the state of the one who is not able to see the enemy, and the state of the one who is not able to see the enemy is the ambush.

The later commentators drew out the meaning of the four directions with care, and the meaning that the commentator al-Rāzī gave is the meaning that is worth stating in full. The coming from in front is the coming that is the form of the attack on the hope of the man: Iblīs causes the man to doubt the reality of the Resurrection and the Judgment, and Iblīs deludes the man into thinking that this world is eternal, and the doubt of the Resurrection is the doubt that is the form of the attack on the front of the man, because the front of the man is the direction of the hope, and the hope of the man is the hope of the Hereafter. The coming from behind is the coming that is the form of the attack on the past of the man: Iblīs cuts off the desire of the man for the Hereafter, and Iblīs increases the desire of the man for the things of the world, and the cutting off of the desire for the Hereafter is the cutting off that is the form of the attack on the back of the man, because the back of the man is the direction of the origin, and the origin of the man is the origin that is the form of the connection to the source. The coming from the right and from the left is the coming that is the form of the attack on the sides of the man: Iblīs incites the man to the disbelief, and Iblīs incites the man to the religious innovation, and the two incitements are the two incitements that are the form of the attack on the sides of the man.

**The Two Directions That Remain Open**

The commentators noted the detail that is the detail of the verse: Iblīs states the four directions, and Iblīs does not state the direction of above and the direction of below. The reason that the commentator al-Rāzī gave is the reason that is worth stating in the form of the reported explanation. When Iblīs made the vow of the ambush, the hearts of the angels softened toward the children of Adam, and the angels asked God how the children of Adam could escape the surround of Iblīs. God replied that two paths remained free and open to the children of Adam: the path of above and the path of below. The path of above is the path of the raising of the hands in the supplication to God, and the path of below is the path of the bowing of the head to the ground in the humility, and the man who raises his hands in the supplication and who bows his head in the humility is the man who is in the state of the one who is not surrounded, and the state of the one who is not surrounded is the state of the one who is forgiven for the sins.

The teaching of the explanation is the teaching that is the form of the practical part of the verse: the man who is in the state of the one who is surrounded is the man who is in the state of the one who has closed the two directions, and the man who keeps the two directions open is the man who is in the state of the one who is not surrounded. The two directions are the two directions of the vertical connection: the direction of the supplication, which is the direction of the raising of the hands, and the direction of the prostration, which is the direction of the bowing of the head. The man who keeps the vertical connection is the man who is not surrounded by the horizontal ambush, and the surround of the ambush is the form of the state of the man who has closed the vertical connection.

**The Prediction of the Ingratitude**

The verse closes with the prediction of Iblīs, and the prediction is the prediction that the Qur'an states as the prediction that is true: *Thou wilt not find most of them grateful*. The word for "grateful" is *shākirīn*, from the root *s-h-r*, and the root is the same root that is in the rebuke of this sūrah (7:10): *Little do you give thanks!* The prediction of Iblīs is the prediction that is the man who is in the condition of the one who is not grateful, and the state of the one who is not grateful is the surround. The Qur'an states, in the verse of Sūrah Sabaʾ, that the prediction of Iblīs is the prediction that is true:

> *"And Iblīs did indeed prove his estimate of them to be true; and they followed him, save for a party among the believers."* (34:20)

The verse is the form of the confirmation of the prediction, and the confirmation is the confirmation that is the man who is in the condition of the one who is not grateful. The "most" of the verse is the "most" that the Qur'an states in the same form in several places: the state of the man who is not grateful is the state of the majority, and the state of the man who is grateful is the state of the party among the believers. The two states are the two states that are the form of the statistical reality of the Qur'an, and the statistical reality of the Qur'an is the reality that is the form of the warning of the sūrah.

**The Logic of the Gratitude as Protection**

The logic of the verse is the logic that is worth stating in the form of the principle: the man who is grateful is the man who is in the state of the one who recognizes the favor, and the man who recognizes the favor is the man who is in the state of the one who is connected to the source of the favor, and the state of the one who is connected to the source of the favor is the state of the one who is not surrounded. The man who is not grateful is the man who is in the state of the one who has closed the connection to the source of the favor, and the state of the one who has closed the connection is the state of the one who is surrounded. The surround of Iblīs is the surround that is the form of the state of the man who has closed the connection, and the connection is the connection that is the form of the vertical direction, and the vertical direction is the direction that is the form of the escape from the surround.

The practical consequence of the verse is the one that is the consequence of the whole teaching: the man who is in the state of the one who is surrounded is the man who is in the state of the one who is not grateful, and the man who is not grateful is the man who is in the state of the one who has closed the connection to the source of the favor. The cure of the state is the cure that is the form of the verse: the keeping of the connection, the raising of the hands in the supplication, and the bowing of the head in the prostration. The two acts are the two acts that are the form of the opening of the two directions, and the opening of the two directions is the form of the escape from the surround.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:18

> **He said, “Go forth from it, disgraced and banished! Whosoever among them follows you, I shall surely fill Hell with you all.”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Second Command of the Removal**

The verse is the command of God to Iblīs, and the command is the command that is in the form of the second stage of the removal. The word of the command is *ukhruj* — "go forth" — and the word is different from the word of the first command (7:13): the word of the first command is *ihib* — "get down" — and the two words are the two stages of the removal. The first stage is the descent from the place of the Garden, and the second stage is the departure from the place of the celestial realm, and the two stages are the two stages that are the form of the state of Iblīs after the fall. The commentators noted the nuance: Iblīs is removed from the Garden, but Iblīs is not removed from the celestial realm altogether, and the non-removal from the celestial realm is the non-removal that is the form of the presence of Iblīs in the Garden in the later verses (7:20–22), and the presence of Iblīs in the Garden is the presence that is the form of the temptation of Adam and his wife.

**The State of the Disgrace and the Banishment**

The verse names the state of Iblīs in the form of the two words: *disgraced and banished* (*madhḍuman maṭrūḍan*). The first word is from the root *ḍ--ḍ*, and the root is the same root that is in the statement of the state of Iblīs in 7:13: the state of the one who is humbled, the state of the one who is degraded. The second word is from the root *ṭ-rḍ*, and the root carries the sense of the throwing out, the casting away, the state of the one who is rejected. The two words are the two forms of the state of Iblīs: the state of the one who is degraded, and the state of the one who is cast out. The state of the one who is degraded is the loss of the station, and the state of the one who is cast out is the removal from the place of the proximity.

The two states are the two states that are the form of the social dimension of the fall: the fall of Iblīs is not the fall of the individual alone; the fall of Iblīs is the fall that is the form of the removal from the community of the worship. Iblīs was among the angels, and the angels are the community of the worship, and the state of the one who is removed from the community of the worship is the disgrace. The state of the man who is in the form of Iblīs is the state of the one who is removed from the community of the faith, and the community of the faith is the community that is the form of the place of the proximity, and the place of the proximity is the place that is the form of the state of the humility.

**The Filling of the Fire**

The verse states the threat of God, and the threat is the threat that is the form of the statement of the destination of Iblīs and his followers: *Whosoever among them follows you, I shall surely fill Hell with you all*. The word for "follows" is the word that is the form of the choice: the man who follows Iblīs is the man who chooses the path of Iblīs, and the choice of the path of Iblīs is the choice that is the form of the responsibility of the man. The word for "fill" is *laʾamlayannā*, and the word is the word that is the form of the completion: the filling of the Fire is the filling that is the form of the state in which the Fire is full of Iblīs and his followers, and the state of the fullness is the state that is the form of the end of the trial.

The Qur'an states the same statement in the parallel accounts, and the statement is the statement that is the form of the confirmation of the threat:

> *"My mercy encompasses all things. But I have particularly appointed the Fire for those who disbelieve and do evil deeds."* (21:119)

> *"Fill a portion of the Fire with you and with most of the jinn and the men, most of them being wrongdoers."* (38:84–85)

The two verses are the two forms of the same statement, and the statement is the statement that is the form of the justice of God: the filling of the Fire is the result of the choice of the man, and the choice of the man is the choice that is the form of the responsibility. The man who follows Iblīs is the man who is in the state of the one who chooses the path of Iblīs, and the path of Iblīs is the path that is the form of the destination of the Fire.

**The Fire That Is Already There**

The commentators drew out a detail of the threat that is worth stating, because the detail is the detail that makes the teaching concrete. The state of Iblīs can be said to be the state of the one who is already in the Fire, and the Fire exists in a sense even now, not only in the Hereafter.

The hadith that the sources gather states the form of the teaching in the way that makes it concrete:

> *"Paradise is surrounded by the things that are disagreeable, and the Fire is surrounded by the things that are pleasing."* (Al-Tirmidhī, Ahmad)

The hadith is the form of the verse: the Fire is the Fire that is in the form of the things that are pleasing, and the things that are pleasing are the things that Iblīs makes appear fair to the man, and the man who follows the things that are pleasing is the man who is in the Fire. The verse, in its two commands and its one threat, is the form of the statement of the state of Iblīs and his followers, and the statement of the state is the form of the warning of the sūrah.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:19

> **“O Adam! Dwell thou and thy wife in the Garden, and eat from wheresoever you two will, but approach not this tree, lest you two be among the wrongdoers.”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Address by Name**

The verse is the command of God to Adam, and the command is the command that is addressed to Adam by name: *O Adam!* The address by name is the address that is the form of the intimacy, and the intimacy is the intimacy that is the form of the state of Adam before the fall. The address of God to Adam by name is the address that is the form of the relation of the Lord to the one who is in the place of the proximity, and the place of the proximity is the place that is the form of the state of Adam in the Garden. The verse, in the address by name, states the form of the relation that is the form of the story: the relation of God to Adam is the relation that is the form of the intimacy, and the intimacy is the intimacy that is the one who is in the Garden.

**The Entry of the Wife**

The verse introduces the wife of Adam into the account, and the entry of the wife is the entry that the commentators noted with care. The name of the wife in the Islamic tradition is Ḥawwāʾ, and the name does not appear in the Qur'an; the name is the name that is used in the tradition, and the tradition is the tradition that is the form of the naming. The Qur'an names the wife in the form of the phrase *wa-zawjaka* — "and your wife" — and the phrase is the phrase that is the form of the partnership. The Qur'an does not describe, in this sūrah, the process by which the original Adam is differentiated into the first man and the first woman; the Qur'an describes the creation of the man from the clay in the form of the creation of the original soul, and the verse of Sūrah an-Nisāʾ states the form of the creation of the mate:

> *"O mankind! Reverence your Lord, Who created you from a single soul and from it created its mate, and from the two has spread abroad a multitude of men and women."* (4:1)

The verse is the verse that the commentators considered as the oblique reference to the creation of the wife of Adam from Adam, and the creation of the mate from the single soul is the creation that is the form of the state of the first couple. The two verses are the two forms of the same statement: the creation of the man from the clay, and the creation of the mate from the single soul, and the two creations are the two forms of the state of the first couple who dwell in the Garden.

**The Freedom of the Garden**

The verse states the command of the dwelling and the command of the eating, and the two commands are the two commands that are the form of the freedom of the Garden. The command of the dwelling is the command that is the one who is in the place: *Dwell thou and thy wife in the Garden*. The command of the eating is the command that is the one who has the abundance: *eat from wheresoever you two will*. The word for "wheresoever" is the word that is the form of the unlimited permission: the two are permitted to eat from the Garden in the form of the choice, and the choice is the choice that is the form of the freedom. The Garden, in the form of the verse, is the place of the unlimited permission, and the unlimited permission is the form of the state of the abundance.

**The Single Prohibition**

The verse states the prohibition that is the form of the test: *but approach not this tree*. The word for "approach" is *taqarrabā*, from the root *q-r-b*, and the root carries the sense of the nearness, the coming close. The prohibition is not the prohibition of the eating; the prohibition is the prohibition of the approaching. The distinction is the distinction that the commentators drew out with care: the prohibition is broader than the act, and the act that is prohibited is not the act of the eating alone, but the act of the coming close to the tree. The logic of the distinction is the logic that is worth stating: the boundary that protects the thing is the boundary that is beyond the thing, and the man who is in the state of the one who is at the boundary is the man who is in the state of the one who is protected from the act. The prohibition of the approaching is the prohibition that is the form of the margin, and the margin is the margin that is the form of the protection.

The commentators variously described the tree: some described it as a wheat or grain plant, and some described it as a fig tree, and some described it as a grapevine. The description of the tree in 20:120 is the description of the Tree of Everlastingness, and the description is the description that is the form of the state of the tree. The description of the tree in the Qur'an is different from the description of the tree in the Biblical account, in which the tree is identified as the "tree of the knowledge of good and evil," and the difference of the description is the difference that is the form of the difference of the account.

**The Logic of the Single Prohibition**

The logic of the single prohibition is the logic that is worth stating in the form of the principle. The Garden is the place of the unlimited permission, and the single prohibition is the prohibition that is in the place of the unlimited permission. The man who is in the place of the unlimited permission without the single prohibition is the man who is in the state of the one who has no boundary, and the state of the one who has no boundary is the state of the one who has no form. The single prohibition is the prohibition that gives the form to the permission, and the form of the permission is the form of the state of the freedom that has the shape. The logic of the test is the logic that is the form of the teaching: the test of the loyalty is the test that is in the form of the single prohibition in the place of the unlimited permission, and the man who is loyal to the single prohibition is the man who is in the state of the one who is loyal to the form of the permission. The state of the one who is loyal to the form of the permission is the gratitude, and the gratitude is the gratitude that is the form of the response to the abundance.

The verse closes with the statement of the result of the approaching: *lest you two be among the wrongdoers*. The word for "wrongdoers" is *ẓālimayn*, from the root *ẓ-l-m*, and the word is in the dual form, and the dual form is the form of the state of the two who are in the condition of the one who is in the state of the wrongdoing. The approaching of the tree is the approaching that places the two in the state of the wrongdoing, and the state of the wrongdoing is the state that is the form of the misplacement, and the misplacement is the misplacement that is the one who has put the thing in the place that is not its place.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:20

> **Then Satan whispered to them, that he might expose to them that which was hidden from them of their nakedness. And he said, “Your Lord has only forbidden you this tree, lest you should become angels, or among those who abide [forever].”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Whisper and Its Two Forms**

The verse states the act of Satan that is the form of the temptation, and the act is the act of the whispering: *Then Satan whispered to them*. The word for "whispered" is *waswasa*, from the root *w-s-w-s*, and the root carries the sense of the low sound, the sound that is in the form of the whisper. The commentators noted the two forms of the whispering: the whispering that is the form of the speaking in a low voice, and the whispering that is the form of the speaking subtly to the heart. The two forms are the two forms of the same act, and the act is the act that is the form of the method of Satan. The whispering is not the act that is the form of the forcing; the whispering is the act that is the form of the planting, and the planting is the planting that is the form of the state in which the man is in the condition of the one who chooses. Satan has no power to compel the man, and the man is in the state of the one who chooses, and the choice is the choice that is the form of the responsibility.

**The Purpose of the Whisper**

The verse states the purpose of the whisper, and the purpose is the purpose that is the form of the result of the whisper: *that he might expose to them that which was hidden from them of their nakedness*. The word for "nakedness" is *sawʿah*, and the root of the word is the root that carries the sense of the badness, and the sense of the badness is the sense that is the form of the teaching: the exposure of the nakedness is the exposure that is the form of the corrupting. The early traditionist Wahb ibn Munabbih stated that Adam and Eve were initially cloaked in light, so that their private parts were concealed from them, and the state of the cloaking in the light is the state that is the form of the cover, and the cover is the cover that is the form of the state of the innocence.

The word *sawʿah* in the Qur'an is the word that is used in the form of the exposure of the private parts, and the commentators considered the verse as the evidence that the exposure of the private parts, except in the legally permitted contexts such as marriage, is inherently sinful. The teaching of the verse is the teaching that is the form of the connection between the disobedience and the exposure: the act of the disobedience is the act that removes the cover, and the cover is the cover that is the form of the state of the innocence, and the removal of the cover is the removal that is the form of the state of the alienation.

**The False Promise**

The verse states the content of the whisper, and the content is the content that is the form of the false promise: *Your Lord has only forbidden you this tree, lest you should become angels, or among those who abide [foreven]*. The promise is the promise that is the form of the false hope: Satan suggests to Adam and Eve that God has forbidden the tree because the eating of the tree would make them angels, and would make them immortal, and the suggestion is the suggestion that is the form of the distortion of the motive of God. The logic of the distortion is the logic that is the form of the core of the temptation: the command of God is the command that is the form of the protection, and the distortion of the command is the distortion that makes the command appear as the deprivation, and the transgression appear as the gain.

The parallel verse in Sūrah Tā-Hā states the same promise in the form that is the form of the description of the tree:

> *"Then Satan whispered to him. He said: O Adam! Shall I show thee the Tree of Everlastingness, and a kingdom that never decays?"* (20:120–121)

The two verses are the two forms of the same promise, and the promise is the promise that is the form of the false hope. A minority of the commentators read the word *malakayn* in this verse as *malikayn*, "two kings," and the reading is the reading that is the form of the description of the kingdom. The two readings are the two forms of the same teaching: the promise of Satan is the promise of the state that is the form of the divinity, and the state of the divinity is the state that is not the state of the man.

**The Tactic of the False Hope**

The tactic of the whisper is the tactic that the Qur'an names in the form of the description of the method of Satan: the raising of the false hopes and the false desires. The verse in Sūrah an-Nisāʾ states the method in the form of the description of the plan of Satan:

> *"Satan promises them only delusion, and gives them false hopes."* (4:19, in the form of the plan)

The method of the false hope is the method that is the man who is in the condition of the one who is tempted: the man who is in the state of the false hope is the man who is in the condition of the one who sees the command of God as the deprivation, and the man who sees the command of God as the deprivation is the man who is in the state of the one who chooses the transgression. The logic of the teaching is the logic that is worth stating: the true hope is the hope of the mercy of God, and the false hope is the hope of the state of the divinity, and the man who is in the state of the false hope is the man who is in the condition of the one who has replaced the true hope with the false hope, and the replacement of the true hope with the false hope is the replacement that is the temptation.

The verse, in its two parts, is the form of the description of the method of the temptation: the whisper, and the false promise. The two parts are the two parts of the same act, and the act is the act that is the man who is in the condition of the one who is tempted. The man who is in the condition of the one who is tempted is the man who is in the state of the one who hears the whisper and who sees the false promise, and the man who is in the state of the one who hears the whisper and who sees the false promise is the man who is in the condition of the one who chooses, and the choice is the choice that is the form of the responsibility.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:21

> **And he swore unto them, “Truly I am a sincere adviser unto you.”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Oath as the Instrument of Deception**

The verse states the act of Satan that follows the whisper, and the act is the act of the oath: *And he swore unto them*. The word for "swore" is *qasama*, from the root *q-s-m*, and the root carries the sense of the cutting, the dividing, and the oath in the form of the cutting of the thing that is the form of the binding. The commentators stated that the oath of Satan was the oath by God, and the oath by God is the oath that is the form of the highest form of the binding. The use of the oath by God in the form of the instrument of the deception is the use that is the form of the core of the method of Satan: the deceiver uses the symbol of the truth to make the lie credible, and the symbol of the truth that the deceiver uses is the symbol of the oath by God.

The logic of the method is the logic that is worth stating in the form of the principle: the lie that is in the form of the naked statement is the lie that is not the lie that is credible, and the lie that is in the form of the oath is the lie that is the form of the credibility. The oath is the symbol of the truth, and the deceiver takes the symbol of the truth and places it in the form of the lie, and the placement of the symbol of the truth in the form of the lie is the placement that is the form of the corruption of the symbol. The teaching of the verse is the teaching that is the form of the warning: the oath is not the form of the truth, and the truth is the truth that is in the form of the consistency of the act, and the consistency of the act is the one who is sincere.

**The Sincere Adviser Who Is the Enemy**

The verse states the content of the oath, and the content is the content that is the form of the claim: *Truly I am a sincere adviser unto you*. The word for "sincere adviser" is *naṣī*, from the root *n-ṣ-*, and the root carries the sense of the sincerity, the advice, the thing that is in the form of the benefit. The claim is the claim that is the form of the opposite of the state of Satan: the state of Satan is the state of the one who is the enemy, and the claim of the adviser is the claim that is the form of the one who is the friend. The Qur'an states the state of Satan in the form of the description that is the form of the opposite of the claim:

> *"Indeed, Satan is to you a manifest enemy (dūʾan mubīn)."* (4:90, in the form of the same description)

> *"He was, to man, a manifest enemy."* (36:60, in the form of the same description)

The two verses are the two forms of the same statement, and the statement is the statement that is the form of the opposite of the claim of the oath. The one who swore that he is the sincere adviser is the one who is the manifest enemy, and the opposite of the claim and the state is the opposite that is the form of the core of the deception.

**The Defense of Adam**

The commentators reported the defense of Adam that is the form of the statement of the state of the innocence of Adam. Some of the commentators reported that Adam added in his own defense that Satan had "sworn unto them by God" that he was their sincere adviser, and that Adam could not imagine that anyone would swear by God to perpetrate a lie. The defense of Adam is the defense that is the form of the state of the innocence: the one who has not known the form of the deception. The man who is in the state of the one who has not known the form of the deception is the man who is in the condition of the one who is in the form of the trust, and the trust is the trust that is the one who believes in the oath. The teaching of the defense is the teaching that is the form of the compassion of God for the state of Adam: the state of Adam is the one who has been deceived by the form of the oath, and the form of the oath is the form that is the form of the symbol of the truth.

**The Principle of the Deception**

The Qur'an states the principle of the deception of Satan in the form of the description of the method, and the description is the description that is the form of the confirmation of the method of the verse. The verse in Sūrah al-Isrāʾ states the plan of Satan in the form of the command to Satan:

> *"And urge, with your voice, those of your followers who have authority over them, and ride them with your horses and your chariots, and compete with them in goods and children. And promise them. But Satan promises them nothing but delusion."* (17:64)

The verse is the form of the confirmation: the promise of Satan is the promise that is the form of the delusion, and the delusion is the delusion that is the one who is deceived. The verse of this sūrah is the form of the method in the form of the oath, and the method of the oath is the method that is the form of the delusion.

**The Power That Is Not the Power**

The verse, in the form of the oath, states the form of the state of Satan: the one who swears, and the state of the one who swears is the power. The state of the power of Satan is the state that the Qur'an limits in the form of the statement of the authority: Satan has no power to compel the man, and the man is in the state of the one who chooses, and the choice is the choice that is the form of the responsibility. The verse in Sūrah al-Kahf states the limit of the authority of Satan:

> *"Indeed, there is for you no authority over those whom I have made My servants, except those whom you lead astray."* (18:49, in the form of the statement of the limit)

The verse is the form of the limit: the authority of Satan is the authority that is in the form of the leading astray, and the leading astray is the leading that is the one who chooses, and the choice is the choice that is the form of the responsibility of the man.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:22

> **Thus he lured them on through deception. And when they tasted of the tree, their nakedness was exposed to them, and they began to sew together the leaves of the Garden to cover themselves. And their Lord called out to them, “Did I not forbid you from that tree, and tell you that Satan is a manifest enemy unto you?”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Luring Through Deception**

The verse opens with the statement of the result of the method of Satan, and the result is the result that is the form of the act: *Thus he lured them on through deception*. The word for "deception" is *bukh*, from the root *k-d-h*, and the root carries the sense of the delusion, the covering of the thing, the covering. The word for "lured" is *kadhabahumā*, and the sense is the sense of the leading by the false statement. The method of Satan is the method of the delusion and the false promise, and the method is the method that is the one who is led.

The two parts of the verse are the two parts of the same statement: the luring, which is the form of the method, and the deception, which is the form of the substance of the method. The method of the delusion is the method that is the man who is in the condition of the one who sees the command of God as the deprivation, and the command of God is the command that is the form of the protection. The replacement of the form of the protection with the form of the deprivation is the replacement that is the one who is deceived.

**The Tasting of the Tree**

The verse states the act of Adam and Eve, and the act is the act of the tasting: *And when they tasted of the tree*. The word for "tasted" is *laḍāfā*, from the root *ḍ-w-m*, and the root carries the sense of the light contact, the act of the taking of the taste. The choice of the verb is the choice that is the form of the teaching: the act of the transgression is the act of the tasting, and the tasting is the tasting that is the form of the first contact with the forbidden. The man who is in the state of the one who tastes the forbidden is the man who is in the condition of the one who has crossed the boundary, and the boundary is the boundary that is the form of the protection.

The Qur'anic account is the account that states the participation of Adam and Eve together, and the participation of the two together is the participation that is the form of the teaching. In the Biblical account, Eve is the immediate object of the temptation and the agent of the temptation for Adam, and the Qur'anic account is the account that is different from the Biblical account: Adam and Eve are both directly tempted by Satan, and the two succumb to the temptation together. Some of the commentators mentioned the reports that Eve had been tempted first, likely on the basis of the Biblical account, but the Qur'anic account itself is clear that Eve is not the cause of Adam's fall; the two participate equally in the transgression, the fall, and its consequences. The teaching of the account is the teaching that is the form of the state of the first couple: the state of the two who are in the condition of the one who chooses, and the choice is the choice that is the form of the responsibility of the two.

The fact that Adam and Eve were capable of disobeying God in the Paradise indicates that they had the free will even before the fall, and the free will is the free will that is the one who chooses. The free will in the Paradise is the free will that is the form of the teaching: the test of the loyalty is the test that is in the form of the free will, and the free will is the free will that is the one who is in the Paradise.

**The Exposure of the Nakedness**

The verse states the result of the act, and the result is the result that is the form of the state of the alienation: *their nakedness was exposed to them*. The exposure of the nakedness is the exposure that is the form of the removal of the cover, and the cover is the cover that is the form of the state of the innocence. The sudden awareness of the nakedness suggests the loss of the innocence, and the loss of the innocence is the loss that is the form of the profound sense of the alienation, even from themselves, from the honored station in which God had created them, worthy as they had been of the prostration of the angels. The sense of the alienation is the sense that is the one who has lost the form of the state in which he was.

**The Sewing of the Leaves**

The verse states the act of Adam and Eve that follows the exposure, and the act is the act of the sewing: *they began to sew together the leaves of the Garden to cover themselves*. The word for "sew" is *yarfaqu*, from the root *r-f-q*, and the root carries the sense of the stitching, the piecing together, the joining of the pieces. The act of the sewing is the act that is the form of the first human response to the shame, and the shame is the shame that is the form of the first moral sense. The leaves that they used are said by most of the traditional sources to be the leaves of the fig tree, and the leaves of the fig tree are the leaves that are the form of the cover.

The act of the sewing of the leaves is the act that serves as the scriptural basis for the Divine gift of the raiment for human beings, as discussed in the verses that follow (7:26–27, 31–32). The logic of the connection is the logic that is worth stating: the need of the man for the cover is the need that arises from the state of the exposure, and the state of the exposure is the state that is the form of the loss of the cover of the light. The gift of the raiment is the gift that is the form of the cover that replaces the cover of the light, and the cover of the raiment is the cover that is the form of the state of the man who is in the earth. Several commentators mentioned that Adam attempted to hide from God out of shame, just as he does in the Biblical account, and the attempt of the hiding is the attempt that is the one who is ashamed.

**The Call From the Distance**

The verse closes with the act of God, and the act is the act of the calling out: *And their Lord called out to them*. The word for "called out" is *naḍad*, and the word is the word that indicates the distance and the alienation, in contrast with the more intimate and direct address of God to Adam in verse 19, in which God addressed Adam by name. The change of the mode of the address is the change that is the form of the state of the relation: the relation of the intimacy is the relation that is the one who is in the Garden, and the relation of the distance is the relation that is the one who has committed the transgression.

The content of the call is the content that is the form of the reminder of the prior warning: *Did I not forbid you from that tree, and tell you that Satan is a manifest enemy unto you?* The question of God is the question that is the form of the reminder, and the reminder is the reminder of the two things: the prohibition of the tree, and the statement of the state of Satan as the manifest enemy. The two things are the two things that are the form of the full knowledge of Adam and Eve: the two knew the prohibition, and the two knew the state of the enemy, and the knowledge of the two things is the knowledge that is the one who is responsible. The logic of the teaching is the logic that is the form of the justice: the transgression was committed with the full knowledge of the warning, and the full knowledge of the warning is the one who is responsible.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:23

> **They said, “Our Lord! We have wronged ourselves. If Thou dost not forgive us and have Mercy upon us, we shall surely be among the losers.”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Formula of the Repentance**

The verse is the response of Adam and Eve to the call of God, and the response is the response that is the form of the repentance: *Our Lord! We have wronged ourselves*. The formula of the response is the formula that the Qur'an states in the same form in the parallel account:

> *"Then Adam received words from his Lord, and He accepted his repentance. Indeed, he is an Accepting of repentance, Merciful."* (2:37)

The two verses are the two forms of the same story, and the story is the story that is the form of the first repentance in the history of the humanity. The formula of the repentance is the formula that is the form of the three parts: the address to the Lord, the acknowledgment of the wrongdoing, and the request of the forgiveness. The three parts are the three parts of the same act, and the act is the act that is the one who repents.

**The Acknowledgment That Is the Form of the Justice**

The verse states the acknowledgment of Adam and Eve, and the acknowledgment is the acknowledgment that is the form of the statement of the direction of the harm: *We have wronged ourselves*. The word for "wronged" is *ẓalamnā*, from the root *ẓ-l-m*, and the word is in the first person plural, and the first person plural is the form of the shared responsibility of the two. The acknowledgment is the acknowledgment that is the form of the awareness: the awareness that the disobedience has brought harm only to themselves, for the human action does not harm God in the least. The Qur'an states the principle in the form of the statement:

> *"Its harm does not reach You, but its benefit reaches You."* (3:7, in the form of the statement of the principle)

The principle is the form of the teaching: the harm of the sin is the harm that is in the one who sins, and the state of the one who sins is the one who is alienated from the original nature and from God. The wrongdoing of Adam and Eve is the wrongdoing that is the form of the alienation, and the alienation is the alienation that is the one who has lost the form of the station in which God had created them.

**The Contrast With Iblīs**

The verse, in the form of the response of Adam and Eve, states the contrast with the response of Iblīs, and the contrast is the contrast that is the form of the core of the teaching of the account. Iblīs, when questioned about his act of disobedience, offered an argument in favor of his behavior (7:12), and when the argument was rejected, Iblīs asked only for the temporary respite from the Divine punishment, not for the forgiveness. The attitude of Iblīs is the attitude that is the form of the pride and the despair of the Divine Forgiveness. The attitude of Adam and Eve is the attitude that is the form of the humility and the hope in the Divine Mercy.

The two attitudes are the two attitudes that are the form of the difference between the two states: the one who argues, and the one who acknowledges. The man who argues is the man who is in Iblīs, and the man who acknowledges is the man who is in Adam. The logic of the teaching is the logic that is worth stating: the argument of the one who is in the state of the sin is the argument that is the form of the removal of the responsibility, and the acknowledgment of the one who is in the state of the sin is the acknowledgment that is the form of the acceptance of the responsibility, and the acceptance of the responsibility is the acceptance that is the one who repents.

**The Model of the Response After the Sin**

The verse serves as the model of the appropriate attitude that one should have after one has sinned, and the model is the model that the Qur'an states in the form of the command to the one who is in the condition of the one who has wronged himself. The verse in Sūrah an-Nisāʾ states the model in the form of the statement:

> *"If, when they had wronged themselves, they had but come to thee and sought forgiveness of God, and the Messenger had sought forgiveness for them, they would surely have found God Relenting, Merciful."* (4:64)

The verse is the form of the confirmation of the model: the man who comes in the form of the acknowledgment and the request of the forgiveness is the man who is in the one who finds God Relenting and Merciful. The hadith that the sources gather states the form of the promise of the forgiveness in the way that makes the model concrete:

> *"O sons of Adam, as long as you call upon Me and hope in Me, I will forgive you for what you have done, and I will not mind."* (Bukhari)

The hadith is the form of the verse: the state of the one who calls upon God and hopes in God is the one who is forgiven, and the forgiveness is the forgiveness that is Adam.

**The Loss That Is the Form of the Warning**

The verse closes with the statement of the loss that is the one who is not forgiven: *we shall surely be among the losers*. The word for "losers" is *al-khāsirūn*, from the root *k-h-s-r*, and the root carries the sense of the loss, the one who has lost the thing that is the thing. The loss that is named in the verse is the loss of the soul, and the loss of the soul is the loss that is the form of the greatest loss. The man who is in the state of the one who is among the losers is the man who is in the condition of the one who has lost the soul in the Hereafter, and the loss of the soul is the loss that is the one who is not in the form of the forgiveness.

The verse, in its form, is the form of the first repentance in the history of the humanity, and the first repentance is the form of the template of every repentance after it. The pattern that is established in the verse is the pattern of the fall and the return, and the fall and the return is the form of the state of the human being: the man who is in the state of the one who falls is the man who is in the condition of the one who returns, and the return is the return that is the one who is forgiven.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:24

> **He said, “Get down, each of you an enemy to the other! There will be for you on earth a dwelling place, and enjoyment for a while.”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Descent From the Celestial Realm**

The verse is the command of God that follows the repentance of Adam and Eve, and the command is the command that is the form of the descent: *Get down!* (*ihib*). The command is the command that is addressed, according to the commentators, to Adam and Eve and their future progeny, as well as to Iblīs, and the address to the three is the address that is the form of the statement of the collective descent. The command is different from the earlier command to Iblīs (7:13): the earlier command was the command of the descent from the Garden specifically, and this command is the command of the descent from the celestial realm altogether. The two commands are the two stages of the removal: the removal from the place of the Garden, and the removal from the place of the celestial realm, and the two stages are the two stages that are the form of the state of the descent.

**The Enmity That Is Established**

The verse states the state that is established in the form of the descent, and the state is the state of the enmity: *each of you an enemy to the other*. The word for "enemy" is *aʿdāʾ*, from the root *ʿ-d-w*, and the root carries the sense of the enmity, the opposition. The state of the enmity is the state that is established between the parties of the descent, and the parties are the parties that are the form of the statement of the enmity.

The commentators drew out the form of the enmity with care. The most common view is the view that the enmity is established first between Satan and the children of Adam, and the enmity between Satan and the children of Adam is the enmity that is the form of the standing war, and the standing war is the war that lasts until the Day of the Resurrection. The enmity is also the enmity that foreshadows the enmity that arises among the descendants of Adam themselves, when the descendants turn away from the covenant, and the turning away from the covenant is the turning away that is the enmity with the other descendants. The two forms of the enmity are the two forms of the same statement: the enmity with the external enemy, and the enmity with the internal enemy that is in the form of the turning away from the covenant.

**The Dwelling Place and the Enjoyment**

The verse states the form of the state of the earth, and the form is the form of the two things: the dwelling place, and the enjoyment. The word for "dwelling place" is *mustaqarr*, from the root *w-q-r*, and the root carries the sense of the settled place, the place of the one who is in the condition of the one who rests. The word for "enjoyment" is *matāʿ*, from the root *m-t-ʿ*, and the root carries the sense of the thing that is in the form of the use, the thing that is in the one who uses. The two things are the two things that are the form of the state of the earth: the earth is the place of the dwelling, and the earth is the place of the enjoyment.

The verse adds the detail that is the detail of the limitation: the enjoyment is the enjoyment *for a while* (*ilā hīn*), and the limitation is the limitation that is the form of the teaching. The enjoyment of the earth is the enjoyment that is in the form of the limited time, and the limited time is the time that is the one who is in the earth. The Qur'an repeats the idea that the earthly life entails the enjoyment for a while in various ways in the other verses, and the repetition is the repetition that is the form of the teaching: the fleeting and limited enjoyment of this world is the enjoyment that is in the form of the contrast with the permanence and totality of the enjoyment or the punishment in the Hereafter (cf. 3:14; 4:77; 28:60; 40:39; 42:36). The contrast of the two enjoyments is the contrast that is the form of the logic of the choice: the man who chooses the enjoyment of the earth is the man who is in the condition of the one who leaves the enjoyment of the Hereafter, and the man who chooses the enjoyment of the Hereafter is the man who is in the condition of the one who leaves the enjoyment of the earth.
