**The Fall as the Occasion of the Mercy**

The Sufi commentators emphasized the providential and even merciful aspect of the fall of Adam and Eve, and the aspect is the aspect that is the form of the teaching that is worth stating in full. The act of the disobedience of Adam and Eve led to the assumption of the vicegerency on the earth, and the assumption of the vicegerency is the assumption that is the one who is in the earth. The fall offered to Adam, and by extension to all human beings, the opportunity to draw near to God once again, and the drawing near is the drawing near that is only possible after the experience of the distance and the exile. The logic of the teaching is the logic that is worth stating: the nearness that is possible only after the distance is the nearness that is the one who knows the meaning of the nearness, and the man who has never been far from God has never known the meaning of the nearness.

The eighth-/fourteenth-century Shādhilī master Ibn ʿAṭāʾ Allāh (d. 921/1515) stated the teaching in the form of the aphorism in his book *al-Ḥikam*:

> *"An act of disobedience that bequeaths humility and need is better than an act of obedience that bequeaths might and pride."* (Al-Ḥikam, no. 96)

The aphorism is the form of the teaching: the fall that bequeaths the humility and the need is the fall that is the repentance, and the repentance is the state that is the form of the perfection that was not possible without the experience of the fall.

The fall also afforded God the opportunity to manifest His Attribute of Forgiveness, and the manifestation of the Attribute of Forgiveness is the manifestation that is the one who is forgiven. The verse, in its form, is the form of the statement of the descent, and the statement of the descent is the form of the introduction of the verse that follows, in which God states the form of the state of the man who is in the earth: the state of the life, the state of the death, and the state of the being brought forth.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:25

> **He said, “Therein you shall live, and therein you shall die, and from there shall you be brought forth.”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Three Clauses, One Place**

The verse is built on a single repeated pronoun. *Fīhā* — "therein", "in it" — governs all three clauses, and the antecedent is the earth named in the command to descend that precedes it. Life is in it, death is in it, and the coming forth is from it. Nothing in the sequence happens anywhere else.

That is the whole force of the construction, and it is easy to read past. The verse does not say that man will live, die and be raised; it says that all three will happen *in relation to one place*, and that the place is the same. The earth is not a corridor the human being passes through on the way to somewhere more important. It is the location of the beginning, the location of the end, and the location of the return.

> *"He said: Therein you shall live, and therein you shall die, and from there shall you be brought forth."*

The third clause changes its preposition deliberately. The first two use *fīhā*, in it; the third uses *minhā*, from it. Life and death are contained by the earth; the rising is an emergence out of it. The same ground that closes over a body is the ground that gives it back.

**Who Is Being Addressed**

The command to descend in verse 24 is plural — *ihbiṭū*, get down, all of you — and al-Ṭabarī reads it as addressed to Adam and Eve, to their future progeny, and to Iblīs together. The enmity named in the same breath, *each of you an enemy to the other*, is therefore not only a statement about the human being and the tempter but about human beings among themselves.

The scope matters for what follows. Verse 13 had banished Iblīs from the Garden specifically: *Get down from it.* This command is wider — a descent from the celestial realm altogether — and it carries the whole human race with it. Verse 25 is what the descent leads to, and it is addressed to everyone who descended.

The verse is also a statement of the terms. Nothing is negotiated; nothing is offered back. The three clauses are declarative, and the human being is told where his life will run and where it will end before he has taken a step.

**A Fall Read as Mercy**

Some Sufi commentators read the whole sequence providentially, and even mercifully. Al-Jīlī and those who follow him note that the act of disobedience led to Adam's assumption of the vicegerency on earth, and that it opened a way of drawing near to God which is only available after distance has been experienced. Proximity that has never been lost is not the same thing as proximity recovered.

Ibn ʿAṭāʾ Allāh, the eighth-/fourteenth-century Shādhilī master, put the principle in a single line of his *Ḥikam*:

> *"An act of disobedience that bequeaths humility and need is better than an act of obedience that bequeaths might and pride."* (Ibn ʿAṭāʾ Allāh, al-Ḥikam 96)

The comparison is not between disobedience and obedience but between two outcomes. What is being weighed is what the act leaves in the soul. An obedience that produces self-sufficiency has lost the thing worship exists for; a fall that produces need has found it. On this reading the descent is not only a punishment but the condition of a perfection that was not reachable otherwise — because it requires repentance, and repentance requires something to return from.

The same reading gives the fall a second consequence, on God's side rather than man's. It occasions the manifestation of an attribute that innocence would never have disclosed. Forgiveness is only manifest where there is something to forgive.

**The Second Account**

This verse closes the second telling of the Adam narrative in the order of the muṣḥaf. The first is at 2:30–39; later accounts run at 17:61–65 and 20:115–24, and at 15:28–43 and 38:71–85 the same story is told of the first human being without naming him.

The repetition is not redundancy, and the differences are where the meaning lies. Each account closes differently, and this one closes on the earth: on where the story's descendants will actually live, die and rise. The telling in Sūrah Ṭā Hā closes on the warning not to forget; this one closes on a geography.

That is why the three clauses are worth taking as a unit rather than as a summary. The verse is the Qur'an's most compressed statement of the human condition — one place, three events, no exit from the sequence and no alternative venue for any of them.

**What the Sequence Forecloses**

The order is fixed and no stage can be skipped. The man who hopes to arrive at the being-brought-forth without passing through the dying has misunderstood the verse; the one who hopes to live somewhere other than the earth has misunderstood it too.

The practical weight of that is not morbid. A life lived on the assumption that it will end in the same ground it began in is a life in which the ground has a claim. The verse makes the earth a witness to the whole of a human career — present at the living, present at the dying, and the thing that hands the person back. It is not scenery.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:26

> **O Children of Adam! We have indeed sent down upon you raiment to cover your nakedness, and rich adornment. But the raiment of reverence, that is better. This is among the signs of God, that haply they may remember.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Address to the Children of Adam**

The verse shifts from the story of Adam to the address to the humanity in general, and the address is the address that is the form of the universal teaching: *O Children of Adam!* The address to the Children of Adam is the address that the sūrah uses in the same form in several places (7:27, 30, 31), and the address is the address that is the form of the state of the humanity as the children of the first man. The shift from the story to the address is the shift that is the form of the method of the sūrah: the story of Adam is the story that is the form of the state of the humanity, and the address to the Children of Adam is the address that is the form of the application of the story to the humanity.

**The Raiment That Is Sent Down**

The verse states the gift of the raiment, and the gift is the gift that is in the form of the sending down: *We have indeed sent down upon you raiment*. The word for "sent down" is *anzalnā*, from the root *n-z-l*, and the root is the same root that is used for the revelation of the Book: the raiment is the raiment that is in the form of the blessing that descends from God, and the blessing that descends from God is the blessing that is in the one who is given the gift. The connection of the gift of the raiment with the story of the leaves in verse 22 is the connection that is the form of the teaching: the leaves that Adam and Eve sewed together to cover themselves were the cover of the state of the exposure, and the raiment that God sent down is the raiment that is the form of the cover that replaces the cover of the leaves. The gift of the raiment is the gift that follows the state of the exposure, and the gift is the gift that is the form of the state of the man who is in the earth.

The word for "raiment" is *libās*, from the root *l-b-s*, and the root carries the sense of the covering, the one who is covered. The raiment is the raiment that has the two functions that the verse names: the function of the covering of the nakedness, and the function of the adornment. The two functions are the two functions that are the man who is in the condition of the one who is in the earth: the man needs the cover for the sake of the modesty, and the man needs the adornment for the sake of the beauty. The two functions are the two functions of the same gift, and the gift is the gift that is the form of the state of the humanity.

**The Rich Adornment**

The verse names the second function of the raiment, and the function is the function of the adornment: *and rich adornment*. The word for "adornment" is *zuḥhā*, from the root *z-h-h*, and the root carries the sense of the beauty, the ornament, the one who is adorned. The adornment is the adornment that is the man who is in the condition of the one who is in the form of the beauty. The gift of the adornment is the gift that is the form of the state of the humanity as the creature of the modesty and the beauty, and the state of the creature of the modesty and the beauty is the state that is the form of the teaching: the man is the creature who needs the cover for the sake of the modesty, and the man is the creature who needs the adornment for the sake of the beauty.

**The Raiment of Reverence**

The verse states the teaching that is the form of the core of the verse: *But the raiment of reverence, that is better*. The word for "reverence" is *taqwā*, from the root *q-w-y*, and the root carries the sense of the guarding, the protecting, the fence. The word for "raiment" in the phrase is the same word that is in the first part of the verse: the raiment of the reverence is the raiment that is the form of the inner cover, and the inner cover is the cover that is the form of the state of the soul.

The logic of the metaphor is the logic that is worth stating in the form of the principle: as the body needs the raiment to protect it from the elements and to maintain the modesty, so the soul needs the reverence to protect it from the corruption of the sin and to maintain the dignity. The raiment of the body is the raiment that covers the outside, and the raiment of the reverence is the raiment that covers the inside, and the inside is the one who is in the place of the account. The man who is in the state of the one who has the raiment of the body without the raiment of the reverence is the man who is in the condition of the one who is covered in the outside and exposed in the inside, and the state of the one who is covered in the outside and exposed in the inside is the state that is the nakedness of the soul.

The hadith that the sources gather states the form of the state of the reverence in the way that makes the teaching concrete: the Prophet ﷺ said,

> *"Taqwa (reverence) is here,"* — and he pointed to his chest three times. (Muslim)

The hadith is the form of the verse: the reverence is in the chest, and the chest is the place of the heart, and the heart is the form of the inside, and the inside is the one who is in the place of the raiment of the reverence.

**The Sign of God**

The verse closes with the statement of the state of the gift, and the state is the state of the sign of God: *This is among the signs of God, that haply they may remember*. The gift of the raiment is the gift that is in the form of the sign, and the sign is the sign that is the form of the state of the blessing of God. The purpose of the sign is the purpose that is the form of the teaching: *that haply they may remember*. The remembrance is the remembrance that is the one who is connected to the source of the blessing. The man who remembers the source of the blessing is the man who is in the gratitude, and the gratitude is the gratitude that is the raiment of the reverence.

The verse, in its form, is the form of the teaching of the two raiments: the raiment of the body, and the raiment of the reverence. The two raiments are the two raiments that are the man who is in the condition of the one who is in the earth, and the man who is in the condition of the one who is in the earth is the state that is the form of the warning of the sūrah: the man who has the raiment of the body is the man who is in the condition of the one who needs the raiment of the reverence, and the raiment of the reverence is the raiment that is the one who is in the place of the proximity to God.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:27

> **O Children of Adam! Let not Satan tempt you, as he caused your parents to go forth from the Garden, stripping them of their raiment to show them their nakedness. Surely he sees you — he and his tribe — whence you see them not. We have indeed made the satans the friends of those who do not believe.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Temptation as the Stripping**

The verse is the warning to the Children of Adam that follows the gift of the raiment, and the warning is the warning that is the form of the connection between the two verses: the gift of the raiment in verse 26, and the warning of the stripping of the raiment in this verse. The word for "tempt" is *takhtumsakum*, from the root *k-m-s*, and the root carries the sense of the stripping, the removing of the cover. The same root is used in the second part of the verse for the act of Satan upon Adam and Eve: *stripping them of their raiment to show them their nakedness*. The two uses of the root are the two uses that are the form of the teaching: the temptation is the temptation that is in the form of the stripping, and the stripping is the stripping that is the form of the exposure.

The verse returns to the story of the parents as the form of the warning, and the return is the return that is the form of the method of the sūrah: the past is the warning of the present, and the story of the parents is the story that is the form of the state of the humanity. The warning is the warning that is the form of the statement: the act of Satan upon the parents is the act that is the one who is stripped, and the state of the one who is stripped is the state that is the form of the descent from the Garden.

**The Asymmetry of the Vision**

The verse states the form of the relation between the humanity and Satan, and the form is the form of the asymmetry of the vision: *Surely he sees you — he and his tribe — whence you see them not*. The word for "tribe" is *šiyaʿatuhu*, from the root *š-y-ʿ*, and the root carries the sense of the followers, the tribe, the following. The tribe of Satan is the tribe of the satans, and the satans are the satans that are in the form of the unseen. The asymmetry of the vision is the asymmetry that is the form of the teaching: Satan sees the man, and the man does not see Satan, and the state of the one who is seen and does not see is the state that is the exposure.

The hadith that the sources gather states the form of the state of the unseen in the way that makes the teaching concrete:

> *"Satan circulates in the son of Adam like the circulation of blood."* (Muslim)

The hadith is the form of the verse: the circulation of Satan is the circulation that is in the one who is not seen, and the state of the one who is not seen is the state that is the form of the asymmetry of the vision.

**The Satans as the Friends of the Disbelievers**

The verse closes with the statement of the state of the satans in the form of the relation to the one who does not believe: *We have indeed made the satans the friends of those who do not believe*. The word for "friends" is *awliyāʾ*, and the word is the same word that is in verse 3 of this sūrah: *follow not any protectors apart from Him*. The connection of the two verses is the connection that is the form of the structure of the sūrah: the one who does not take God as the protector is the one who takes the satans as the protectors, and the state of the one who takes the satans as the protectors is the state that is the enmity with God.

The two verses of the sūrah (7:3 and 7:27) are the two forms of the same statement: the statement of the exclusive loyalty, and the statement of the consequence of the removal of the exclusive loyalty.

**The Practical Consequence of the Warning**

The defense of the man who is in the warning is the defense that is the form of the remembrance of God, and the remembrance of God is the remembrance that is the raiment of the reverence. The hadith that the sources gather states the form of the defense in the way that makes the teaching concrete:

> *"When a man sleeps, Satan ties three knots at the joints of his nape, and strikes between his two shoulders, saying: The night is long, so sleep. Then, if he wakes in the night and mentions God, he unties one knot for every mention; and if he performs the prayer, he unties the knots and rises with a good spirit; and if he does not, he rises with a bad spirit and a lazy body."* (Bukhari)

The hadith is the form of the verse: the knots of Satan are the knots that are the sleep, and the remembrance of God is the remembrance that is the form of the undoing of the knots, and the undoing of the knots is the defense.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:28

> **When they commit an indecency, they say, “We found our fathers practicing it, and God has commanded us thus.” Say, “Truly God commands not indecency. Do you say of God that which you know not?”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Two Excuses of the One Who Commits**

The verse describes the state of the one who commits the indecency, and the state is the one who makes the excuse. The word for "indecency" is *fāḥišah*, from the root *f-h-š*, and the root carries the sense of the great transgression, the obscenity, the thing that is in the form of the excess. The state of the one who commits the indecency is the state of the one who does not admit to the sin in the form of the admission; the one who commits the indecency is the one who is in the condition of the one who makes the excuse, and the excuse is the excuse that is the form of the removal of the responsibility.

The verse states the two excuses that the one who commits the indecency makes, and the two excuses are the two forms of the same act: the excuse of the tradition, and the excuse of the authority. The first excuse is the excuse of the tradition: *We found our fathers practicing it*. The second excuse is the excuse of the authority: *God has commanded us thus*. The two excuses are the two excuses that are the one who dresses the sin in the clothes of the legitimacy.

**The Logic of the Two Excuses**

The logic of the two excuses is the logic that is worth stating in the form of the principle.

The two removals are the two removals that are the sin without the responsibility.

**The Reply of the Prophet**

The verse states the reply of the Prophet to the two excuses, and the reply is the reply that is the form of the correction of the two excuses. The first part of the reply is the correction of the excuse of the authority: *Say: "Truly God commands not indecency."* The statement is the statement that is the form of the correction of the attribute of God: God is the One who is free from the command of the indecency, and the command of the indecency is the command that is not the command of God. The command of God is the command that is in the form of the justice, and the justice is the justice that is the one who is free from the command of the indecency.

The second part of the reply is the rebuke that is the form of the statement of the state of the one who makes the excuse: *Do you say of God that which you know not?* The rebuke is the rebuke that is the form of the warning of the attribution to God of what God did not command. The Qur'an states the warning of the attribution in the form of the statement in the other verses, and the statement is the statement that is the form of the confirmation of the warning:

> *"Do not say about your tongues' falsehoods, 'This is lawful and this is forbidden,' fabricating a lie against God. Indeed, he who fabricates a lie against God will not succeed."* (16:116)

The verse is the form of the confirmation: the attribution to God of what God did not command is the attribution that is in the form of the fabrication of the lie against God, and the fabrication of the lie against God is the fabrication that is the one who does not succeed.

**The Modern Form of the Two Excuses**

The verse, in the form of the two excuses, is the form of the warning that is repeated in the modern form. The modern form of the excuse of the tradition is the modern form of the statement: *this is how we have always done it*, and the statement is the statement that is the form of the excuse of the custom. The modern form of the excuse of the authority is the modern form of the statement: *this is our religion*, and the statement is the statement that is the form of the excuse of the label. The two modern forms are the two forms of the same act: the act of the dressing of the sin in the clothes of the legitimacy.

The cure of the state is the cure that is the form of the reply of the verse: the correction of the attribute of God, and the acknowledgment of the state of the one who says of God that which he does not know. The man who is in the warning is the man who is in the condition of the one who tests the claim: *Do you know that God commanded this?* The test of the claim is the test that is the knowledge, and the knowledge is the knowledge that is the reply of the verse.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:29

> **Say, “My Lord has commanded justice. So set your faces [toward Him] at every place of prayer, and call upon Him, devoting religion entirely to Him. Just as He originated you, so shall you return.”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Command of the Balance**

The verse opens with the statement of the command of God, and the command is the command that is the form of the standard: *My Lord has commanded justice*. The word for "justice" is *al-qisṭ*, from the root *q-s-ṭ*, and the root carries the sense of the balance, the equity, the putting of the thing in its place. The command of the balance is the command that is the form of the standard of the act of the man: the act of the man is the act that is in the form of the balance, and the balance is the balance that is the putting of the thing in its place. The command of God is the command that is in the form of the balance, and the balance is the balance that is the one who is free from the excess and the deficiency.

The word for "commanded" is *aṣḍarā*, from the root *ʿ--r*, and the root carries the sense of the command, the directing. The command of God is the command that is in the form of the directing of the man to the balance, and the directing of the man to the balance is the directing that is the standard.

**The Turning of the Face**

The verse states the first form of the practice that follows the command of the balance, and the form is the form of the turning of the face: *So set your faces [toward Him] at every place of prayer*. The word for "set your faces" is *ūʿīnāhu*, from the root *ʿ-w-r*, and the root carries the sense of the turning of the face, the directing of the face. The turning of the face is the turning that is in the form of the directing of the man to God, and the directing of the man to God is the directing that is the worship.

The verse adds the detail of the place and the time: *at every place of prayer*. The detail is the detail that is the form of the teaching: the turning of the face is the turning that is the worship at every place and at every time. The commentators noted the two readings of the phrase: the reading that is the form of the place of the prayer, and the reading that is the form of the time of the prayer. The two readings are the two forms of the same teaching: the worship of the man is the worship that is the turning of the face at every place and at every time.

**The Call and the Sincerity**

The verse states the second form of the practice, and the form is the form of the call: *and call upon Him*. The word for "call" is *udʿū*, from the root *d-ʿ-w*, and the root carries the sense of the call, the address. The call to God is the call that is the supplication, and the supplication is the supplication that is the connection to the source.

The verse adds the quality that is the form of the state of the practice: *devoting religion entirely to Him*. The word for "devoting" is *mukhlisīn*, from the root *kh-l-ṣ*, and the root carries the sense of the purity, the singling out, the purification. The devotion of the religion entirely to God is the devotion that is the purification of the worship from the mixing of the worship with the worship of the other than God. The hadith that the sources gather states the form of the sincerity in the way that makes the teaching concrete:

> *"The religion is sincere counsel (naṣīah)."* We said: To whom, O Messenger of God? He said: *"To God, to His Book, to His Messenger, to the leaders of the Muslims and their common folk."* (Muslim)

The hadith is the form of the verse: the sincerity is the sincerity that is the devotion of the religion entirely to God.

**The Return That Is the Form of the Argument**

The verse closes with the statement of the return, and the statement is the statement that is the form of the argument of the creation to the resurrection: *Just as He originated you, so shall you return*. The word for "originated" is *bāʾadakum*, from the root *b-ʾ-d*, and the root carries the sense of the bringing forth, the origination, the bringing forth from the nothing. The argument of the creation to the resurrection is the argument that is the form of the logic: the One who brought the man forth from the nothing is the One who will bring the man back, and the bringing back is the bringing back that is the return.

The Qur'an states the same argument in the form of the statement in the other verses, and the statement is the statement that is the form of the confirmation of the argument:

> *"He who created you the first time will create you again, and you will not be able to resist it."* (29:19–20, in the form of the statement of the argument)

The verse is the form of the confirmation: the creation from the nothing is the creation that is in the form of the evidence of the possibility of the re-creation, and the re-creation is the re-creation that is the return.

The verse, in its form, is the form of the chain of the teaching: the standard of the balance, the practice of the turning of the face and the call, the quality of the sincerity, and the end of the return.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:30

> **Some He has rightly guided, and some are deserving of error. Truly they took satans as their protectors apart from God and deem them rightly guided.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Two States of the Humanity**

The verse states the form of the division of the humanity, and the form is the form of the two states: the state of the one who is rightly guided, and the state of the one who is deserving of error. The word for "rightly guided" is *wahāda*, from the root *w-y-d*, and the root carries the sense of the guidance, the directing to the path. The word for "deserving of error" is *ḍalla*, from the root *ḍ-l-l*, and the root carries the sense of the straying, the going aside from the path. The two states are the two states that are the form of the division of the humanity, and the division is the division that is the choice.

The ʿaqīdah of the guidance and the error is the ʿaqīdah that is the form of the balance of the divine decree and the human responsibility, and the balance is the balance that is the teaching of the Qur'an. The Qur'an states the teaching in the form of the statement: the One who guides is the One who guides the one who strives, and the One who causes to stray is the One who abandons the one who chooses the rejection. The verse in Sūrah al-Furqān states the form of the guidance of the one who strives:

> *"And as for those who strive for Us, We will surely guide them to Our ways. And indeed, God is with those who do good."* (29:69, in the form of the statement of the guidance of the one who strives)

The verse is the form of the teaching: the guidance of God is the guidance that is in the one who strives, and the straying of God is the straying that is in the one who chooses the rejection. The "deserving of error" of the verse is the "deserving" that is the straying by his own choice, and the choice is the choice that is the form of the responsibility.

**The Satans as the Protectors**

The verse states the one who is deserving of error, and the form is the form of the taking of the satans as the protectors: *Truly they took satans as their protectors apart from God*. The word for "protectors" is *awliyāʾ*, and the word is the same word that is in verse 3 and verse 27 of this sūrah. The three verses are the three forms of the same statement: the statement of the exclusive loyalty, the statement of the consequence of the removal of the exclusive loyalty, and the statement of the state of the one who takes the satans as the protectors.

**The Inversion of the Guide**

The verse states the detail that is the form of the core of the verse: *and deem them rightly guided*. The detail is the detail that is the form of the inversion: the one who is in the condition of the one who takes the satans as the protectors is the one who is in the state of the one who deems the satans to be the guides, and the satans are the satans that are in the form of the misleaders. The inversion of the guide and the misleader is the inversion that is the form of the greatest form of the error: the one who is astray is the one who does not see that he is astray, and the one who does not see that he is astray is the one who is in the state of the one who deems the error to be the guidance.

The greatest form of the error is not the form of the error that is seen as the error, but the form of the error that is seen as the guidance. The hadith that the sources gather states the following of the satan in the way that makes the teaching concrete:

> *"Satan sits at every junction of the path of the children of Adam; he turns back to the one who mentions God, and when he leaves the mention, he provokes him to the sin, and the sin is more beloved to him."* (Al-Tirmidhī)

The hadith is the form of the verse: the junction of the path is the junction that is the choice, and the satan is at the junction in the form of the one who provokes to the sin, and the one who follows the provocation is the one who is in the state of the one who deems the sin to be the path.

**The Social Dimension of the Inversion**

The verse, in the form of the inversion, is the form of the warning that has the social dimension. The modern form of the taking of the satans as the protectors is the modern form of the taking of the ideology that leads to the destruction as the guide, and the ideology that leads to the destruction is the ideology that is in the one who deems the destruction to be the guidance. The modern form of the inversion is the modern form of the statement of the leader who is followed as the guide but who is in the form of the misleader, and the leader who is in the form of the misleader is the leader that is in the one who deems the misguidance to be the guidance. The man who is in the warning is the man who is in the condition of the one who tests the guide: *Is this the guide that is in the form of the guidance, or is this the guide that is in the form of the misguidance?* The test of the guide is the test that is the knowledge, and the knowledge is the knowledge that is the reply of the verse.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:31

> **O Children of Adam! Put on your adornment at every place of worship, and eat and drink, but be not prodigal. Truly He loves not the prodigal.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Adornment at the Places of Worship**

The verse is the command to the Children of Adam that follows the teaching of the raiment of the reverence in verse 26, and the command is the command that is the form of the connection of the two raiments: the raiment of the body and the raiment of the reverence. The verse commands the wearing of the adornment at the places of worship: *Put on your adornment at every place of worship*. The word for "adornment" is *libāsakum*, and the word is the same word that is in verse 26, and the connection of the two verses is the connection that is the form of the teaching: the man who is in the worship is the man who is in the condition of the one who wears the adornment of the body and who wears the adornment of the reverence, and the two adornments are the two adornments that are the worship.

The word for "places of worship" is *al-maḥārib*, from the root *ḥ-w-b*, and the root carries the sense of the place of the standing, the place of the congregation, the gathering for the worship. The command of the wearing of the adornment at the places of worship is the command that is the form of the correction of the neglect of the body in the worship. The worship of the man is the worship that is the whole of the man, and the whole of the man is the whole that is in the form of the body and the soul, and the body is the body that is in the one who needs the adornment. The hadith that the sources gather states the form of the teaching in the way that makes it concrete:

> *"A man asked the Prophet: O Messenger of God, which is better for a man, the camel that is shaggy-haired or the one that is sleek-haired? He said: The sleek-haired one. Indeed, God is beautiful and loves beauty."* (Abū Dāwūd)

The hadith is the form of the verse: the beauty of the body is the beauty that is the adornment, and the beauty is the beauty that is the form of the attribute of God, and the attribute of God is the attribute that is the loving of the beauty.

**The Permission of the Eating and the Drinking**

The verse states the permission of the eating and the drinking, and the permission is the permission that is the form of the statement of the blessing: *and eat and drink*. The two commands are the two commands that are in the form of the statement of the lawfulness of the things of the body: the food and the drink. The religion of God is the religion that is in the one who permits the lawful pleasures of the body, and the lawful pleasures of the body are the pleasures that are the blessing of God. The religion of God is not the religion of the deprivation of the body; the religion of God is the religion of the balance of the body and the soul, and the balance of the body and the soul is the balance that is the permission of the eating and the drinking.

**The Prohibition of the Excess**

The verse states the prohibition that follows the permission, and the prohibition is the prohibition that is the form of the limit: *but be not prodigal*. The word for "prodigal" is *tasrifū*, from the root *s-r-f*, and the root carries the sense of the transgression, the spending wastefully, the exceeding of the limit, the going beyond the boundary.

The Qur'an states the law of the middle in the form of the statement of the state of the community of the believers:

> *"And thus We have made you a middle (wasat) community, that you may be witnesses over the mankind."* (2:143, in the form of the statement of the middle)

The verse is the form of the confirmation of the law of the balance: the community of the believers is the community that is in the form of the middle, and the middle is the middle that is in the one who is not in the form of the excess and the form of the one who is not in the form of the deficiency.

**The Attribute of the Love and the State of the Excess**

The verse closes with the statement of the attribute of God, and the attribute is the attribute of the love: *Truly He loves not the prodigal*. The word for "loves not" is *lā yuḥibbu*, from the root *h-b-b*, and the root carries the sense of the love, the attraction. The statement of the attribute of the love is the statement that is in the form of the teaching: God is the One who is in the one who loves, and the love of God is the love that is in the one who loves the balance and who does not love the excess. The logic of the statement is the logic that is the form of the connection with the verse of the balance in this sūrah (7:8): the balance is the balance that is the justice, and the excess is the excess that is the removal of the balance.

The hadith that the sources gather states the form of the balance of the consumption in the way that makes the teaching concrete:

> *"No human being fills a vessel worse than the stomach of a human being. It is sufficient for the son of Adam to eat a few mouthfuls to keep his back straight. But if he must (eat more), then one-third for his food, one-third for his drink, and one-third for his breath."* (Al-Tirmidhī)

The hadith is the form of the verse: the balance of the consumption is the balance that is the eating of the few mouthfuls, and the few mouthfuls are the few mouthfuls that are the keeping of the back straight.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:32

> **Say, “Who has forbidden the adornment of God, which He has brought forth for His servants, and the good things among [His] provision?” Say, “These are for those who believe, in the life of this world, and on the Day of Resurrection they are for them alone.” Thus do We expound the signs for a people who know.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Rhetorical Question of the Forbidding**

The verse opens with the rhetorical question that is the form of the refutation: *Say: "Who has forbidden the adornment of God, which He has brought forth for His servants, and the good things among [His] provision?"* The question is the question that is in the form of the statement of the refutation of the claim of the ascetics, and the claim of the ascetics is the claim that is in the one who forbids himself the lawful things on the grounds that God has forbidden them. The question is the question that is in the form of the refutation of the claim: the one who says that God has forbidden the adornment is the one who is in the condition of the one who says of God that which he does not know, and the state of the one who says of God that which he does not know is the state that is the fabrication of the lie against God. The connection of the verse with verse 28 of this sūrah is the connection that is the form of the structure: the excuse of the authority in verse 28, and the refutation of the excuse of the authority in this verse.

**The Adornment That Is Brought Forth**

The verse states the form of the adornment, and the form is the form of the thing that is brought forth: *which He has brought forth for His servants*. The word for "brought forth" is *akhraja*, from the root *k-h-r-j*, and the root carries the sense of the bringing forth, the bringing out from the state of the hidden. The adornment of God is the adornment that is the blessing that is brought forth from God, and the blessing that is brought forth from God is the blessing that is the gift. The logic of the teaching is the logic that is worth stating: the renouncing of the gift is the renouncing that is the rejection of the giver, and the rejection of the giver is the rejection that is the ingratitude. The man who renounces the lawful gift of God is the man who is in the condition of the one who is in the one who does not know the form of the blessing.

The word for "good things" is *ṭayyibāti al-rizq*, from the root *ṭ-y-b*, and the root carries the sense of the purity, the goodness, the purity. The good things of the provision are the good things that are the lawfulness and the purity, and the lawfulness and the purity are the lawfulness and the purity that are the blessing of God.

**The Enjoyment in the Two Worlds**

The verse states the form of the enjoyment of the adornment and the good things, and the form is the form of the two states: the state of the enjoyment in this world, and the state of the enjoyment in the Hereafter. The first state is the state of the enjoyment in this world: *These are for those who believe, in the life of this world*. The statement is the statement that is in the form of the correction of the claim of the ascetics: the believers are the ones who enjoy the adornment and the good things in this world, and the enjoyment of the believers is the enjoyment that is the use of the blessing in the form of the gratitude. The second state is the state of the enjoyment in the Hereafter: *on the Day of Resurrection they are for them alone*. The statement is the statement that is in the form of the exclusivity: the enjoyment of the Hereafter is the enjoyment that is the exclusive enjoyment of the believers, and the exclusive enjoyment of the believers is the exclusive enjoyment that is the return to the One who is the One who is the source of the blessing.

The logic of the two states is the logic that is worth stating: the blessing is the blessing that is the shared enjoyment in this world, and the blessing is the blessing that is the exclusive enjoyment in the Hereafter. The difference between the two states is the difference that is the use of the blessing: the believers use the blessing in the form of the gratitude, and the disbelievers use the blessing in the form of the excess, and the difference of the use is the difference that is the return.

**The Balance of the Religion**

The verse, in the form of the refutation of the claim of the ascetics, states the form of the balance of the religion, and the balance of the religion is the balance that is the permission of the lawful and the prohibition of the excess. The hadith that the sources gather states the form of the balance in the way that makes the teaching concrete:

> *"A man said: O Messenger of God, some of us fast and do not sleep at night, and we do not touch the women. The Prophet said: I am the one who is most afraid of God among you and most obedient to Him. But I fast and I do not fast, and I pray and I sleep, and I marry the women. Whoever turns away from my practice is not of me."* (Bukhari, Muslim)

The hadith is the form of the verse: the balance of the religion is the balance that is the fasting and the not fasting, the praying and the sleeping, and the marrying, and the balance is the balance that is in the form of the practice of the Prophet ﷺ, and the practice of the Prophet ﷺ is the practice that is the most afraid of God and the most obedient to God.

**The Expounding of the Signs**

The verse closes with the statement of the form of the expounding of the signs, and the form is the knowledge: *Thus do We expound the signs for a people who know*. The word for "expound" is *nunaddu*, from the root *b-y-n*, and the root carries the sense of the making clear, the making evident. The expounding of the signs is the expounding that is the making clear of the signs for the people who know, and the people who know are the people who are the capacity of the knowledge. The logic of the statement is the logic that is in the form of the teaching: the clarity of the signs is for those who have the capacity of the knowledge, and the capacity of the knowledge is the capacity that is the one who is in the form of the understanding of the signs.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:33

> **Say, “My Lord has only forbidden indecencies — both outward and inward — and sin, and tyranny without right, and that you should ascribe partners unto God, for which He has sent down no authority, and that you should say of God that which you know not.”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Short List of the Real Prohibitions**

After the two verses on adornment and the good things, this verse gives the other half of the same teaching: the things that are actually forbidden. The word that opens the list is one that does the quiet work of the whole verse — *only* (*innamā*). God has not forbidden the earth and its pleasures, God has forbidden a short and specific list, and the list is what the verse then states. Read the two verses together and the argument is complete: the ascetic who has renounced the adornment of God and the good things of His provision has mistaken his own severity for the command of God, and the command of God is the command named here, in the short form, with the word *only* doing the work of the correction. The religion permits a wide field and marks a narrow fence, and the man who builds a fence around the whole field has built a fence that is not God's.

**The Indecencies, Outward and Inward**

The first item of the list is the indecencies, and the verse adds the detail that makes the item broader than it first appears: *both outward and inward*. The word for indecency is *fāḥishah*, the same root that was used in verse 28 for the act the wrongdoer dresses in the clothes of his fathers and of God. The addition of the two words — outward (*zāhirah*) and inward (*bāṭinah*) — is the addition that the commentators drew out with care, and the teaching is one of the most practical in the sūrah. The outward indecency is the act that can be seen: the deed of the hand, the word of the tongue, the act of the body in the open. The inward indecency is the state that can be seen by no one but the One who sees the heart: the lust that is nursed, the desire that is entertained in the chamber of the mind, the intention that has not yet become an act but has already crossed the boundary in the heart. The law covers both, and the reason is the reason that the hadith states in one of its sharpest forms:

> *"The eye commits adultery, and its adultery is the looking; and the ear commits adultery, and its adultery is the hearing."* (Bukhari, Muslim)

The hadith is the form of the verse: the indecency of the inward is the indecency that the outward will follow if it is not guarded, and the guarding of the inward is the guarding that is the form of the real modesty. The man who is pure in the act but who is not pure in the state is the man who has guarded the door and not the house. The logic of the double prohibition is the logic of the seed and the tree: the inward indecency is the seed, and the outward indecency is the tree that the seed grows into, and the law that forbids the tree alone is a law that has left the seed in the ground.

**The Sin, the Tyranny, and the Two Attributions**

The list continues with *sin* — the comprehensive word that covers the wrong in its general form — and with *tyranny without right*, from the root *b-gh-y*, the transgression, the pushing of a thing beyond its boundary, the taking of a place that is not one's place. The tyranny of the verse is the social form of the sin: the sin that is done by one man upon the other, the force that is applied without the right, the measure that is taken by the strong against the weak. The word *without right* (*bi-l-ḥaqq*) adds the qualification that matters: the verse is not forbidding the establishment of order, which is the act of the authority that is given the right; the verse is forbidding the establishment of order by one who has not the right, the tyranny that is the form of the force without the mandate.

The last two items of the list are the items that are the theological and the epistemological forms of the prohibition. The first is the ascribing of partners to God, or the attributing to God of that which has no authority in the revelation — the act of building a religion of one's own and naming it the religion of God. The second is the saying of a lie against God, the attribution to Him of words and commands that He has not spoken. The two items are the two forms of the same act: the man who adds a partner to God and the man who puts words in God's mouth are both in the state of the one who has placed himself in the position of the legislator, and the position of the legislator is the position that belongs to God alone. The verse, in its list, is the map of the whole moral law in the form of the prohibitions: the indecency in the heart and in the act, the sin in the general, the tyranny in the social, and the two attributions in the theological and the epistemological. A man who is guarded against these five is guarded against the whole field of the wrong, and the field of the wrong is the field that is fenced by the short list, while the rest of the field is open.

**The Prohibition That Is the Form of the Justice**

There is a point of the ʿaqīdah in the shape of the list that is worth stating. The list begins with the indecency, which is the wrong against the self, passes to the sin and the tyranny, which are the wrongs against the other, and ends with the ascription and the lie, which are the wrongs against God. The order of the list is the order of the gravity as it rises: from the wrong that is done within, to the wrong that is done between, to the wrong that is done to the source of the whole. The law of God is the law that is built on the same order, and the order is the form of the justice: the justice that begins with the self and ends with God, and the man who is just with his self and with the other is the man who is in the state of the one who is just with God, and the man who is unjust with God in the attribution is the man who is in the state of the one who has broken the whole order from the top.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:34

> **And for every community there is a term appointed. When their term comes, they shall not delay it by a single hour, nor shall they advance it.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Law of the Term**

The verse states one of the quiet laws of the Qur'an's view of history, and the law is the law that the whole narrative section of the sūrah will then demonstrate: *for every community there is a term appointed*. The word for community is *ummah*, and the word covers the people, the nation, and the age — the unit of the Qur'an's moral history that was named in the opening of the destroyed towns in verse 4. The word for the term is *ajal*, from the root *ʾ-j-l*, and the root carries the sense of the limit, the boundary of time, the point at which the thing ends. The law of the term is the law that says: the life of a community is not open-ended, and the life of a community is not a thing that the community can extend by its own effort in the way that a debtor extends a debt. The community has a term, and the term is appointed, and the appointment is the appointment of God.

The Qur'an states the same law in several places, and the repetition is the teaching. The verse in Sūrah ʿImrān says:

> *"For every community there is an appointed time."* (3:157)

And the verse in Sūrah al-Muʾminūn states the same in the same short form:

> *"For every community there is an appointed time, and when their time comes, they shall not remain behind it by an hour, nor shall they advance it."* (23:56)

The two verses are the forms of the law that this verse states, and the three verses together are the statement of the principle that the sūrah will then apply, case by case, to the people of Noah, the people of Hud, the people of Salih, the people of Lot, the people of Shuayb, and the people of Pharaoh.

**The Term That Is Neither Delayed Nor Advanced**

The verse adds the detail that is the detail of the precision: *When their term comes, they shall not delay it by a single hour, nor shall they advance it.* The two negations are the two sides of the same statement: the term cannot be pushed back, and the term cannot be brought forward. The community that has reached the point of the complete corruption cannot buy more time, and the community that has reached the point of the complete justice cannot be struck before its time. The precision of the *hour* in the verse is the precision that the commentators noted: even the smallest unit of the time that the community can measure is the unit that the term does not shift by. The term is the term that is fixed in the knowledge of God, and the fixing of the term is the fixing that is the form of the state of the decree.

The logic of the law is the logic that is worth stating in full, because it is the logic that the sūrah's narratives are built upon. The term of a community is not a date that is chosen at random and then applied; the term is the time at which the cause is ripe. A community that spends its years in the corruption is a community that is ripening its own harvest, and the harvest comes when the fruit is ripe, not before and not after. The term is the harvest-time of the choice, and the justice of the term is the justice of the harvest: the farmer does not cut the wheat before it is ripe, and the farmer does not let it stand after it has fallen. The community that is struck at its term is the community that has, by its own long choice, made the striking the necessary event, and the striking, when it comes, is the event that was already in the ground of the community's own acts. The verse, in its short form, is the statement of this law, and the law is the law that makes the destroyed towns of the sūrah not examples of cruelty but examples of the harvest.

**The Psychology of the Delay**

The verse names, in the negation, the instinct of the community that is in the state of the corruption: the instinct of the delay. The community that is in the state of the corruption wants the term to be delayed, and the wanting of the delay is the wanting that is the one who does not want to meet the account. The Qur'an's own description of the state of the disbelievers is the description of this instinct: they ask for the postponement, they ask for the respite, they ask for the time in which the warning will pass and the account will be forgotten. The verse closes the door of the delay with the negation, and the closing of the door is the closing that is the form of the warning. The hadith that the sources gather states the form of the law in the way that makes it concrete for the individual:

> *"The affair of the son of Adam is hastened by two things: his death and his poverty. And as for his health and his wealth, there is no term for them."* (Bukhari)

The hadith is the form of the verse applied to the person: the two things that come at their appointed time and cannot be delayed are the two things that the man wants most to delay, and the two things that do not have a fixed term — the health and the wealth — are the two things that the man treats as if they were fixed, and the man who treats the fixed as if it were open and the open as if it were fixed is the man who is in the state of the one who has not understood the law of the term.

**The Practical Consequence of the Law**

The practical consequence of the verse is the one that is the consequence of the whole teaching of the sūrah. The man who understands the law of the term is the man who understands that his own community and his own age are under the same law, and the man who understands that his own community is under the law of the term is the man who is in the state of the one who does not take the term of his own community for granted. The community that is in the state of the corruption is the community that is ripening its harvest, and the community that is in the state of the justice is the community that is the harvest that is not yet due. The verse, in its two lines, is the statement of the law that governs the whole narrative of the sūrah, and the law is the law that the destroyed towns of the sūrah are the demonstration of, and the demonstration is the demonstration that the term is the term of the choice, and the choice is the choice that the community made in its long day, and the long day is the day that is over for the towns that the sūrah will now name.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:35

> **O Children of Adam! Should there come unto you messengers from among yourselves, recounting My signs unto you, then whosoever is reverent and makes amends, no fear shall come upon them, nor shall they grieve.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Conditional That Governs Every Age**

The verse is built as a conditional, and the shape of the conditional is the shape of the whole teaching. The condition is the coming of messengers from among the people, recounting the signs of God; and the result is the safety of the one who is reverent and does good. The conditional is stated in the form that covers every age: it does not say "when this messenger comes" or "when that messenger comes"; it says *should there come unto you messengers*, in the plural and in the general form. The sūrah will then walk the condition through its prophets one by one — Noah, Hud, Salih, Lot, Shuayb, Moses — and each walk will return to the same result: the one who is reverent and does good is the one for whom there is no fear and no grief. The verse is the template, and the narratives of the sūrah are the applications of the template.

The two halves of the result are the two halves that the Qur'an uses again and again as the formula of the promise of the reverent: *no fear shall come upon them, nor shall they grieve*. The fear is the fear of the future — the fear of what is coming, the fear of the account; and the grief is the grief of the present and the past — the grief for what has been lost, the grief for what has been done. The reverent man is the man who is free of both: he is free of the fear of the future because he has prepared for the future, and he is free of the grief of the past because he has turned from the past. The formula appears in the same short form in several places in the Qur'an, and the repetition is the teaching:

> *"As for those who fear their Lord, for them is a garden... and no fear shall come upon them, nor shall they grieve."* (10:62–63, in the form of the same formula)

The two verses are the two forms of the same promise, and the promise is the promise that is the one who is reverent and does good.

**The Messenger From Among Yourselves**

The condition names the form of the messenger, and the form is the form that removes the last excuse: the messengers are *from among yourselves*. The messenger is of the same kind as the people to whom he is sent; he is a man, he eats as they eat, he speaks their tongue, he is born in their land. The Qur'an states the principle in several places:

> *"And there is no community but that a warner has passed among them."* (35:24)

> *"We have not sent any messenger except with the language of his people, that he may make things clear to them."* (14:4, in the form of the statement of the principle)

The logic of the form is the logic that is worth stating: the excuse of the strangeness is removed. The man cannot say that the message came from a foreign people, in a foreign tongue, in a form that is not the form of his own life. The messenger is from among him, and the message is in his tongue, and the message is addressed to his life. The form of the messenger is the form of the proof that the message is for the man, not for a being other than the man, and the message that is for the man is the message that the man cannot claim not to understand, because it is in the language of his own kind.

**The Two Conditions of the Safety**

The result names the two conditions of the safety, and the two conditions are the two that the Qur'an pairs again and again: the reverence (*taqwā*) and the good deed. The reverence is the state of the heart — the guarding of the heart from the neglect of the account. The good deed is the act of the body — the deed that is the one who acts in the form of the justice. The two conditions are the two sides of the same state: the man who is reverent in the heart is the man who does the good deed in the body, and the man who does the good deed in the body is the man who is in the state of the one who is reverent in the heart. The man who claims the reverence without the deed is the man who is in the claim without the state, and the man who claims the deed without the reverence is the man who is in the act without the quality.

**The Door That Is Open to the Whosoever**

The verse closes with the word that is the form of the invitation: *whosoever* (*faman*). The word is the word that opens the door to every person, without the limit of the lineage, without the limit of the birthplace, without the limit of the age. The safety of the verse is not the safety of the one who is born in the right place, or the one who is of the right family, or the one who is in the right age; the safety of the verse is the safety of the one who is reverent and does good, in whatever place and in whatever age. The word *whosoever* is the word that the sūrah uses in the same form in the narratives of the prophets, and the use of the word is the use that is the form of the teaching: the door of the safety is open to every person who is in the condition of the one who is reverent and does good, and the door is the door that is the invitation of the sūrah.

The verse, in its form, is the form of the invitation that is opposite to the warning of the destroyed towns in the opening of the sūrah. The destroyed towns are the towns that were in the state of the one who did not meet the condition, and the condition is the condition of the reverence and the good deed. The sūrah will now show the towns that did not meet the condition, and the showing of the towns is the showing that is the form of the warning that makes the condition of the verse concrete: the condition is not an abstract requirement, the condition is the condition that the towns did not meet, and the towns that did not meet the condition are the towns that the sūrah will now name.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:36

> **But those who deny Our signs and treat them with disdain, it is they who are the inhabitants of the Fire. They shall abide therein.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Two Stages of the Refusal**

The verse is the second half of the pair that verse 35 opens, and it names the state that is the opposite of the state of the reverence and the good deed. The state is the state of the one who denies the signs and treats them with disdain, and the two words of the state are the two stages of the refusal. The first stage is the denial (*kaẓabū*), from the root *k-ẓ-b*, and the root carries the sense of the refusal of the truth, the one who says the opposite of the truth. The second stage is the disdain (*istakbarū*), from the root *k-b-r*, and the root carries the sense of the taking of the greatness for oneself, the one who turns away from the thing with the contempt.

The two stages are the two stages that the commentators drew out with care, and the teaching is the teaching of the psychology of the refusal. The denial is the act of the mouth and the heart: the man says that the sign is false, and the man believes that the sign is false. The disdain is the act of the state: the man does not even enter into the examination of the sign, because the examination of the sign is, in the state of the man, beneath him. The man who denies the sign is the man who has examined the sign and refused it, and the man who treats the sign with disdain is the man who has not examined the sign, because the examination of the sign would cost him the state of his pride. The disdain is the protection of the pride, and the protection of the pride is the protection that is the one who does not want to meet the sign, because the meeting of the sign is the meeting that is the one who has to choose.

The Qur'an states the form of the disdain in the same way in the other verses, and the statement is the statement that is the form of the confirmation of the teaching:

> *"Who is more unjust than one who is reminded of the signs of his Lord and then turns away from them?"* (32:22, in the form of the statement of the turning away)

The verse is the form of the confirmation: the man who is reminded of the sign and who turns away from it is the man who is in the disdain, and the disdain is the disdain that is the injustice.

**The Inhabitants of the Fire**

The verse states the form of the fate of the two stages, and the form is the form of the statement of the deft: *it is they who are the inhabitants of the Fire*. The construction of the deft is the construction that is the form of the exclusive statement: the inhabitants of the Fire are the ones who deny the signs and treat them with disdain, and the state of the inhabitants of the Fire is the state that is the form of the result of the two stages. The verse adds the detail that is the detail of the eternity: *They shall abide therein*. The word for "abide" is *khālidūn*, from the root *kh-l-d*, and the root carries the sense of the permanence, the not leaving.

The aqīdah of the eternity of the punishment of the disbelievers is the aqīdah that the verse states, and the aqīdah is the aqīdah that the Qur'an states in the same form in several places. The verse in Sūrah al-Baqarah states the form of the eternity in the way that makes the teaching concrete:

> *"Indeed, those who disbelieve and die while they are disbelievers — for them is the curse of God and the angels and the people, all of them. They will abide therein; the shade will not vanish from them, nor will they be relieved."* (2:161–162, in the form of the statement of the eternity)

And the verse in Sūrah an-Nisāʾ states the form of the renewal of the punishment:

> *"Every time their skins are roasted therein, We replace them with other skins, that they may taste the punishment."* (4:56, in the form of the statement of the renewal)

The two verses are the two forms of the same statement, and the statement is the statement that is the form of the confirmation of the eternity of the verse.

**The Contrast of the Two Verses**

The verse, read with the verse that precedes it, is the form of the contrast that is the form of the balance of the sūrah. Verse 35 is the conditional of the safety: the one who is reverent and does good is the one for whom there is no fear and no grief. This verse is the statement of the fate: the one who denies the signs and treats them with disdain is the one who is in the state of the inhabitants of the Fire. The two verses are the two sides of the same balance, and the balance is the balance that is the weighing of the deeds in the opening of the sūrah (7:8). The two sides of the balance are the two sides that are the choice, and the choice is the choice that is the form of the responsibility.

The logic of the teaching is the logic that is worth stating: the two verses are not the two verses of the cruelty and the mercy of God, but the two verses of the justice of God. The justice of God is the justice that is in the one who gives to every person the result of his choice, and the result of the choice of the one who is reverent and does good is the result of the safety, and the result of the choice of the one who denies the signs and treats them with disdain is the result of the Fire. The two verses are the two forms of the justice, and the justice is the justice that is the balance of the sūrah.

The verse, in its two lines, is the form of the warning that is opposite to the invitation of the previous verse. The invitation of the previous verse is the invitation that is the door that is open, and the warning of this verse is the warning that is the door that is closed. The two verses are the two doors, and the two doors are the two doors that are the choice of the sūrah.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:37

> **And who does greater wrong than one who fabricates a lie against God or denies His signs? For such as these, their portion of the book will reach them, till, when Our messengers come to take them away, they will say, “Where is that which you used to call upon apart from God?” They will respond, “They have forsaken us.” And they bear witness against themselves that they were disbelievers.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Question of the Greatest Wrong**

The verse opens with the rhetorical question that the Qur'an uses for the naming of the greatest wrong: *Who does greater wrong than...* The question is not a question that expects an answer; it is a question that does the work of the definition. The two wrongs that are named in the question are the two that are the theological and epistemological forms of the wrong: the fabrication of a lie against God, and the denial of His signs. The first is the act of the man who adds to the religion of God what God did not reveal, and the second is the act of the man who removes from the religion of God what God did reveal. The man who adds and the man who removes are both in the state of the one who has placed himself in the position of the legislator, and the position of the legislator is the position that belongs to God alone. The question of the verse, in its form, is the continuation of the teaching of verse 33, in which the two attributions were the last items of the list of the prohibitions.

**The Portion of the Book**

The verse then moves to the scene of the account, and the scene begins with the arrival of the portion of the Book: *their portion of the book will reach them*. The portion is the portion of the record that belongs to them, the record of their deeds in the form in which the deeds were done. The arrival of the portion is the arrival that is the meeting of his own account. The logic of the teaching is the logic that the verse of the weighing in the opening of the sūrah (7:8) states: the account is not a reconstruction, the account is the disclosure of what was already recorded, and the recording was done by the One who was present in the hour of the act.

**The Seizure and the Question of the Idols**

The verse states the scene of the seizure, and the scene is the scene that the Qur'an describes in the same form in the other verses of the description of the Hour. The messengers of God come to take them away, and the taking away is the taking that is the removal from the state of the denial into the state of the exposure. The question that is addressed in the scene is the question that is the form of the core of the verse: *Where is that which you used to call upon apart from God?* The question is addressed to the idols, to the false gods, to the protectors that the disbelievers took apart from God in the verse of the opening of the sūrah (7:3) and in the verse of the satans (7:27, 7:30). The question is the question that the disbelievers asked in the life, and the question is now asked in the scene of the account, and the question is the question that is the seeking of the protector in the hour of the need.

**The Forsaking That Is the Form of the Exposure**

The answer of the idols is the answer that is the form of the exposure: *They have forsaken us*. The word for "forsaken" is *ṣaraūnā*, and the word carries the sense of the abandoning, the leaving of the thing in the one who is not protected. The forsyaking is the forsyaking that is the form of the reversal of the relation: the one who was worshipped forsakes the one who worshipped, and the protector that was taken forsakes the one who took it. The logic of the reversal is the logic that is worth stating: the relation of the worship is the relation that is in the one who gives the strength to the object of the worship, and the object of the worship that has no strength of its own is the object that is in the one who has no strength to give back. The idol is the idol that is in the one who has no strength, and the idol that has no strength is the idol that is in the one who forsakes the one who worshipped it in the hour of the need.

The parable of the spider's house in the verse of Sūrah al-ʿAnkabūt (29:41) is the parable that is the form of the teaching of the forsyaking: the spider believes in the strength of its web, and the web is the frailest of houses, and the house that is the frailest is the house that is in the one who forsakes the one who took it for a house.

**The Witness Against Themselves**

The verse closes with the statement of the witness, and the witness is the witness that is the exposure: *And they bear witness against themselves that they were disbelievers*. The witness against the self is the witness that is the acknowledgment of the state of the disbelief. The logic of the teaching is the logic that is worth stating: the man who is in the exposure is the man who is in the one who cannot deny the state of his own disbelief, and the witness of the self is the witness that is the final acknowledgment. The man who fabricated the lie against God and who denied the signs of God is the man who is in the witness against himself, and the witness against himself is the witness that is the end of the denial.

The verse, in its form, is the form of the scene of the account that is the exposure of the denier of the signs, and the exposure of the denier of the signs is the exposure that is the forsyaking of the idols and the witness against the self, and the two forms of the exposure are the two forms that are the introduction of the entering of the Fire in the next verse.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:38

> **He will say, “Enter the Fire among communities of jinn and men that have passed away before you!” Every time a community enters, it curses its sister, till, when they have all successively arrived there, the last of them will say of the first of them, “Our Lord, it was they who led us astray; so give them a double punishment in the Fire.” He will say, “For each of you it shall be doubled, but you know not.”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Command of the Entering**

The verse is the command of God that follows the scene of the account in the previous verse, and the command is the command that is the entering of the Fire: *Enter the Fire among communities of jinn and men that have passed away before you*. The command is addressed to the disbelievers of the present, and the command is the command that places them among the communities of the jinn and the men who passed away before them. The statement of the jinn and the men together is the statement that is the form of the teaching: the Fire is the common destination of the jinn and the men who erred, and the error of the jinn is the error that is the state of Iblīs, and the error of the men is the error that is the following of the jinn. The two errors are the two errors that are the common destination.

**The Cursing of the Communities**

The verse states the scene of the entering, and the scene is the scene that is the cursing: *Every time a community enters, it curses its sister*. The word for "curses" is *tukāfiru*, and the sense is the sense of the blaming, the accusing, the throwing of the blame upon the other. The scene is the scene that is in the form of the state of the communities of the Fire, and the state of the communities of the Fire is the state that is in the form of the mutual blaming: each community blames the community that came before it, and the blaming is the blaming that is the saying that the error of the present is the error that was caused by the example of the past.

The logic of the scene is the logic that is worth stating: the blaming of the community is the blaming that is the removal of the responsibility to the other. The community that is in the blaming is the community that is in the one who does not want to meet his own account, and the meeting of his own account is the meeting that is the tasting of the punishment of his own deeds.

**The Request of the Last and the Answer of God**

The verse states the act of the last community, and the act is the act that is the request: *the last of them will say of the first of them, "Our Lord, it was they who led us astray; so give them a double punishment in the Fire."* The request is the request that is the asking of the doubling of the punishment of the first community. The answer of God is the answer that is the correction of the request: *For each of you it shall be doubled, but you know not.*

The answer is the answer that is in the form of the teaching that is worth stating in full. The request of the last community is the request that is the asking of the increase of the punishment of the other, and the answer of God is the answer that is the doubling of the punishment of every one, including the last community that asked. The logic of the answer is the logic that is the form of the justice: the blaming of the other does not reduce the share of the one who blames, and the share of the one who blames is the share that is the doubling. The addition of the rebuke — *but you know not* — is the addition that is the ignorance of the measure of his own share.

The Qur'an states the same scene in the form of the statement of the leaders of the communities, and the statement is the statement that is in the form of the confirmation of the teaching:

> *"And they will say: O our Lord, it was these leaders of ours who misled us with errors; give them a double punishment in the Fire. God will say: Have a double share of the punishment, and do not seek a helper against Me."* (37:51–52, in the form of the statement of the scene)

The verse is the form of the confirmation: the request of the doubling of the punishment of the leaders is the request that is the asking of the increase of the punishment of the other, and the answer of God is the answer that is the doubling of the punishment of the one who asked.

**The Futility of the Blame**

The verse, in its form, is the form of the teaching of the futility of the blame in the hour of the account. The blame is the blame that is the throwing of the responsibility to the other, and the hour of the account is the hour that is the meeting of his own responsibility. The man who is in the hour of the account is the man who is in the tasting of the punishment of his own deeds, and the tasting of the punishment of his own deeds is the tasting that is the end of the blame. The verse, in its two scenes and its one answer, is the form of the teaching of the individual responsibility in the state of the collective blame, and the individual responsibility is the responsibility that is the tasting of the punishment of his own deeds.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:39

> **And the first of them will say to the last of them, “You are no better than us; so taste the punishment for that which you have earned.”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Return of the Blame**
