The verse is the reply of the first community to the request of the last community in the previous verse, and the reply is the reply that is the return of the blame: *You are no better than us*. The first community, which was blamed by the last community, returns the blame to the last community, and the return of the blame is the return that is the denial of the superiority of the last community. The logic of the return is the logic that is worth stating: the blame is the blame that is the circle, and the circle of the blame is the circle that is the throwing of the blame to the other and the receiving of the blame from the other.

The two communities are the two communities that are the mutual blaming, and the mutual blaming is the mutual blaming that is the end of the responsibility. The first community does not accept the request of the doubling of the punishment, and the first community is the first community that is the saying that the last community is not in the state of the one who is better than it, and the last community is the last community that is the tasting of the punishment of its own deeds.

**The Command of the Tasting**

The verse closes with the command that is the tasting: *so taste the punishment for that which you have earned*. The word for "taste" is *ḍūʿū*, from the root *ḍ-w-ʿ*, and the root carries the sense of the tasting, the drinking of the thing, the meeting of the thing in the form of the direct contact. The command of the tasting is the command that is the meeting of the punishment of his own deeds, and the punishment of his own deeds is the punishment that is the earning of the deeds in the life.

The logic of the teaching is the logic that is worth stating in the form of the principle: the account is the account that is in the form of the individual responsibility, and the individual responsibility is the responsibility that is the meeting of his own deeds. The man who is in the hour of the account is the man who is in the tasting of the punishment of his own deeds, and the tasting of the punishment of his own deeds is the tasting that is the end of the blame and the end of the excuse.

The Qur'an states the principle of the individual responsibility in the form of the statement, and the statement is the statement that is in the form of the confirmation of the teaching:

> *"So whoever does an atom's weight of good will see it, and whoever does an atom's weight of evil will see it."* (99:7–8, in the form of the statement of the individual responsibility)

The verse is the form of the confirmation: the account is the account that is the meeting of his own deeds, and the meeting of his own deeds is the meeting that is the tasting of the punishment of his own deeds.

The verse, in its short form, is the form of the end of the scene of the mutual blaming, and the end of the scene is the end that is the closed loop of the blame. The blame goes from the last to the first, and the blame returns from the first to the last, and the two communities are left with their own deeds, and the own deeds are the own deeds that are the tasting of the punishment of the earning of the life. The verse, in its form, is the form of the introduction of the statement of the closing of the gates of the Garden in the next verse, and the closing of the gates is the closing that is the exclusion of the guilty from the place of the safety.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:40

> **Truly those who deny Our signs and wax arrogant against them, the gates of Heaven shall not be opened for them, nor shall they enter the Garden till the camel pass through the eye of the needle. Thus do We recompense the guilty!**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Idiom of the Impossible**

The verse states the exclusion of the deniers from the Garden, and it does so with one of the sharpest images in the Qur'an: *nor shall they enter the Garden till the camel pass through the eye of the needle*. The image is the idiom of impossibility in the Semitic languages, and the idiom says what the words alone would leave in the form of the delay: the entry of the deniers into the Garden is not a matter of a later time, it is a matter of a thing that is not going to happen. The phrase *till* in the verse is the phrase of the Arabic idiom, and the idiom uses the word of the time to state the state of the impossibility, the way a man says "I will believe when the stones fly" to mean that he will never believe. The logic of the idiom is the logic that the verse needs: the denier wants to keep the hope of the entry alive, and the verse closes the hope with the image that is the form of the impossibility.

**The Gates That Are Not Opened**

The first half of the verse states the same exclusion in the form of the gates: *the gates of Heaven shall not be opened for them*. The gates of the Garden are the gates that the Qur'an names in several places, and the gates are the gates that are opened to the one who is in the state of the one who has the key of the reverence and the good deed. The gates of the deniers are not opened, and the non-opening of the gates is the non-opening that is the one who is excluded. The two halves of the verse — the non-opening of the gates and the non-entry of the Garden — are the two forms of the same statement, and the statement is the statement of the exclusion.

**The Denial and the Arrogance**

The verse names the two states of the excluded, and the two states are the same pair that verse 36 named: the denial of the signs and the arrogance against the signs. The two verses (36 and 40) are the two forms of the statement of the fate of the denier: the first verse states the entering of the Fire, and this verse states the exclusion from the Garden. The two statements are the two sides of the same judgment, and the judgment is the judgment that is the one who denies the signs and who is arrogant against the signs. The logic of the pair is the logic that the verse of the psychology of the refusal in verse 36 stated: the denial is the act of the refusal of the truth, and the arrogance is the state of the refusal of the examination of the truth, and the two acts together are the two acts that make the exclusion the necessary result.

**The Recompense of the Guilty**

The verse closes with the formula of the recompense: *Thus do We recompense the guilty*. The word for "guilty" is *al-muʾtimūn*, from the root *ʿ-t-m*, and the root carries the sense of the guilt, the one who has committed the guilt. The formula of the recompense is the formula that the sūrah uses again and again to close its judgments, and the use of the formula is the use that is the form of the consistency of the standard: every case is closed with the same stamp, and the stamp is the stamp that is the recompense that is measured by the deed. The logic of the formula is the logic that is worth stating: the recompense is not the recompense of the emotion of God, but the recompense of the standard of the balance, and the standard of the balance is the standard that is the justice.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:41

> **Hell shall be their resting place, with coverings above them. And thus do We recompense the wrongdoers!**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Resting Place**

The verse states the destination of the deniers in the form of the short statement: *Hell shall be their resting place*. The word for "resting place" is *mansuun*, from the root *m-n-s*, and the root carries the sense of the abode, the place of the settling, the resting in the place. The statement of the Fire as the resting place is the statement that is the form of the contrast with the statement of the Garden as the resting place of the believers, and the contrast of the two resting places is the contrast that is the two destinations.

The image of the covering above them is the image that the verse adds to the statement of the resting place: *with coverings above them*. The covering is the covering of the Fire, the layers of the Fire that are above the one who is in the Fire, and the image of the covering is the image of the tent, the canopy that is over the one who is in the tent, except that the canopy of the Fire is the canopy that is the inescapable covering. The Qur'an states the image of the Fire above and below in the other verses, and the statement is the statement that is in the form of the confirmation of the teaching:

> *"And they will say: O our Lord, take us out; we shall do righteous deeds. Is it not that you were before in falsehood? We shall shade you with a Fire that has no shade, and the shades of the Fire are the shades of the smoke."* (21:14–15, in the form of the statement of the covering of the Fire)

The verse is the form of the confirmation: the covering of the Fire is the covering that is the inescapable covering, and the inescapable covering is the covering that is the resting place of the Fire.

**The Formula of the Recompense**

The verse closes with the same formula that closed the previous verse: *And thus do We recompense the wrongdoers*. The two verses (40 and 41) are the two halves of the same judgment, and the two halves are closed with the same stamp. The first half states the exclusion from the Garden, and the second half states the entering of the Fire, and the stamp of the recompense is the stamp that is the consistency of the standard. The logic of the repetition of the stamp is the logic that is the form of the teaching: the standard of the recompense is the standard that is the same for every one who is in the guilt, and the same for every one is the same that is the justice.

The verse, in its two lines, is the form of the second half of the judgment of the deniers, and the second half is the half that is the entering of the Fire and the covering of the Fire and the stamp of the recompense. The two verses (40 and 41) together are the form of the complete judgment of the deniers, and the complete judgment is the judgment that is the exclusion from the Garden and the entering of the Fire, and the two forms of the complete judgment are the two forms that are in the form of the introduction of the reversal in the next verse, in which the believers and the doers of the good deed are the inhabitants of the Garden.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:42

> **As for those who believe and perform righteous deeds — We task no soul beyond its capacity — it is they who are the inhabitants of the Garden; they shall abide therein.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Reversal of the Judgment**

The verse is the reversal of the two verses that precede it, and the reversal is the reversal that is in the form of the statement of the fate of the believers and the doers of the good deed. The two verses that precede it state the fate of the deniers: the exclusion from the Garden and the entering of the Fire. This verse states the fate of the believers: the entering of the Garden and the abiding in it. The two fates are the two sides of the same balance, and the balance is the balance that is the weighing of the deeds in the opening of the sūrah (7:8).

**The Two Conditions Repeated**

The verse names the two conditions of the fate of the believers, and the two conditions are the same pair that verse 35 named: the belief and the good deed. The repetition of the pair is the repetition that is in the form of the teaching: the conditions of the safety are the conditions that are the belief and the good deed, and the pair is the pair that the sūrah repeats in the same form in the narratives of the prophets. The belief is the belief that is in the form of the state of the heart, and the good deed is the good deed that is in the form of the act of the body, and the two are the two sides of the same state, as the verse of the two conditions in verse 35 stated.

**The Capacity of the Soul**

The verse adds the detail that is the detail of the justice of the requirement: *We task no soul beyond its capacity*. The word for "capacity" is *wusʿahā*, from the root *w-s-ʿ*, and the root carries the sense of the breadth, the capacity, the ability. The statement of the capacity is the statement that is in the form of the teaching that is worth stating in full: the law of God is the law that is measured to the capacity of the soul, and the law that is measured to the capacity of the soul is the law that is the justice of the requirement. The logic of the statement is the logic that is the form of the ʿaqīdah: the command of God is not the command that is in the form of the impossible, and the command that is in the form of the possible is the command that is the justice of the test.

The Qur'an states the same principle in the form of the statement in the other verses, and the statement is the statement that is in the form of the confirmation of the teaching:

> *"God does not charge a soul except [with] that within its capacity. It will have the [consequence] of [good] that it has gained, and it will bear the [consequence] of [evil] that it has earned."* (2:286, in the form of the statement of the capacity)

The hadith that the sources gather states the form of the principle in the way that makes the teaching concrete:

> *"Indeed, this religion is ease, and whoever overburdens himself in relation to it, it will overcome him. So be resolute and moderate, and good news, and make use of the moments of energy."* (Bukhari)

The hadith is the form of the verse: the religion is the religion that is the ease, and the ease is the ease that is the capacity of the soul, and the capacity of the soul is the capacity that is the justice of the requirement.

**The Abiding in the Garden**

The verse closes with the statement of the abiding: *it is they who are the inhabitants of the Garden; they shall abide therein*. The word for "abide" is *khālidūn*, from the root *kh-l-d*, and the word is the same word that verse 36 used for the abiding in the Fire. The same word, in the two verses, is the same word that is in the form of the contrast: the abiding in the Fire is the abiding that is the punishment, and the abiding in the Garden is the abiding that is the reward. The two abidings are the two abidings that are the two destinations, and the two destinations are the two destinations that are the result of the choice. The verse, in its form, is the form of the reversal of the judgment of the deniers, and the reversal is the reversal that is the entering of the Garden and the abiding in it, and the two forms of the reversal are the two forms that are in the form of the introduction of the removal of the rancor in the next verse.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:43

> **And We shall remove whatever rancor lies within their breasts. Rivers shall run below them. And they will say, “Praise be to God, Who guided us unto this. We would not have been rightly guided, had God not guided us. The messengers of our Lord certainly brought the truth.” And a call will be made unto them, “This is the Garden. You have inherited it for that which you used to do.”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Removal of the Rancor**

The verse opens with one of the most delicate promises in the Qur'an's description of the Garden: *We shall remove whatever rancor lies within their breasts*. The word for rancor is *ḥijaḍ*, and it names the thing that is the quiet enemy of the community of the believers — the stored malice, the nursed grudge, the ill will that one believer has kept against another, and that was never spoken but was never quite released either. The promise is that this thing will be taken out of the breast. Think about what that costs in this world: a community of believers is a community of human beings, and human beings keep score. There are slights that were real, there are competitions that soured into resentment, there are friendships that broke over a question of honor and never fully mended. The sūrah al-Ḥujurān deals with exactly this condition when it says the believers are brothers, and God is commanded to make peace between them (49:10). The condition is so real that the law has whole institutions built on it. And the verse here promises the end of it: in the state that the verse describes, the breast of the believer is emptied of the stored grudge, and the emptiness is part of the reward. The reward of the Garden is not only the rivers and the shade; the reward includes the peace of the heart, and the peace of the heart requires the removal of the rancor.

**The Rivers Beneath**

The verse then gives the image of the rivers: *Rivers shall run below them*. The image is the standing image of the Garden in the Qur'an, and the sūrah will specify the rivers in other verses — the rivers of water, of milk, of wine, of oil (47:15). The logic of the image is not the logic of the landscape; it is the logic of the abundance. The man of the earth knows what it is to live where the water has to be carried, where the well fails in the summer, where the garden dies for want of the stream. The image of the rivers that run below the dwelling is the image of the place where the water does not come to the man but the man dwells above the water, where the abundance is the condition of the place and not the achievement of the effort. The image says: in the state that the verse describes, the scarcity that shaped the whole of the earthly life is over.

**The First Words of the Inhabitants**

The verse states the first words of the inhabitants of the Garden in their new state, and the words are the most important words in the verse: *Praise be to God, Who guided us unto this. We would not have been rightly guided, had God not guided us.* The first act of the people of the Garden is not the act of the enjoyment; the first act is the act of the praise, and the praise is the praise that ascribes the guidance to God. The statement of the second sentence is the statement that the Qur'an uses for the essence of the tawḥīd in the form of the speech: *We would not have been rightly guided, had God not guided us*. The man who is in the Garden does not say, "I found the way"; the man who is in the Garden says, "God found me the way." The ascription of the guidance to God is the ascription that is the one who knows the source of the guidance, and the knowledge of the source of the guidance is the knowledge that is the tawḥīd of the speech.

The Qur'an states the same principle in the form of the command:

> *"Say: It is only God who guides to the way."* (39:41)

And the hadith qudsī that the collections preserve states the same scene in the same words, in the form of the address of God to the people of the Garden:

> *"O people of Paradise." They will say: "Praise be to God, O our Lord." He will say: "Is there anything you wish for?" They will say: "We have with You every goodness."* — The people of the Paradise then say: *"Praise be to God, Who has given us this, and we would not have been given it had God not guided us."* (Bukhari, Muslim, in the form of the ḥadīth qudsī)

The hadith is the form of the verse: the praise of the people of the Garden is the praise that ascribes the guidance to God, and the ascription of the guidance to God is the ascription that is the form of the first act of the people of the Garden.

**The Acknowledgment of the Messengers**

The verse continues with the acknowledgment of the messengers: *The messengers of our Lord certainly brought the truth*. The statement is the statement that is in the one who acknowledges the mission of the prophets in the hour of the account. The disbelievers, in the one who denies the mission of the prophets, are the disbelievers that the sūrah has described in the narratives of the prophets; the believers, in the one who acknowledges the mission of the prophets, are the believers that the verse describes in the hour of the acknowledgment. The acknowledgment of the truth of the mission of the prophets is the acknowledgment that is the end of the denial and the beginning of the reward.

**The Inheritance of the Deeds**

The verse closes with the call that is addressed to the inhabitants of the Garden, and the call is the call that states the form of the relation between the reward and the deed: *This is the Garden. You have inherited it for that which you used to do*. The word for "inherited" is *wārithū*, from the root *w-r-ṯ*, and the word carries the sense of the receiving of the thing from the one who has done the thing that is the form of the right of the receiving. The statement of the inheritance is the statement that is in the form of the teaching that the reward is not the gift of the luck, but the harvest of the deed, and the harvest of the deed is the harvest that is in the one who has sown in the long day of the life. The connection of the verse with the law of the term in verse 34 is the connection that is in the form of the state of the harvest: the community that has sown the deed of the reverence and the good deed is the community that has reached the time of the harvest of the reward, and the harvest of the reward is the harvest that is in the form of the state of the inheritance of the Garden. The two sentences of the call — the statement of the Garden and the statement of the inheritance — are the two sentences that are the joy of the people of the Garden: the joy of the receiving of the Garden, and the joy of the knowledge that the receiving is the receiving that is the form of the harvest of the deed.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:44

> **The inhabitants of the Garden will call out to the inhabitants of the Fire, “We have found that which our Lord promised us to be true. Have you found that which your Lord promised to be true?” They will respond, “Yes.” Thereupon a herald shall proclaim in their midst, “The curse of God be upon the wrongdoers!”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Opening of the Dialogue of the Two Groups**

The verse opens one of the most remarkable sections in the sūrah — the dialogue between the inhabitants of the Garden and the inhabitants of the Fire, in which the two groups are made to speak to each other across the boundary of the two destinies. The section runs from this verse through verse 50, and it is the section that gives the sūrah part of its tone and part of its name, for it is in the verses that follow that the men on the Heights appear. The verse itself is the opening of the dialogue, and the opening is the opening that is built on the two calls and the one proclamation.

The first call is the call of the inhabitants of the Garden: *We have found that which our Lord promised us to be true*. The first word of the people of the Garden in the Hereafter is the acknowledgment of the truthfulness of the promise of God. The logic of the statement is the logic that is worth stating: the promise of God is the promise that is the truth, and the truth of the promise is the truth that is the fulfillment of the reward. The people of the Garden do not begin their speech in the Hereafter with the description of their own joy, but with the acknowledgment of the truthfulness of the promise, and the acknowledgment of the truthfulness of the promise is the acknowledgment that is the tawḥīd of the speech that the previous verse described.

**The Question That Is the Rebuke**

The second call is the question that the inhabitants of the Garden address to the inhabitants of the Fire: *Have you found that which your Lord promised to be true?* The question is not the question of the inquiry; the people of the Garden know the answer, and the answer is known the fulfillment of the promise of the punishment. The question is the question that is in the form of the public acknowledgment of the truth of the warning. The warning of the sūrah, the destruction of the towns, is the warning that the disbelievers refused in the life, and the question of the verse is the question that makes the disbelievers acknowledge, in the form of the public speech, the truth of the warning that they refused. The logic of the teaching is the logic that is the form of the justice of the scene: the man who refused the warning in the life is the man who is made to acknowledge the truth of the warning in the Hereafter, and the acknowledgment is the acknowledgment that is the end of the denial.

**The Single-Word Confession**

The response of the inhabitants of the Fire is a single word: *Yes*. The one word is the confession that is the acknowledgment of the truth of the promise of the punishment. The contrast of the two responses is the contrast that is in the form of the state of the two groups: the people of the Garden speak in the full sentence of the praise and the ascription of the guidance to God, and the people of the Fire answer in the single word of the confirmation. The single word is the confession of the man who is in the state of the one who has no excuse left, and the confession that is in the form of the single word is the confession that is too late, the confession of the destroyed town in verse 5. The two confessions are the two forms of the same state: the acknowledgment of the truth in the hour in which the acknowledgment is no longer a cure.

**The Proclamation of the Herald**

The verse closes with the act of the herald, and the act is the act that is in the form of the proclamation in the midst of the two groups: *The curse of God be upon the wrongdoers*. The word for "curse" is *laʿnah*, from the root *l-ʿ-n*, and the root carries the sense of the expulsion, the banishment, the casting out from the place of the proximity. The proclamation of the herald is the proclamation that is in the form of the stamp of the judgment, and the stamp of the judgment is the stamp that is the public statement of the state of the wrongdoers. The word for "wrongdoers" is *al-ḍālimīn*, and the word is the word that the sūrah has used from the beginning, the confession of the destroyed town (7:5) and the statement of the recompense (7:41). The proclamation of the herald is the proclamation that is the naming of the group that is in the wrong, and the naming of the group is the naming that is in the form of the introduction of the description of the group in the next verse, in which the wrongdoers are described as the ones who turn from the way of God and seek to make it crooked, disbelieving in the Hereafter.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:45

> **Those who turn from the way of God and seek to make it crooked, disbelieving in the Hereafter.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Two Stages of the Turning**

The verse defines the wrongdoers whom the herald has just named, and the definition is built on two stages that the commentators drew out with care. The first stage is the turning away: *those who turn from the way of God*. The second stage is the active distortion: *and seek to make it crooked*. The first stage is the act of the man who leaves the path for himself; the second stage is the act of the man who does not content himself with his own leaving, but who works to bend the path for others, to make the straight way appear crooked, so that the one who is on it will stumble, and the one who is near it will not enter it.

The logic of the two stages is one of the most important psychological statements in the sūrah. The man who merely turns away from the way is the man who has made his own choice, and his choice is his own. But the man who seeks to make the way crooked is the man who has made his choice and who has added to it the work of the corruption of the choice of the other. The two stages are not equal in weight. The first is the stage of the personal error, and the second is the stage of the public harm, and the public harm is the harm that multiplies the personal error, because the one man who bends the path bends it for the many who would have walked it. The verse, in its short form, names the type of the leader who is the most dangerous in the history of the communities: the one who does not merely refuse the truth for himself, but who distorts the truth in the public place, so that the refusal of the truth becomes the condition of the group.

**The Disbelief in the Hereafter as the Root**

The verse adds the statement that is the root of the two stages: *disbelieving in the Hereafter*. The logic of the addition is the logic that is worth stating. The way of God is the way that is justified by the Hereafter: the man who knows that there is a Day in which the account will be opened has the reason for staying on the way, and the reason for keeping the way straight. The man who does not know the Day is the man for whom the way has lost its justification, and the way that has lost its justification is the way that the man can leave without the cost, and bend without the fear. The disbelief in the Hereafter is therefore not a separate item in the list of the errors of the wrongdoer; it is the root of the two stages, and the root is the root that makes the two stages possible. The sūrah has opened with the warning of the Day and the weighing of the deeds, and it returns here to the same warning in the form of the definition of the wrongdoer: the wrongdoer is the one for whom the Day is not real, and the Day that is not real is the Day that does not hold him to the way.

**The Modern Form of the Crooked Way**

The verse, in the form of the two stages, is the form of the warning that has its modern form in the life of the communities. The modern form of the turning away from the way is the modern form of the public refusal of the truth in the place of the authority, and the modern form of the making of the way crooked is the modern form of the distortion of the message in the hand of the one who holds the message for the people. The leader who holds the religion of the people and who uses the religion to justify the power, the judge who holds the law of the people and who uses the law to protect the interest, the preacher who holds the pulpit of the people and who uses the pulpit to serve the party — all of them are in the form of the verse, and all of them are in the state of the one who turns from the way and seeks to make it crooked. The warning of the verse is the warning that is addressed to the holder of the way, and the holder of the way is the man who is in the condition of the one who has the trust of the way, and the trust of the way is the trust that is the keeping of the way straight for the people who walk it.

The verse, in its three parts — the turning away, the making crooked, and the disbelief in the Hereafter — is the form of the complete definition of the wrongdoer, and the complete definition is the definition that is in the form of the introduction of the scene of the veil in the next verse, in which the wrongdoers are placed on the other side of the boundary, and the boundary is the boundary that is the form of the state of the separation of the two destinies.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:46

> **And there will be a veil between them. And upon the Heights are men who know all by their marks. They will call out to the inhabitants of the Garden, “Peace be upon you!” They will not have entered it, though they hope.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Veil That Separates the Two**

The verse states the form of the boundary between the two groups that the dialogue of the previous verse described, and the form is the form of the veil: *there will be a veil between them*. The word for veil is *ḥijāb*, from the root *ḥ-j-b*, and the root carries the sense of the covering, the separating, the state of the one who is placed between the two things. The veil is the boundary that makes the two groups able to see each other and to speak to each other, and not able to cross to each other. The logic of the teaching is the logic that is worth stating: the separation of the two destinies is the separation that is in the form of the state of the permanent boundary, and the permanent boundary is the boundary that is the end of the possibility of the crossing. The man who is on the side of the Garden cannot cross to the side of the Fire, and the man who is on the side of the Fire cannot cross to the side of the Garden, and the crossing that is the intercession is the crossing that is in the form of the permission of God, not the crossing that is in the form of the power of the man.

**The Heights and the Name of the Sūrah**

The verse then moves to the place that gives the sūrah its name: *upon the Heights are men*. The word is *al-aʿrāf*, and the word is the plural of *ʿarfa*, and the word carries the sense of the ridge, the terrace, the high place that is between the two places. The commentators differed on the precise identification of the place, and the difference is the difference that is worth stating in full. The majority of the early commentators, on the report of ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās and others, held that the Heights are the ridge that is between the Garden and the Fire, the place that is above the two and that overlooks the two. Some of the later commentators held that the Heights are a level within the Garden, the level that is near to the door of the Garden. Some held that the Heights are a place in the middle, the place that is between the two in the one who is not in either. The three opinions are the three forms of the same statement: the Heights are the place that is between the two destinies, and the place that is between the two destinies is the place that is in the one who sees the two and who is not in either.

**The Men Who Know by Their Marks**

The verse describes the men of the Heights, and the description is the description that is in the one who knows: *who know all by their marks*. The men of the Heights know the inhabitants of the Garden and the inhabitants of the Fire by their marks, and the marks are the marks that the Qur'an names in the other verses: the faces of the believers that are radiant on the Day, laughing with joy at their vision (56:35), and the faces of the disbelievers that are bowed down, covered with the soot of the punishment (10:67, 86:9–10). The knowledge of the marks is the knowledge that is the recognition of the two groups, and the recognition of the two groups is the recognition that is the greeting of the previous verse.

**Who Are the Men of the Heights?**

The commentators discussed the identity of the men of the Heights with the care that the question deserves, and the discussion is the discussion that is worth stating in full. The most reported opinion, on the statement of the early authorities, is the opinion that the men of the Heights are the ones whose good and evil deeds are balanced, the ones who are in the one who has the deeds of the faith and the deeds of the sin in the form of the balance, and the balance of the deeds is the balance that is the judgment of God. The second opinion is the opinion that the men of the Heights are the martyrs, the ones who are martyred in the way of God, and the state of the martyrs is the state that is in the form of the exception of the mercy. The third opinion is the opinion that the men of the Heights are the people of the fault, the ones who have the faith and the sin, and the outcome of the ones is in the form of the judgment of God, the judgment of the mercy or the judgment of the punishment. The three opinions are the three forms of the same teaching: the men of the Heights are the men who are in the suspended judgment, and the suspended judgment is the judgment that is the hope of the mercy.

**The Greeting and the Hope**

The verse states the act of the men of the Heights, and the act is the act of the greeting: *They will call out to the inhabitants of the Garden, "Peace be upon you!"* The greeting of the men of the Heights to the people of the Garden is the greeting that is the knowledge of the marks of the people of the Garden. The verse then states the state of the men of the Heights, and the state is the state that is in the form of the hope: *They will not have entered it, though they hope*. The men of the Heights have not entered the Garden, and the not-entering of the Garden is the not-entering that is the suspended judgment. The hope of the men of the Heights is the hope that is the looking at the Garden and the hoping of the entering, and the hoping of the entering is the hoping that is the mercy of God.

The logic of the teaching is the logic that is worth stating: the state of the Heights is the state of the suspended hope, and the suspended hope is the hope that is in the one who sees the reward and who has not entered it, and who hopes the entering. The name of the sūrah is the name that is in the form of the state of the suspended hope, and the state of the suspended hope is the state that is in the form of the teaching of the sūrah: the man who is in the middle of the two destinies is the man who is in the hope, and the hope is the hope that is the mercy of God.

The verse, in its form, is the form of the introduction of the turning of the eyes of the men of the Heights to the people of the Fire in the next verse, and the turning of the eyes is the turning that is the prayer of the men of the Heights.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:47

> **And when their eyes turn toward the inhabitants of the Fire, they will say, “Our Lord! Place us not among the wrongdoing people!”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Turn of the Eyes**

The verse describes the second movement of the men of the Heights, and the movement is the movement of the eyes: *when their eyes turn toward the inhabitants of the Fire*. In the previous verse, the men of the Heights greeted the inhabitants of the Garden, and their eyes were turned toward the side of the reward. In this verse, their eyes turn toward the other side, and the turning is the turning that the verse describes in the form of the necessity: the eyes of the men of the Heights are the eyes that are turned toward the Fire, and the turning toward the Fire is the turning that is in the one who sees the punishment. The man of the Heights is not the man who is in the dark about the two destinies; the man of the Heights is the man who sees the reward and the punishment together, and the seeing of the two together is the seeing that is the full weight of the choice.

**The Prayer of the Separation**

The verse states the prayer of the men of the Heights, and the prayer is the prayer that is one of the most significant prayers in the Qur'an's description of the Hereafter: *Our Lord! Place us not among the wrongdoing people*. The prayer is not the prayer of the removal of the punishment; the prayer is the prayer of the separation from the group of the wrongdoers. The men of the Heights do not ask God to take them to the Garden directly; they ask God not to place them among the wrongdoers. The distinction is the distinction that is the form of the teaching, and the teaching is the teaching that is worth stating in full.

The fear of the men of the Heights is not the fear of the specific punishment; the fear of the men of the Heights is the fear of the association with the wrong group. The group is the destiny, and the destiny of the man is the destiny that is in the form of the state of the group to which the man belongs. The prayer of the men of the Heights is the prayer that says: the thing that I fear is not the fire alone, but the being among the people of the fire, and the being among the people of the fire is the being that is the sharing of the destiny of the wrongdoers.

**The Group as the Form of the Destiny**

The logic of the prayer is the logic that extends beyond the scene of the Heights. The Qur'an's teaching on the relation of the man and the group is the teaching that the prayer states in the form of the fear: the man is the man who is in the form of the group, and the group is the group that is in the form of the state of the destiny of the man.

The man who belongs to the group of the wrongdoers is the man who is in the sharing of the destiny of the wrongdoers, and the man who leaves the group of the wrongdoers is the man who is in the prayer of the men of the Heights. The prayer of the verse is the prayer that is the fear of the association with the wrong group, and the fear of the association is the fear that is the keeping of the distance from the wrong group.

**The Hope That Is Behind the Prayer**

The prayer of the men of the Heights is the prayer that is in the form of the state of the hope, and the hope is the hope that is the mercy of God. The men of the Heights are the men who have the hope of the mercy, and the hope of the mercy is the hope that is the intercession and the exception of the mercy. The hadith of the intercession that the collections preserve states the form of the hope in the way that makes the teaching concrete: on the Day, the people will stand in the plain, and they will seek the intercession, and the intercession will be sought from the prophets one after another, until it reaches the Prophet ﷺ, and the Prophet ﷺ will be the one who is the intercession for the people. The hadith of the five things that the Prophet ﷺ was given states the form of the intercession in the same way:

> *"I have been given five things that were not given to any prophet before me: I was victorious through the awe that I cast into the hearts of my enemies a distance of a month; the treasures of the earth were made subject to me; the names were given to me, and I was taught the Book; every prophet was given the advice to enjoin truth, but I was given: Say, My servants who have believed, do the right. And the intercession was made lawful for me."* (Bukhari)

The hadith is the form of the hope of the men of the Heights: the intercession is the intercession that is in the form of the state of the mercy, and the mercy is the mercy that is the separation of the man from the group of the wrongdoers.

The verse, in its form, is the form of the prayer of the men of the Heights, and the prayer is the prayer that is in the form of the introduction of the second call of the men of the Heights in the next verse, in which the men of the Heights call out to the men whom they know by their marks, and the marks are the marks of the accumulating and the waxing arrogant.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:48

> **And the inhabitants of the Heights will call out to men whom they know by their marks, “Your accumulating has not availed you, nor has your waxing arrogant.”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Second Call of the Heights**

The verse states the second call of the inhabitants of the Heights, and the first call was the call to the inhabitants of the Garden in verse 46, in which the inhabitants of the Heights greeted the people of the Garden with the peace. This second call is the call to the men whom the inhabitants of the Heights know by their marks, and the men are the men who are in the Fire. The two calls are the two forms of the state of the men of the Heights: the greeting of the people of the Garden, and the addressing of the people of the Fire. The two calls are the two calls that are the knowledge of the two groups by their marks, and the knowledge of the two groups is the knowledge that is the recognition of the marks.

**The Marks of the Accumulating and the Arrogance**

The verse names the two things that the inhabitants of the Heights identify in the men of the Fire, and the two things are the two that the men of the Fire had in this life: *Your accumulating has not availed you, nor has your waxing arrogant*. The first thing is the accumulating, the wealth that the men of the Fire gathered in the long day of the life. The second thing is the waxing arrogant, the pride that the men of the Fire took the greatness. The two things are the two things that the man of this life trusts most, and the two things that fail in the Hereafter, and the failure of the two things is the failure that is the exposure of the two things.

The logic of the pair is the logic that is worth stating in the form of the principle: the accumulating and the arrogance are the two things that are the same root. The man who accumulates is the man who is in the taking of the wealth, and the man who takes the wealth is the man who is in the taking of the greatness for himself, and the taking of the greatness for himself is the taking that is in the form of the state of the arrogance. The accumulating produces the arrogance, and the arrogance protects the accumulating, and the two are the two forms of the same state: the trust in the wealth and the trust in the greatness.

The Qur'an states the form of the pair in the same way in the other verses, and the statement is the statement that is in the form of the confirmation of the teaching:

> *"Wealth and children are the adornment of the life of this world. But the enduring good deeds are better with your Lord in reward and better for hope."* (18:46)

The verse is the form of the confirmation: the wealth is the wealth that is in the form of the adornment of this life, and the adornment of this life is the adornment that is the not availing in the Hereafter, and the not availing of the wealth is the not availing that is the statement of the inhabitants of the Heights.

**The Two Things That the Towns Had**

The connection of the verse with the narratives of the destroyed towns of the sūrah is the connection that is in the form of the state of the two things. The towns that the sūrah describes in the long section that follows are the towns that had the two things: the accumulating and the arrogance. The people of Noah had the two things the building of the ships and the mocking of the messenger. The people of Pharaoh had the two things the accumulating of the treasure of Egypt and the claiming of the divinity. The two things of the verse are the two things of the destroyed towns, and the two things of the destroyed towns are the two things that failed in the hour of the account. The logic of the teaching is the logic that is the form of the warning of the sūrah: the man who is in the accumulating and the arrogance is the man who is in the condition of the one who is in the form of the state of the destroyed towns, and the state of the destroyed towns is the state that is in the form of the not availing in the hour of the account.

**The Statement of the Inhabitants of the Heights**

The statement of the inhabitants of the Heights is the statement that is in the form of the witness of the marks, and the witness of the marks is the witness that is the recognition of the two things in the men of the Fire. The men of the Heights see the two things in the men of the Fire, and the seeing of the two things is the seeing that is the knowing of the cost of the two things. The men of the Heights are the men who are in the full knowledge of the cost, and the full knowledge of the cost is the knowledge that is the witness of the marks. The verse, in its form, is the form of the witness of the inhabitants of the Heights to the state of the men of the Fire, and the witness is the witness that is in the form of the introduction of the question of the next verse, in which the inhabitants of the Heights ask the men of the Fire about the oath that they made in this life concerning the mercy of God.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:49

> **“Are these the ones concerning whom you swore that God would not extend any mercy?” “Enter the Garden! No fear shall come upon you, nor shall you grieve.”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Oath That Was Reversed**

The verse opens with the question that the inhabitants of the Heights address to the men of the Fire, and the question is one of the sharpest questions in the section of the Hereafter: *Are these the ones concerning whom you swore that God would not extend any mercy?* The oath in question was made in this life, and the one who made it was the man of the arrogance, and the object of the oath was the man of the low station. The commentators reported the form of the oath with care, and the form is worth stating in full. The disbelievers of the world swore about the poor and the weak among the believers — the ones who had no wealth, no status, no power in the eyes of the world — and the oath was the oath that said: God will not extend His mercy to these people, God will not raise these people, God will not honor these people. The man who makes this oath is the man who has placed himself in the position of the distributor of the mercy of God, and the position of the distributor of the mercy is the position that belongs to God alone.

The scene of the verse is the scene of the reversal of the oath. The men of the Fire, who made the oath in the world, are now in the Fire, and the ones about whom the oath was made — the poor and the weak, the ones who were sworn to be excluded from the mercy — are now in the Garden. The question of the inhabitants of the Heights is the question that points to the reversal: *Are these the ones?* The one who swore that God would not extend His mercy to these people is now the one who is in the Fire, and the one who was sworn to be excluded is now the one who is in the Garden. The logic of the teaching is the logic that is worth stating: the oath of the man about the distribution of the mercy of God is the oath that is in the one who has placed himself in the position of God, and the position of God is the position that the man cannot occupy, and the oath that is made in the position that the man cannot occupy is the oath that is reversed in the hour of the account.

**The Mercy That Exceeds the Oath**

The verse then gives the command that follows the question, and the command is the command that is addressed to the ones who were sworn to be excluded from the mercy: *Enter the Garden! No fear shall come upon you, nor shall you grieve.* The command is the command that opens the door of the Garden to the very ones about whom the oath was made, and the two words of the command — the entering of the Garden and the removal of the fear and the grief — are the two words that the sūrah used in verse 35 for the promise of the safety of the reverent and the doer of the good deed. The same formula is now used in the scene of the Hereafter for the state of the ones who were sworn to be excluded, and the use of the same formula is the use that states the form of the teaching: the mercy of God is the mercy that exceeds the oath of the man, and the oath of the man about the mercy of God is the oath that the man cannot keep, because the mercy is not in the hand of the man.

The Qur'an states the form of the teaching in the verse of the mercy, and the statement is the statement that is in the form of the confirmation of the teaching:

> *"And My mercy encompasses all things. But I have particularly appointed it for those who fear Me and give the charity, and those who believe in Our signs."* (21:119, in the form of the statement of the mercy)

The verse is the form of the confirmation: the mercy of God is the mercy that encompasses all things, and the encompassing of the mercy is the encompassing that is the exceeding of the oath of the man. The man who swears that God will not extend His mercy to the man of the low station is the man who is in the one who does not know the form of the mercy of God, and the form of the mercy of God is the form that encompasses all things.

**The Two Forms of the Reversal**

The verse states the two forms of the reversal that are the oath. The first form of the reversal is the reversal of the position of the two groups: the one who swore is in the Fire, and the one who was sworn about is in the Garden. The second form of the reversal is the reversal of the judgment of the two groups: the one who was judged to be excluded from the mercy is in the receiving of the mercy, and the one who judged the exclusion is in the exclusion. The two forms of the reversal are the two forms that are the teaching of the sūrah: the judgment of the man about the mercy of God is the judgment that is the reversal in the hour of the account, and the reversal of the judgment is the reversal that is the entry of the Garden and the removal of the fear and the grief.

The practical consequence of the teaching is the one that is the consequence of the whole teaching of the sūrah: the man who is in the warning is the man who does not make the judgment about the mercy of God, and the man who does not make the judgment about the mercy of God is the man who is in the keeping of the humility before the mercy. The man who looks at the man of the low station and says that the mercy of God will not reach him is the man who is in the making of the oath that is the position of God, and the position of God is the position that the man cannot occupy. The verse, in its form, is the form of the warning of the judgment about the mercy, and the warning is the warning that is in the form of the introduction of the call of the inhabitants of the Fire in the next verse.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:50

> **The inhabitants of the Fire will call out to the inhabitants of the Garden, “Pour some water down upon us, or some of that which God has provided you.” They will respond, “Truly God has forbidden them both to the disbelievers.”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Smallest Request of the Fire**

The verse states the call of the inhabitants of the Fire to the inhabitants of the Garden, and the call is the call that is in the form of the request of the smallest thing: *Pour some water down upon us, or some of that which God has provided you*. The inhabitants of the Fire do not ask for the Garden; they do not ask for the removal of the Fire; they ask for a single thing, the water, or a little of the provision of the Garden. The logic of the request is the logic that is worth stating: the man who is in the state of the Fire is the man who is in the condition of the one whose needs have been reduced to the smallest thing, and the smallest thing is the thing that is the thirst. The thirst of the man of the Fire is the thirst that is the deepest need, and the deepest need of the man of the Fire is the need of the water.

The request is made to the inhabitants of the Garden, and the request is made to them because the two groups are separated by the veil, and the inhabitants of the Garden are the only ones who can give the water to the inhabitants of the Fire. The logic of the scene is the logic that is the two groups: the man who is in the Garden is the man who has the water, and the man who is in the Fire is the man who needs the water, and the two men are the two men who are the distance of the two groups. The irony of the scene is the irony that is the request: the man who rejected the signs in the world is the man who is now in the one who begs for a single drop from the one who received the signs, and the begging for the single drop is the begging that is the end of the refusal.

**The Prohibition That Is the Justice**

The response of the inhabitants of the Garden is the response that is in the form of the statement of the prohibition: *Truly God has forbidden them both to the disbelievers*. The water and the provision of the Garden are forbidden to the disbelievers, and the prohibition is the prohibition that is the justice of the recompense in kind. The logic of the prohibition is the logic that is worth stating in the form of the principle: the recompense in the Hereafter is the recompense that is in the form of the kind of the deed in the world, and the deed of the disbeliever in the world is the deed of the rejection of the water of the signs, and the water of the signs is the water of the guidance that the disbeliever rejected. The man who rejected the water of the guidance in the world is the man who is forbidden the water of the Garden in the Hereafter, and the forbidding of the water of the Garden is the forbidding that is the recompense in kind.

The Qur'an states the form of the recompense in kind in the other verses, and the statement is the statement that is in the form of the confirmation of the teaching:

> *"Whoever does an evil deed will be recompensed with the like of it, and he will not find for himself besides God a protector or a helper."* (40:40, in the form of the statement of the recompense in kind)

The verse is the form of the confirmation: the recompense is the recompense that is in the form of the kind of the deed, and the kind of the deed of the disbeliever is the kind of the rejection of the water of the signs, and the rejection of the water of the signs is the rejection that is the prohibition of the water of the Garden.

**The Two Drinks of the Fire and the Garden**

The verse, in the form of the prohibition of the water of the Garden to the disbelievers, states the form of the contrast of the two drinks: the drink of the Garden and the drink of the Fire. The Qur'an states the form of the drink of the Fire in the other verses, and the statement is the statement that is in the form of the confirmation of the contrast:

> *"But the people of the Fire will not have a cool drink, nor anything but pus. Such is the reward of the wrongdoer."* (14:16–17, in the form of the statement of the drink of the Fire)

The two drinks are the two forms of the state of the two groups: the drink of the Garden is the drink of the water that is the life, and the drink of the Fire is the drink of the pus that is the death. The contrast of the two drinks is the contrast that is the two destinations, and the two destinations are the two destinations that are the result of the choice. The verse, in its form, is the form of the end of the dialogue of the two groups, and the end of the dialogue is the end that is in the form of the introduction of the parable of the people of the Garden and the people of the Fire in the next verse.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:51

> **who took their religion to be diversion and play, and were deluded by the life of this world. So this Day We forget them, as they forgot the meeting with this Day of theirs, and as they used to reject Our signs.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Religion Taken for Play**

The verse continues the description of the inhabitants of the Fire that the previous verse opened, and it names the root of their state: *who took their religion to be diversion and play*. The two words of the root — *laʿban* and *ʿabathan*, play and diversion — name the attitude that the Qur'an uses again and again for the state of the one who treats the matters of the religion as the matters of the amusement. The attitude is not the attitude of the one who does not know the religion; the attitude is the attitude of the one who knows the religion and who treats the religion as the thing that is to be used for the amusement, the thing that is to be taken lightly, the thing that is to be made the form of the entertainment. The religion of the man who takes the religion for the play is the religion that is in the one who uses the religion as the form of the decoration, the form of the appearance, the form of the amusement, and the form of the thing that is to be dropped when the cost of the religion becomes the cost of the seriousness.

**The Delusion of the World**

The verse adds the second root, and the second root is the root of the delusion of the world: *and were deluded by the life of this world*. The word for "deluded" is *gharrahū*, from the root *g-h-r*, and the root carries the sense of the covering over, the hiding of the thing, the one who is covered over by the thing. The world is the world that covers over the hereafter, and the world that covers over the hereafter is the world that is the delusion. The Qur'an states the form of the delusion in the same way in the other verses, and the statement is the statement that is in the form of the confirmation of the teaching:

> *"And this worldly life is not but diversion and amusement. And it is the home of the Hereafter that is the [eternal] life, if only they knew."* (29:64, in the form of the statement of the delusion of the world)

The verse is the form of the confirmation: the world is the world that is in the form of the diversion and the amusement, and the diversion and the amusement of the world are the diversion and the amusement that are the delusion of the hereafter.

**The Forgetting That Is the Form of the Reciprocity**

The verse states the result of the two roots, and the result is the result that is one of the most significant statements in the Qur'an's teaching on the relation of the man and God: *So this Day We forget them, as they forgot the meeting with this Day of theirs*. The statement of the forgetting of God is the statement that raises the question of the ʿaqīdah, and the question is the question that the commentators answered with care. God does not forget in the form of the loss of the knowledge, and the loss of the knowledge is not the state of the One who is the All-Knowing. The Qur'an states the form of the teaching in the same way in the verse of the Tā-Hā:

> *"Indeed, He — God — errs not and does not forget."* (20:52, in the form of the statement of the non-forgetting of God)

The forgetting of the verse is not the forgetting in the form of the loss of the knowledge; the forgetting of the verse is the forgetting in the form of the neglect, the abandonment, the one who is not responded to. The man who is forgotten by God in the verse is the man who is in the one who is not responded to in his plea, and who is not shown the mercy in the form of the relief. The logic of the teaching is the logic of the reciprocity that the Qur'an states again and again: the man who forgets God is the man who is forgotten by God, and the forgetting of the man is the forgetting that is the closing of the door of the remembrance.

The Qur'an states the law of the reciprocity in the other verses, and the statement is the statement that is in the form of the confirmation of the teaching:

> *"But when they forget that of which they were reminded, We open for them every door. And until, when they rejoiced in what they were doing, We seized them suddenly. And they were in despair."* (6:44, in the form of the statement of the forgetting)

And the verse in Sūrah Sabaʾ states the form of the forgetting of the one who forgets God:

> *"And as for those who do not believe in the meeting with Us and are deluded by the worldly life, and who are secure in [repose from] their disbelief, for those is the abode of the Fire."* (32:20, in the form of the statement of the delusion and the security)

The two verses are the two forms of the same statement, and the statement is the statement that is in the form of the confirmation of the law of the reciprocity: the man who forgets the meeting with the Day is the man who is forgotten by God in the Day, and the forgetting of the man is the forgetting that is the abandonment in the punishment.

**The Reciprocity of the Deceiver**

The logic of the reciprocity extends beyond the forgetting of God. The Qur'an's teaching on the reciprocity is the teaching that the deceiver is deceived, that the one who withholds is withheld, and that the one who turns from the mercy is turned from the mercy. The statement of the verse is the statement that is in the form of the same law: the man who took the religion for the play is the man who is treated in the form of the play, and the man who was deluded by the world is the man who is deluded by the world in the hour of the account. The recompense in the Hereafter is the recompense that is in the form of the kind of the deed in the world, and the kind of the deed of the one who took the religion for the play is the kind of the treatment in the form of the play, and the treatment in the form of the play is the treatment that is the forgetting and the abandonment. The verse, in its form, is the form of the statement of the law of the reciprocity, and the law of the reciprocity is the law that is in the form of the introduction of the statement of the Book in the next verse.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:52

> **We have indeed brought them a Book, which We have expounded with knowledge, as a guidance and a mercy for a people who believe.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Book That Is Brought**

The verse moves from the description of the state of the inhabitants of the Fire to the statement of the Book that is the cause of the state, and the statement is the statement that is in the form of the short and complete summary of the position of the Book in the sūrah: *We have indeed brought them a Book*. The Book in the verse is the Book that the commentators identified with the divine revelation that is sent to all the people, on the report of the general statement of the Qur'an that the messengers were sent to every community with the same message of the worship of God and the avoidance of the false deities (16:36). Some of the commentators, notably al-Rāzī, held that the Book in the verse is the Qur'an specifically, and the identification with the Qur'an is the identification that is the book that is in the hand of the sūrah. The two identifications are the two forms of the same teaching: the Book is the Book that is the revelation that is the form of the guidance and the mercy.

**The Expounding With Knowledge**

The verse states the form of the Book, and the form is the form of the expounding: *which We have expounded with knowledge*. The word for "expounded" is *tafṣīlan*, from the root *f-ṣ-l*, and the root carries the sense of the separation, the distinction, the making distinct of the things. The expounding of the Book is the expounding that is the making distinct of the truth from the falsehood, and the making distinct of the truth from the falsehood is the making distinct that is the clarity of the Book. The addition of the word "with knowledge" is the addition that is in the form of the statement of the form of the expounding: the expounding is the expounding that is the knowledge, and the knowledge is the knowledge that is the complete understanding of the Book.

The logic of the statement is the logic that is worth stating: the Book is the Book that is the full clarity, and the full clarity of the Book is the full clarity that is the removal of the excuse of the obscurity. The man who is in the denial of the Book cannot claim the excuse of the obscurity, because the Book is the Book that is the expounding with the knowledge, and the expounding with the knowledge is the expounding that is the making clear of the truth and the falsehood.

**The Guidance and the Mercy for the Believers**

The verse closes with the statement of the function of the Book, and the function is the function that is in the form of the two things: the guidance and the mercy. The guidance is the guidance that is the directing to the way, and the mercy is the mercy that is the covering with the compassion. The two functions are the two functions that are the Book, and the Book is the Book that is the guidance and the mercy for the people who believe.

The qualification of the "people who believe" is the qualification that is in the form of the teaching that the Qur'an states again and again: the Book is the Book that is the guidance for the reverent, on the statement of the opening of Sūrah al-Baqarah:

> *"This is the Book about which there is no doubt, a guidance for those conscious of God."* (2:2, in the form of the statement of the guidance for the reverent)

The logic of the qualification is the logic that is worth stating: the Book is the Book that is the full clarity for every one, and the full clarity of the Book is the full clarity that is the removal of the excuse of the obscurity for every one. But the effect of the Book is the effect that is the belief, and the belief is the belief that is the response of the heart to the Book. The man who is in the belief is the man who is in the receiving of the guidance and the mercy, and the man who is in the denial is the man who is in the exclusion from the guidance and the mercy, even if the Book is the Book that is the full clarity for him. The logic of the teaching is the logic that is in the form of the justice of the guidance: God gives the clarity, and the man gives the response, and the response of the man is the response that is the belief or the denial. The verse, in its form, is the form of the introduction of the question of the waiting in the next verse.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:53

> **Do they wait for aught save the full disclosure thereof? The Day when its full disclosure comes, those who forgot it beforehand will say, “The messengers of our Lord indeed brought the truth! Have we any intercessors who might intercede for us? Or might we be returned, that we might do other than what we used to do?” They have surely lost their souls, and that which they used to fabricate has forsaken them.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Waiting for the Full Disclosure**

The verse opens with the rhetorical question that is in the form of the statement of the state of the disbelievers: *Do they wait for aught save the full disclosure thereof?* The word for "full disclosure" is *taʾwīl*, and the word is the word that the Qur'an uses in the other verses for the interpretation of the revelation, and for the fulfillment of the events that the revelation describes. The full disclosure of the Book in the verse is the full disclosure that is the fulfillment of the promises and the threats of the Book, and the fulfillment of the promises and the threats is the fulfillment that is the resurrection and the judgment and the reward and the punishment. The waiting of the disbelievers is the waiting that is the one who is not in the form of the preparation for the full disclosure, and the full disclosure is the full disclosure that is the coming that is certain.

**The Three Requests of the Hour**

The verse states the scene of the full disclosure, and the scene is the scene in which the ones who forgot the Book beforehand are the ones who speak the three requests, and the three requests are the three requests that are the last hour of the life. The first request is the confession: *The messengers of our Lord indeed brought the truth*. The confession is the confession that is the acknowledgment of the truth of the mission of the messengers, and the acknowledgment of the truth is the acknowledgment that is the hour in which the acknowledgment is too late. The second request is the request of the intercession: *Have we any intercessors who might intercede for us?* The request of the intercession is the request that is the seeking of the help of the other in the hour of the account. The Qur'an states the form of the day of the intercession in the other verses, and the statement is the statement that is in the form of the confirmation of the teaching:

> *"No intercession will be of any benefit on that Day, except for the one to whom permission is given by the Most Merciful and He is pleased with his word."* (20:109, in the form of the statement of the day of the intercession)

The third request is the request of the return: *Or might we be returned, that we might do other than what we used to do?* The request of the return is the request that is the seeking of the second chance in the form of the return to the world. The Qur'an states the form of the answer to the request of the return in the other verses, and the statement is the statement that is in the form of the confirmation of the teaching:

> *"But if you would, you would wish to be shot through with arrows and they would be struck down with a terrible strike."* (26:102, in the form of the statement of the request of the return)

And the verse in Sūrah al-Anʿām states the form of the answer to the request of the return in the same way:

> *"And if your Lord so desired, He could have made mankind one community; and they cease not to differ, except those upon whom your Lord has bestowed His mercy. And for that He created them. And the word of your Lord is fulfilled: I will surely fill Hell with jinn and men, most of them."* (11:119, in the form of the statement of the difference)

The logic of the three requests is the logic that is worth stating: the three requests are the three forms of the last hour of the life, and the three forms are the three forms that are the seeking of the cure that is not available in the hour of the account. The confession is the cure that is too late, the intercession is the cure that is not for the deniers of the messengers, and the return is the cure that is not available, because the man who is in the denial in the long day of the life is the man who is in the return to the denial in the second chance.

**The Loss of the Soul and the Forsaking of the Fabrications**

The verse closes with the statement of the two results, and the two results are the two results that are the end of the requests. The first result is the loss of the soul: *They have surely lost their souls*. The word for "lost" is *khāsat*, from the root *k-h-s-r*, and the word is the same word that the sūrah used in verse 9 for the ruin of the soul of the one whose balance is light. The loss of the soul is the loss that is the greatest loss. The second result is the forsaking of the fabrications: *and that which they used to fabricate has forsaken them*. The word for "fabricated" is *yaftarūn*, from the root *f-t-r*, and the root carries the sense of the fabrication, the making of the thing that is not, the making of the lie. The fabrications of the disbelievers are the fabrications that are the false idols and the false beliefs, and the forsaking of the fabrications is the forsaking that is the abandonment of the fabrications of their worshippers.

The connection of the verse with verse 37 of the sūrah is the connection that is in the form of the state of the double forsaking: the false idols forsake their worshippers in verse 37, and the fabrications forsake their fabricators in this verse, and the double forsaking is the double forsaking that is the end of the relation between the false and the false. The verse, in its form, is the form of the statement of the three requests and the two results, and the three requests and the two results are the three requests and the two results that are in the form of the introduction of the discourse of the signs of God in creation in the next verse.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:54

> **Truly your Lord is God, Who created the heavens and the earth in six days, then mounted the Throne. He causes the night to cover the day, which pursues it swiftly; and the sun, the moon, and the stars are made subservient by His Command. Do not creation and command belong to Him? Blessed is God, Lord of the worlds!**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Six Days Without the Rest**

The verse opens a brief discourse on the signs of God in creation, and the discourse runs through verse 58, serving as the introduction of the long narrative section of the destroyed peoples that follows. The first statement of the discourse is the statement of the creation: *Who created the heavens and the earth in six days*. The statement appears in the same short form in several places in the Qur'an (10:3; 11:7; 25:59; 32:4; 50:38; 57:4), and the repetition is the repetition that is the form of the teaching. The statement differs from the Biblical account in the detail that is worth stating: the Qur'an does not attribute the rest to God on the seventh day, and the non-attribution of the rest is the non-attribution that is in the form of the state of the One who is in the condition of the one who is in the form of the non-slumber and the non-tiredness. The Qur'an states the form of the teaching in the same way in the verse of the Āyat al-Kursī:

> *"No slumber overtakes Him, nor sleep."* (2:255)

And the verse in Sūrah Qāf states the same in the form of the statement:

> *"And We created the heavens and the earth and that between them in six days, and no fatigue touched Us."* (50:38)

The two verses are the two forms of the same statement, and the statement is the statement that is in the form of the confirmation of the non-rest of God. The logic of the non-rest is the logic that is in the form of the ʿaqīdah: the rest is the rest that is the tiredness, and the tiredness is not the state of the One who is the Creator, and the state of the One who is the Creator is the state that is in the form of the non-tiredness. The Islamic tradition has no sabbath in the form of the Jewish and Christian sabbath, and the non-existence of the sabbath is the non-existence that is in the form of the state of the non-rest of God.

**The Mounting of the Throne**

The verse adds the statement that follows the statement of the creation, and the statement is the statement that is one of the most discussed statements in the ʿaqīdah of the Islam: *then mounted the Throne*. The word for "mounted" is *istawā*, from the root *w-ṣ-w*, and the root carries the sense of the establishment, the settling, the establishment in the place. The mounting of the Throne raises the question of the corporeality, and the question is the question that the commentators answered with the utmost care. The mounting is not the mounting in the form of the movement of the body, and the body is not the state of the One who is the Creator, and the state of the One who is the Creator is the state that is in the form of the non-corporeality. The commentators held that the mounting of the Throne is the mounting that is in the form of the demonstration of the sovereignty of God over His creation, and the demonstration of the sovereignty is the demonstration that is the establishment of the lordship.

The hadith that the collections preserve states the form of the teaching in the way that makes the ʿaqīdah concrete:

> *"Verily, God is in the heaven, and He is above His Throne."* (Bukhari)

The hadith is the form of the statement of the mounting: the mounting of God is the mounting that is the being above the Throne, and the being above the Throne is the being that is the sovereignty over the creation.

**The Six Days and the Day of the Gathering**

The commentators discussed the meaning of the six days, and the discussion is the discussion that is worth stating in full. Some of the commentators held that the six days are the six days of the twenty-four hours, and the holding of the six days is the holding that is the literal understanding. Others held that the six days are not the six days of the twenty-four hours, and the holding of the non-literal understanding is the holding that is the symbolic understanding, on the statement of the Qur'an:

> *"Indeed, a day with your Lord is like a thousand years of those which you count."* (22:47)

The two holdings are the two forms of the same teaching: the six days are the six days that are the creation, and the state of the creation is the state that is in the form of the non-exactness of the measure of the day. The Islamic tradition holds that the six days began on Sunday and continued through Friday, and the Friday is the day on which the tradition holds that God created Adam and gathered all the creation, and the gathering of the creation is the gathering that is in the form of the state of the root of the name of the day. The hadith that the collections preserve states the form of the state of the Friday in the way that makes the teaching concrete:

> *"The best day on which the sun rises is Friday: on it Adam was created, on it he was admitted to Paradise, and on it he was expelled from it, and the Hour will not occur except on a Friday."* (Bukhari, Muslim)

The hadith is the form of the state of the Friday: the Friday is the day that is in the form of the state of the creation of Adam and the gathering of the creation, and the state of the creation of Adam and the gathering of the creation is the state that is in the form of the preeminence of the Friday in the Islam.
