**The Covering of the Day and the Subjection of the Stars**

The verse continues with the statement of the alternation of the night and the day, and the statement is the statement that is in the form of the sign of the power of God: *He causes the night to cover the day, which pursues it swiftly*. The word for "cover" is *yaghshā*, from the root *g-h-sh*, and the root carries the sense of the covering over, the covering of the thing. The night covers the day, and the day pursues the night, and the covering and the pursuing are the two forms of the alternation of the two. The Qur'an uses the alternation of the night and the day as the sign of the power and the beneficence of God in several places (2:164; 3:190; 13:3; 17:12; 39:5), and the use of the alternation as the sign is the use that is the reflection on the order of the creation.

The verse then states the subjection of the sun and the moon and the stars, and the statement is the statement that is in the form of the sign of the command of God: *and the sun, the moon, and the stars are made subservient by His Command*. The word for "made subservient" is *masakhirāt*, from the root *s-kh-r*, and the root carries the sense of the subjection, the submitting to the command. The sun and the moon and the stars are the stars that are the submission to the command of God, and the submission to the command of God is the submission that is the running of the order of the cosmos.

**The Question of the Creation and the Command**

The verse closes with the rhetorical question that is in the form of the statement of the sovereignty of God, and the statement is the statement that is in the form of the two things: the creation and the command: *Do not creation and command belong to Him?* The two things are the two aspects of the lordship of God: the creation is the act of the bringing forth of the thing, and the command is the act of the directing of the thing, and the two acts are the two acts that are in the form of the state of the One who is the Creator and the One who is the Commander. The logic of the question is the logic that is worth stating: the One who creates the thing is the One who has the right to command the thing, and the right to command the thing is the right that is the sovereignty over the thing. The question of the verse is the question that is in the form of the statement of the sovereignty of God over the creation and the command, and the sovereignty of God over the creation and the command is the sovereignty that is the blessedness of God, the Lord of the worlds.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:55

> **Call upon your Lord humbly and in secret. Truly He loves not the transgressors.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Call in the Form of the Humility and the Secret**

The verse is the command that follows the discourse of the signs of the creation, and the command is the command that is the calling upon God: *Call upon your Lord humbly and in secret*. The two qualities of the call are the two qualities that are the duʿāʾ: the humility, and the secret. The word for "humbly" is *taḍāḍiran*, from the root *d-h-r*, and the root carries the sense of the humility, the softness of the voice, the lowering of the voice. The word for "in secret" is *was-sarra*, from the root *s-r-r*, and the root carries the sense of the secrecy, the hiddenness of the call. The two qualities are the two qualities that are the duʿāʾ that is in the form of the sincerity.

The logic of the two qualities is the logic that is worth stating: the duʿāʾ is the duʿāʾ that is the recognition of the dependence, and the recognition of the dependence is the recognition that is the humility. The man who is in the duʿāʾ is the man who is in the one who knows that he is in the condition of the one who is in the form of the dependence on the One who is the One who responds to the call, and the knowledge of the dependence is the knowledge that is the humility of the voice. The secret of the duʿāʾ is the secret that is the absence of the showing off, and the absence of the showing off is the absence that is the sincerity of the call.

The hadith that the sources gather states the form of the teaching in the way that makes the duʿāʾ concrete:

> *"There is nothing more noble before God than supplication (duʿāʾ), for supplication is worship."* (Reported in the later collections)

The hadith is the form of the verse: the duʿāʾ is the duʿāʾ that is in the form of the state of the worship, and the state of the worship is the state that is in the form of the nobility of the duʿāʾ before God.

**The Two Extremes of the Voice**

The command of the verse is the command that is in the form of the correction of the two extremes of the voice: the extreme of the loud voice, and the extreme of the excessive length. The Qur'an states the form of the middle in the same way in the verse of the Isrāʾ:

> *"And do not raise your voice in your prayer or lower it, but seek a mean between the two."* (17:110, in the form of the statement of the middle of the voice)

The verse is the form of the confirmation of the command of the verse: the voice of the duʿāʾ is the voice that is the mean between the two extremes, and the mean between the two extremes is the mean that is the humility and the secret. The hadith that the sources gather states the form of the correction of the excessive length in the way that makes the teaching concrete:

> *"It is sufficient for a man to say: 'O God, I ask You for the Garden and for whatever word or deed draws me near to it; and I seek refuge in You from the Fire and from whatever word or deed draws me near to it.'"* (Al-Tirmidhī)

The hadith is the form of the command of the verse: the duʿāʾ is the duʿāʾ that is the short and complete asking for the reward and the seeking of the refuge from the punishment, and the short and complete duʿāʾ is the duʿāʾ that is the non-excess in the duʿāʾ.

**The Transgressors That God Does Not Love**

The verse closes with the statement of the attribute of God, and the attribute is the attribute of the love: *Truly He loves not the transgressors*. The word for "transgressors" is *al-muʿtidīn*, from the root *ʿ-d-w*, and the root carries the sense of the transgression, the exceeding of the bound, the going beyond the limit. The transgressors of the verse are the transgressors that are the exceeding of the bound in anything, and the exceeding of the bound in anything is the exceeding that is the excess in the voice or the excess in the length or the excess in the deed. The logic of the statement is the logic that is worth stating: the love of God is the love that is the justice, and the justice of the love is the justice that is the non-excess and the non-deficiency. The man who is in the excess is the man who is in the state of the one who is not loved by God, and the state of the one who is not loved by God is the state that is in the form of the warning of the sūrah.

The verse, in its form, is the form of the command of the duʿāʾ and the warning of the excess, and the command of the duʿāʾ and the warning of the excess are the command of the duʿāʾ and the warning of the excess that are in the form of the introduction of the command of the non-corruption of the earth in the next verse.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:56

> **And work not corruption upon the earth after it has been set aright, but call upon Him in fear and in hope. Surely the Mercy of God is ever nigh unto the virtuous.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Corruption and the Setting Aright**

The verse opens with the command that is the non-corruption of the earth: *And work not corruption upon the earth after it has been set aright*. The two words of the command are the two words that are the juxtaposition of the two acts: the corruption of the man, and the setting aright of God. The word for "corruption" is *fasād*, from the root *f-s-d*, and the root carries the sense of the corruption, the disorder, the disorder of the thing. The word for "set aright" is *iṣlāh*, from the root *ṣ-l-ḥ*, and the root carries the sense of the rectification, the establishment, the order of the thing. The juxtaposition of the two acts is the juxtaposition that is in the form of the teaching: the earth is the earth that is the setting aright of God, and the man is the man who is the corruption of the earth, and the corruption of the earth is the corruption that is the undoing of the setting aright of God.

**The Five Categories of the Corruption**

The commentators drew out the categories of the corruption of the earth with care, and the categories are the categories that are worth stating in full. Al-Rāzī held that the warning against the corruption of the earth is the warning that is in the form of the prohibition of any act that corrupts the five things: the body, through the violence; the wealth, through the fraud and the theft; the religion, through the disbelief and the innovation; the lineage, through the adultery and the slander; and the intellect, through the intoxication. The five categories are the five categories that are the corruption of the whole of the life, and the corruption of the whole of the life is the corruption that is the spreading of the corruption upon the earth. The Qur'an states the form of the corruption of the killing in the other verses, and the statement is the statement that is in the form of the confirmation of the teaching:

> *"Whoever kills a soul unless for a soul or for corruption [done] in the land — it is as if he had slain mankind entirely. And whoever lets one live — it is as if he had let mankind live entirely."* (5:32, in the form of the statement of the corruption of the killing)

The verse is the form of the confirmation: the corruption of the killing is the corruption that is the corruption of the whole of the mankind, and the corruption of the whole of the mankind is the corruption that is the corruption of the earth.

**The Corruption of the Environment**

The corruption of the earth in the verse is the corruption that is the corruption of the natural environment, in the modern form of the teaching. The setting aright of God is the setting aright that is the balance and the harmony of the natural order, and the balance and the harmony of the natural order are the balance and the harmony that are the beneficence of the earth for the mankind. The man who is in the corruption of the natural environment is the man who is in the undoing of the setting aright of God, and the undoing of the setting aright of God is the undoing that is the corruption of the earth in the modern form of the teaching. The Qur'an states the form of the balance of the creation in the other verses, and the statement is the statement that is in the form of the confirmation of the teaching:

> *"It is He who has made the earth submissive to you. So traverse its regions and eat of His provision. And to Him is the return."* (67:15, in the form of the statement of the submissiveness of the earth)

The verse is the form of the confirmation: the earth is the earth that is the submissiveness to the man, and the submissiveness of the earth is the submissiveness that is the trust of the man in the earth, and the trust of the man in the earth is the trust that is the non-corruption of the earth.

**The Fear and the Hope**

The verse states the second command, and the second command is the command that is the calling upon God in the two qualities: *but call upon Him in fear and in hope*. The two qualities are the two qualities that are the two poles of the religious consciousness of the believer: the fear of the wrath of God, and the hope of the mercy of God. The balance of the two poles is the balance that is the prevention of the two states: the state of the moral laxity, which is the excess of the hope without the fear, and the state of the spiritual despair, which is the excess of the fear without the hope. The hadith qudsī that the collections preserve states the form of the teaching in the way that makes the balance concrete:

> *"When God decreed the created realm, He prescribed for Himself in a Book that is with Him: 'My mercy prevails over My wrath.'"* (Bukhari)

The hadith is the form of the verse: the mercy of God is the mercy that is the preceding of the wrath, and the preceding of the mercy is the preceding that is the balance of the fear and the hope.

**The Mercy That Is Nigh unto the Virtuous**

The verse closes with the statement of the mercy, and the statement is the statement that is the nearness of the mercy to the virtuous: *Surely the Mercy of God is ever nigh unto the virtuous*. The word for "virtuous" is *al-muḥsanīn*, from the root *ḥ-s-n*, and the root carries the sense of the doing of the good, the excellence, the iḥsān. The nearness of the mercy to the muḥsinīn is the nearness that is the singling out of the mercy for the doers of the good, on the statement of the Qur'an that God singles out for His mercy whomsoever He wills (2:105; 3:74), and that God causes whomsoever He wills to enter into His mercy (48:25). The verse, in its form, is the form of the command of the non-corruption of the earth and the command of the calling upon God in the fear and the hope, and the two commands are the two commands that are the preservation of the earth and the preservation of the heart.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:57

> **He it is Who sends the winds as glad tidings ahead of His Mercy, so that when they bear heavy-laden clouds, We may drive them toward a land that is dead, and send down water upon it, and thereby bring forth every kind of fruit. Thus shall We bring forth the dead, that haply you may remember.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Winds as the Glad Tidings**

The verse continues the discourse of the signs of the creation, and it moves from the discourse of the heavens to the discourse of the earth, and the discourse of the earth begins with the discourse of the winds: *He it is Who sends the winds as glad tidings ahead of His Mercy*. The word for "glad tidings" is *bashāran*, from the root *b-sh-r*, and the root carries the sense of the good news, the announcement of the good. The winds of the verse are the winds that are the announcement of the mercy, and the announcement of the mercy is the announcement that is the rain. The logic of the teaching is the logic that is worth stating: the wind is the wind that is the harbinger of the mercy, and the harbinger of the mercy is the harbinger that is the glad tidings of the rain.

The Qur'an states the same teaching in the other verses, and the statement is the statement that is in the form of the confirmation of the teaching:

> *"And it is He who sends the winds as tidings preceder of His mercy until, when they have driven heavy-laden clouds, We drive them to a dead land and revive thereby the earth after its death. Thus will be the resurrection."* (30:46, in the form of the statement of the winds as the glad tidings)

The verse is the form of the confirmation: the winds are the winds that are the glad tidings of the mercy, and the glad tidings of the mercy are the glad tidings that are the revival of the dead land.

**The Revival of the Dead Land**

The verse states the form of the act of God, and the act is the act that is in the form of the revival of the dead land: *We may drive them toward a land that is dead, and send down water upon it, and thereby bring forth every kind of fruit*. The two acts of the verse are the two acts that are the driving of the clouds and the sending down of the water, and the two acts are the two acts that are the revival of the dead land. The dead land is the dead land that is the drought, and the drought of the dead land is the drought that is the death of the land. The revival of the dead land is the revival that is the bringing forth of the fruits, and the bringing forth of the fruits is the bringing forth that is the every kind of the fruit.

The logic of the teaching is the logic that is worth stating: the revival of the dead land is the revival that is the metaphor of the resurrection of the man, and the metaphor of the resurrection of the man is the metaphor that is the proof of the resurrection. The Qur'an uses the revival of the dead land as the proof of the resurrection in several places (30:19; 35:9; 43:11; 50:11), and the use of the revival as the proof is the use that is the argument of the creation to the resurrection, on the form of the argument in verse 29 of the sūrah. The logic of the argument is the logic that is in the form of the teaching: the One who revives the dead land is the One who revives the dead man, and the revival of the dead man is the revival that is the resurrection.

**The Fear and the Hope of the Storm**

The verse, in the form of the discourse of the rain, states the form of the teaching of the fear and the hope that the previous verse commanded, and the form is the storm. The storm is the storm that is the rain, and the rain is the rain that is the life, but the storm is the storm that is the destruction. The gathering of the storm is the gathering that is the reminder of the fear and the hope, and the reminder of the fear and the hope is the reminder that is the calling upon God in the two qualities of the previous verse. The Qur'an states the form of the fear and the hope of the lightning in the other verses, and the statement is the statement that is in the form of the confirmation of the teaching:

> *"And He is the One who sends down rain after [periods of] difficulty for them and scatters [thereby] His mercy. And He is the Inheritor, the Praiseworthy."* (7:57, in the form of the statement of the rain after the difficulty)

The verse, in its form, is the form of the teaching of the rain as the mercy and the storm as the warning, and the two forms of the teaching are the two forms that are in the form of the introduction of the parable of the good land and the bad land in the next verse.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:58

> **As for the good land, its vegetation comes forth by the leave of its Lord. And as for the bad, it comes forth but sparsely. Thus do We vary the signs for a people who give thanks.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Two Lands and the One Rain**

The verse is the parable that closes the discourse of the rain, and the parable is the parable that is the two lands: the good land and the bad land. The rain of the previous verse is the rain that falls upon the two lands, and the rain that falls upon the two lands is the rain that is the same mercy, and the same mercy is the same mercy that is the different results. The good land is the good land that is the abundance of the vegetation, and the bad land is the bad land that is the sparseness of the vegetation. The two lands are the two lands that are the same rain and the different results, and the same rain and the different results are the same rain and the different results that are in the form of the teaching of the parable.

**The Land as the Heart**

The commentators drew out the meaning of the parable with care, and the meaning is the meaning that is worth stating in full. The two lands of the parable are the two lands that are the two hearts: the believing heart and the disbelieving heart. The rain is the rain that is the mercy of God, and the mercy of God is the mercy that is the revelation. The believing heart is the believing heart that is the good land, and the good land is the good land that is the bringing forth of the vegetation by the leave of its Lord. The disbelieving heart is the disbelieving heart that is the bad land, and the bad land is the bad land that is the sparseness of the vegetation. The logic of the parable is the logic that is worth stating: the mercy of God is the mercy that is the rain that falls upon the two hearts, and the rain that falls upon the two hearts is the rain that is the different results in the two hearts, and the different results are the different results that are the receptivity of the heart.

The commentators connected the coming of the divine mercy, the descent of the rain, and the descent of the Qur'an, and the connection is the connection that is the teaching that the words of the Qur'an are the words that are the taking root in the soul of the believers, and the taking root in the soul of the believers is the taking root that is the bearing fruit through the thoughts and the actions. The words of the Qur'an are the words that are the bringing no benefit to the ones who are predisposed to the disbelief, and the bringing no benefit to the ones who are predisposed to the disbelief is the bringing no benefit that is the sparseness of the vegetation of the bad land.

**The Varying of the Signs**

The verse closes with the statement of the varying of the signs, and the statement is the statement that is the demonstration of the truth in the multiple ways: *Thus do We vary the signs for a people who give thanks*. The word for "vary" is *nubayyinu*, from the root *b-y-n*, and the root carries the sense of the making clear, the demonstration, the making evident of the thing. The varying of the signs is the varying that is the use of the symbols and the parables, and the use of the symbols and the parables is the use that is the making clear of the truth for the people who give thanks. The people who give thanks are the people who are the inclination to the reflection upon the spiritual meaning of the natural phenomena, and the inclination to the reflection is the inclination that is the thanksgiving.

The logic of the teaching is the logic that is worth stating: the sign of God is the sign that is the demonstration of the truth in the multiple ways, and the demonstration of the truth in the multiple ways is the demonstration that is the reaching of the different capacities of the different hearts. The man who is in the thanksgiving is the man who is in the reflection upon the signs, and the reflection upon the signs is the reflection that is the receiving of the demonstration of the truth.

The parable of the verse is the parable that serves as the introduction of the long narrative section of the destroyed peoples that follows, and the introduction is the introduction that is the teaching that the communities that rejected the messages of the warning are the communities that are the bad land, and the bad land is the bad land that is the sparseness of the results of the prophetic warnings. The verse, in its form, is the form of the end of the discourse of the signs of the creation, and the end of the discourse is the end that is in the form of the introduction of the first narrative of the destroyed peoples, the narrative of the people of Noah.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:59

> **Indeed, We sent Noah unto his people, and he said, “O my people! Worship God! You have no god other than Him. Truly I fear for you the punishment of a tremendous day!”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Opening of the Procession**

The verse opens the long narrative section of the sūrah, the procession of the destroyed peoples that runs from this verse through the story of Pharaoh. The accounts of the prophets in this section share a remarkable unity of form: each begins with the same call to the worship of the One God, each meets the same rejection from the notables of the people, each ends with the same destruction and the same salvation of the prophet and his believers. The repetition of the form is not the repetition of the information; the repetition of the form is the repetition of the teaching, and the teaching is the teaching that the history of the communities of the prophets is the history of the same story told again and again, in which the message is the same, the rejection is the same, and the result is the same. The sūrah presents the cases in the order that is the form of the warning: the listener who hears the first case is the listener who is made to recognize the pattern, and the pattern is the pattern that makes the warning concrete.

**The First Account of Noah**

This is the first narrative account of Noah in the order of the text of the Qur'an, and the other accounts are in 10:71–73, 11:25–48, 23:23–30, 26:105–21, 54:9–15, and Sūrah 71, which is the sūrah that carries the name of the prophet. The repetition of the story of Noah in the Qur'an is the repetition that is the importance of the story, and the importance of the story is the importance that is the first prophet of the series. The Islamic tradition identifies Noah as Nūḥ ibn Lamak ibn Mitūshalah ibn Ukhnūkh, the last of the four being identified with Idrīs, and the other tradition traces the lineage of Noah back to Adam through Shīth. Noah is said to have been a carpenter, and to have been fifty years old at the time of his call, and the chronology of the tradition places him as the prophet sent after Idrīs.

**The Call That Unifies the Prophets**

The call of Noah is the call that is the modified form of the testament of the faith: *Worship God! You have no god other than Him*. The call is the call that is the unifying element of the prophetic narratives of the section: the same call is made by Hūd in verse 65, by Ṣāli in verse 73, and by Shuʿayb in verse 85. The four calls are the four forms of the same statement, and the statement is the statement that is in the form of the teaching of the fundamental unity of the messages of the prophets. The unity of the messages is the unity that is in the form of the state of the understanding of the Islam that Muhammad ﷺ is the restorer of the one original and true religion that was brought by all the prophets, and not the founder of a new religion. The Qur'an states the form of the teaching in the same way in the verse of the Ṭā-Hā:

> *"Say: I am no innovation among the messengers, nor do I know what will be done with me or with you. I only follow that which is revealed to me by revelation, and I am but a clear warner."* (46:9, in the form of the statement of the restoration)

The difference between the Qur'anic account and the Biblical account is the difference that is worth stating: the Biblical account of Noah does not mention the worship of other gods by his people, and the Qur'an indicates that they worshipped the false deities that are named in Sūrah 71: Wadd, Suwāʿ, Yaʿūth, Yaʿūq, and Naʣr. Some of the commentators held that these were the names of the ancestors of the people of Noah, whom the people had, over the long time, come to worship, and the deification of the ancestors is the deification that is the slow corruption of the worship.

**The Fear of the Tremendous Day**

The call of Noah closes with the warning, and the warning is the warning that is in the form of the state of the fear of the prophet for his people: *Truly I fear for you the punishment of a tremendous day*. The word for "tremendous" is *al-ʿaẓīm*, from the root *ʿ-ẓ-m*, and the root carries the sense of the greatness, the magnitude, the great event. The tremendous day of the verse is the day that the commentators identified with the day of the flood, and with the day of the judgment, and with the two days together. The fear of the prophet is the fear that is the love of the prophet for his people, and the love of the prophet for his people is the love that is the warning that is in the condition of the mercy. The logic of the warning is the logic that is in the form of the teaching: the prophet who warns is the prophet who is the fear for the people whom he loves, and the fear for the people is the fear that is the warning that is in the condition of the mercy.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:60

> **The notables among his people said, “Truly we think that you are in manifest error.”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Notables Who Voice the Rejection**

The verse states the response of the people of Noah, and the response is the response that is the notables. The word for "notables" is the word that the sūrah uses for the leaders and the great ones of the people, and the notables are the notables who voice the rejection of the prophet the representatives of the community. The pattern of the notables is the pattern that is the punishment narratives of the Qur'an: in most of the accounts, it is the notables who articulate the rejection of the prophets (7:66, 75, 88, 103; 10:75, 88; 11:27, 38, 97; 23:24, 33, 46; 26:34; 28:20, 32). The Qur'an states the form of the pattern in the same way in the verse of the Anʿām:

> *"And in no town are there any of the guilty who would dwell therein but they will be destroyed."* (6:123, in the form of the statement of the guilty in the towns)

The logic of the pattern is the logic that is worth stating: the notables are the notables who are the holders of the status, and the holders of the status are the holders who are the ones who have the most to lose in the change of the religion. The notables reject the prophet not because they are the most ignorant of the people, but because they are the ones who are the holding of the status that is in the condition of the dependence on the old religion.

**The Purpose of the Narratives**

The narrative of the sūrah is the narrative that is the consolation of the Prophet ﷺ in the face of the rejection of the Quraysh. The repeated mention of the notables as the most outspoken critics of the previous prophets is the repeated mention that is the particular comfort to the Prophet ﷺ in the form of the struggle with the leaders of the Quraysh and their persistent opposition to the message. The parallel between the notables of the people of Noah and the notables of the Quraysh is the parallel that is the teaching that the rejection of the prophets is the rejection that is the notables, and the rejection of the notables is the rejection that is the history of the communities of the prophets. The recounting of the assistance of God to the righteous against those who reject His religion is the recounting that serves as the comfort for all the believers, and the comfort for all the believers is the comfort that is the hope of the victory of the truth.

**The Charge of the Manifest Error**

The charge of the notables is the charge that is the accusation of the error: *Truly we think that you are in manifest error*. The word for "error" is *ḍalāl*, from the root *ḍ-l-l*, and the word for "manifest" is *mubīn*, from the root *b-y-n*, and the two words together are the two words that are the clear and obvious error. The notables consider Noah to be in the manifest error for having rejected the worship of their idols, and the rejection of the worship of the idols is the rejection that is the error in the form of the judgment of the notables. The logic of the charge is the logic that is worth stating: the notables do not charge Noah with the error the ignorance; the notables charge Noah with the error the certainty, and the certainty of the charge is the certainty that is the "manifest" in the charge. The man who is in the charge of the manifest error is the man who is in the one who is certain of his own position, and the certainty of the own position is the certainty that is the refusal of the examination of the position.

The verse, in its form, is the form of the statement of the rejection of the notables, and the statement of the rejection is the statement that is in the form of the introduction of the reply of the prophet in the next verse.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:61

> **He said, “O my people! There is no error in me, but rather I am a messenger from the Lord of the worlds.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Denial and the Correction**

The verse is the reply of Noah to the charge of the notables, and the reply is the reply that is in the form of the two parts: the denial of the charge, and the assertion of the mission. The first part is the denial: *There is no error in me*. The denial is the denial that is the rejection of the charge, and the rejection of the charge is the rejection that is the assertion of the absence of the error. The second part is the correction, introduced by the word *bal*, "but rather": *I am a messenger from the Lord of the worlds*. The correction is the correction that is the replacement of the charge with the fact, and the replacement of the charge with the fact is the replacement that is the statement of the mission.

The logic of the reply is the logic that is worth stating: the reply of the prophet is not the reply the self-defense by the credentials; the reply of the prophet is the reply the declaration of the source. The prophet does not say, "Look at my character, look at my wisdom, look at my power"; the prophet says, "I am a messenger from the Lord of the worlds," and the declaration of the source is the declaration that is the transfer of the authority from the self to the source. The authority of the messenger is the authority that is the one who receives the authority from the Lord of the worlds, and the Lord of the worlds is the Lord who has the authority over all the worlds, including the world of the notables who reject the messenger.

**The Lord of the Worlds**

The phrase "the Lord of the worlds" is the phrase that is the opening of the Fātiḥah, and the phrase is the phrase that is the declaration of the lordship of God over all the realms of the creation. The logic of the phrase in the reply of Noah is the logic that is in the form of the teaching: the prophet who declares that he is the messenger of the Lord of the worlds is the prophet who is the placement of his mission in the form of the state of the lordship of God over all, and the placement of the mission in the form of the state of the lordship of God over all is the placement that is the removal of the question of the authority of the prophet. The notables who reject the prophet are the notables who are the one who rejects the messenger of the Lord of the worlds, and the rejection of the messenger of the Lord of the worlds is the rejection that is the rejection of the lordship of God over the worlds.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:62

> **I deliver unto you the messages of my Lord, and advise you sincerely, and I know from God what you know not.”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Three Declarations of the Messenger**

The verse completes the reply of Noah, and the reply is the reply that is in the form of the three declarations that are the three parts of the complete profile of the messenger. The first declaration is the declaration of the duty: *I deliver unto you the messages of my Lord*. The word for "deliver" is *aballighu*, from the root *b-l-gh*, and the root carries the sense of the reaching, the delivery, the conveying of the message to the destination. The delivery of the messages is the delivery that is the duty of the messenger, and the duty of the messenger is the duty that is the conveyance of the message and nothing more. The Qur'an states the form of the duty in the same way in the other verses, and the statement is the statement that is in the form of the confirmation of the teaching:

> *"The messengers are but conveyors."* (16:35, in the form of the statement of the duty)

The second declaration is the declaration of the intent: *and advise you sincerely*. The word for "advise sincerely" is *naṣīḥan mubīnan*, from the root *n-ṣ-*, and the root carries the sense of the sincerity, the advice, the seeking of the benefit of the one who is advised. The declaration of the sincere advice is the declaration that is the contrast with the false claim of the sincere advice of Satan in verse 21 of the sūrah. The two claims of the sincere advice are the two claims that are the contrast of the true and the false, and the contrast of the true and the false is the contrast that is the criterion of the distinction: the outcome. The sincere advice of the prophet is the sincere advice that ends in the benefit of the one who is advised, and the false claim of the sincere advice of Satan is the false claim that ends in the ruin of the one who is deceived.

The third declaration is the declaration of the source of the knowledge: *and I know from God what you know not*. The declaration of the knowledge from God is the declaration that is the knowledge of the great punishment that is about to come upon the people, on the report of the tradition. The knowledge from God is the knowledge that is the knowledge that is not the knowledge of the self, and the knowledge that is not the knowledge of the self is the knowledge that is the knowledge that is received from the source.

The three declarations are the three parts of the complete profile of the messenger: the duty of the delivery, the intent of the sincere advice, and the source of the knowledge. The logic of the profile is the logic that is in the form of the teaching: the messenger is the messenger who delivers the message that is not his own, with the intent that is not his own interest, with the knowledge that is not his own, and the three parts of the profile are the three parts that are the complete transfer of the authority from the self to the source. The verse, in its form, is the form of the complete reply of the prophet, and the complete reply of the prophet is the complete reply that is in the form of the introduction of the challenge of the next verse, in which the prophet challenges his people on the ground of the marvel of the human messenger.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:63

> **Or do you marvel that a reminder from your Lord should come unto you by means of a man from among yourselves, so as to warn you, that you might be reverent, and that haply you may receive mercy?”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Objection of the Human Messenger**

The verse is the challenge that Noah addresses to his people, and the challenge is the challenge that names one of the grounds of the rejection of the message: *Or do you marvel that a reminder from your Lord should come unto you by means of a man from among yourselves?* The word for "marvel" is *ʿajibakum*, from the root *ʿ-j-b*, and the root carries the sense of the astonishment, the wonder, the marvel at the thing. The objection of the people is the objection that is the incredulity that God would send the message through the ordinary man of their kind. The objection is the objection that the Qur'an records in the mouths of the people of the other prophets in the same form (11:27, 23:24), and the objection is the objection that is the common human tendency of the rejection of the human messenger.

The Quraysh who rejected the Prophet ﷺ rejected him in part for the same reason, and the reason is the reason that is the incredulity that God would send the message through the human being like themselves, rather than through the angel, or through the one who enjoyed the greater prestige among them. The Qur'an records the form of the demand of the Quraysh in the same way in the other verses, and the statement is the statement that is in the form of the confirmation of the teaching:

> *"They said: We will never believe in you until you cause a spring to gush forth for us from the earth, or you have a garden of palm trees and grapes and cause the rivers to gush forth through it."* (17:90, in the form of the statement of the demand)

And the verse in Sūrah al-Zukhruf states the form of the demand of the prestige in the same way:

> *"Why has there not been sent to us a messenger of the people? Then would they have guided us."* (43:45, in the form of the statement of the demand of the prestige)

The two verses are the two forms of the same objection, and the objection is the objection that is the demand of the messenger who is not of the kind of the people.

**The Logic of the Man From Among Yourselves**

The logic of the reply of the verse is the logic that is worth stating in full. The man from among the people is the man who is the same kind as the people, and the same kind as the people is the same kind that is the proof that the message is for the kind of the people. The messenger who is not of the kind of the people is the messenger who is the message for the kind other than the people, and the messenger who is of the kind of the people is the messenger who is the message for the kind of the people. The logic of the familiarity is the logic that is in the form of the teaching: the people know the man of their kind, and the knowledge of the man of their kind is the knowledge that is the verification of the character of the messenger. The people who reject the messenger of their kind are the people who are the rejection of the message in the form of the knowledge of the character of the messenger, and the rejection of the message in the form of the knowledge of the character of the messenger is the rejection that is the refusal of the examination of the message.

**The Chain of the Warning, the Reverence, and the Mercy**

The verse closes with the statement of the chain of the purpose of the warning, and the chain is the chain that is in the form of the three links: the warning, the reverence, and the mercy. The first link is the warning: the reminder comes in order to warn, and the warning is the warning that is the notice of the coming harm. The second link is the reverence: the warning is in order that the people may be reverent, and the reverence is the reverence that is the guarding of the heart from the state of the neglect of the account. The third link is the mercy: the reverence is in order that the people may receive the mercy, and the mercy is the mercy that is the covering of the compassion. The logic of the chain is the logic that is worth stating: the chain of the warning to the reverence to the mercy is the chain that is the way of the salvation, and the way of the salvation is the way that is the warning that is in the condition of the mercy. The verse, in its form, is the form of the challenge of the prophet and the chain of the salvation, and the challenge of the prophet and the chain of the salvation are the challenge of the prophet and the chain of the salvation that are in the form of the introduction of the statement of the denial and the flood in the next verse.

## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:64

> **Yet they denied him. So We saved him and those who were with him in the Ark, and We drowned those who denied Our signs. Truly they were a blind people.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Denial and the Flood**

The verse states the result of the story of Noah, and the result is the result that is in the form of the two acts: the salvation of the prophet and his believers, and the drowning of the deniers. The first statement is the statement of the denial: *Yet they denied him*. The denial is the denial that is the continuation of the rejection after the complete reply of the prophet, and the continuation of the rejection after the complete reply of the prophet is the continuation that is the closing of the door of the possibility of the acceptance. The second statement is the statement of the salvation: *So We saved him and those who were with him in the Ark*. The word for "Ark" is *al-jāriyah*, from the root *j-r-y*, and the root carries the sense of the moving, the sailing, the vessel that moves in the water. The Ark is the Ark that is the wooden vessel that Noah built under the command of God (11:37–38; 23:27; 54:13), and the wooden vessel is the wooden vessel that is the means of the salvation in the form of the flood.

The third statement is the statement of the drowning: *and We drowned those who denied Our signs*. The word for "drowned" is *gharaqnā*, from the root *g-h-r-q*, and the root carries the sense of the sinking, the going down into the water. The drowning of the deniers is the drowning that is the destruction of the people who denied the signs, and the destruction of the people who denied the signs is the destruction that is the fulfillment of the warning of the tremendous day.

**The Details of the Tradition**

The tradition gives the details of the salvation, and the details are the details that are worth stating. The ones who were saved along with Noah included the members of his family, on the report of the verse of the Ḥūd (11:40), except for one of his sons, and the son is the son who is described in 11:42–43, and perhaps his wife, who is described as the disbeliever in 66:10. The ones who were aboard the Ark are said to have included the three sons of Noah, Sām, Ḥām, and Yāfath, and their wives, and six other believers, on the report of some of the commentators. The other accounts indicate that there were eighty people saved in the Ark, forty men and forty women, on the report of the other commentators. The two reports are the two forms of the same teaching: the salvation is the salvation that is the few, and the few are the few who are the believers.

**The Pattern of the Salvation of the Prophets**

The pattern of the salvation of the prophets from the destruction of their people is the pattern that is the constant rule of the punishment narratives of the Qur'an. The prophets are always saved from the destruction that comes upon their people, and the salvation of the prophets is the salvation that is the believers who are with them. The Qur'an states the form of the rule in the rhetorical question of the Anʿām:

> *"Were the punishment of God to come upon you suddenly or openly, would any be destroyed save the wrongdoing people?"* (6:47, in the form of the statement of the rule)

The verse is the form of the confirmation of the pattern: the destruction of God is the destruction that is the striking of the wrongdoers, and the striking of the wrongdoers is the striking that is the sparing of the prophets and their believers.

**The Blindness That Is the Form of the Drowning**

The verse closes with the statement of the blindness: *Truly they were a blind people*. The word for "blind" is *ʿamāh*, from the root *ʿ-m-h*, and the root carries the sense of the blindness, the not seeing. The blindness of the people of Noah is the blindness that is the blindness of the eyes and the blindness of the heart, and the blindness of the eyes and the blindness of the heart are the two forms of the same blindness. The logic of the teaching is the logic that is worth stating: the physical drowning is the form of the spiritual blindness, and the spiritual blindness is the blindness that is the not seeing of the signs in the life. The people who were drowned in the flood are the people who were blind in the life, and the blindness in the life is the blindness that is the refusal of the seeing of the signs. The verse, in its form, is the form of the end of the story of Noah, and the end of the story of Noah is the end that is in the form of the introduction of the story of Hūd and his people of ʿĀd in the next verse.

## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:65

> **And unto ʿĀd, their brother, Hūd. He said, “O my people! Worship God! You have no god other than Him. Will you not be reverent?”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Pattern of the Serial Narrative**

The verse is the second link in the procession of the destroyed peoples, and the verse is the verse that is the pattern of the serial narrative: *And unto ʿĀd, their brother, Hūd*. The pattern is the pattern that is the "and to the people, their brother the prophet," and the pattern is the pattern that is repeated in the other links of the procession. The brotherhood of the prophet with his people is the brotherhood that is the kinship and the origin, and the kinship and the origin of the prophet with his people are the kinship and the origin that are the removal of the excuse of the strangeness of the messenger. The prophet of the people is the prophet who is one of the people, and the prophet who is one of the people is the prophet who is the man of the kind of the people, on the form of the teaching of verse 63.

**The People of ʿĀd**

The people of Hūd are the people of ʿĀd, and the people of ʿĀd are the people that the Islamic tradition identifies as the people of the southern Arabia, between Ḥaḍramawt in the southern Yemen and Oman. The region of the people of ʿĀd is the region that is the many sand dunes, and the sand dunes are the sand dunes that carry the name of Sūrah 46, al-Aḥqāf, which includes the account of the people of ʿĀd. Hūd has no counterpart in the Biblical text, and the Islamic tradition maintains that Hūd and his people are the descendants of Noah through the son of Noah, Sām. The tradition gives the lineage of Hūd in two forms: Hūd ibn Shāliḥ ibn Arfakhshād ibn Sām, and the other form is Hūd ibn ʿĀd ibn Iram ibn ʿAwṣ ibn Sām ibn Nūḥ, on the report of the verse of the Fajr:

> *"Have you not considered how your Lord dealt with ʿĀd, Iram of the pillars, the like of whom was not created in the land?"* (89:6–8, in the form of the statement of ʿĀd)

The two forms of the lineage are the two forms of the same teaching: the people of ʿĀd are the people who are the descendants of Noah, and the descendants of Noah are the descendants that are the continuation of the history of the humanity after the flood.

**The Call and the Question of the Reverence**

The call of Hūd is the call that is identical to the call of Noah in verse 59, and the identity of the call is the identity that is the unity of the messages of the prophets. The call is the call that is the worship of the One God: *O my people! Worship God! You have no god other than Him*. The call of Hūd adds the question that is the reverence: *Will you not be reverent?* The question of the reverence is the question that is the appeal to the reason of the people, and the appeal to the reason of the people is the appeal that is the asking of the people to be in the fear of the tremendous day. The verse, in its form, is the form of the second link of the procession, and the second link of the procession is the second link that is in the form of the introduction of the rejection of the notables of the people of ʿĀd in the next verse.

## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:66

> **The notables among his people who disbelieved said, “Truly we think that you are foolish, and we consider you to be among the liars.”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Two Charges of the Notables**

The verse states the response of the notables of the people of Hūd, and the response is the response that is in the form of the two charges: the charge of the foolishness, and the charge of the lying. The first charge is the charge of the foolishness: *Truly we think that you are foolish*. The second charge is the charge of the lying: *and we consider you to be among the liars*. The two charges are the two charges that are the attack on the character of the prophet, and the attack on the character of the prophet is the attack that is the refusal of the examination of the message.

The difference between the charge of the notables of the people of Noah and the charge of the notables of the people of Hūd is the difference that is worth stating. The notables of the people of Noah charged Noah with the error (7:60), and the notables of the people of Hūd charged Hūd with the foolishness and the lying. The three charges are the three forms of the same act: the attack on the character of the prophet, and the attack on the character of the prophet is the attack that is the removal of the question of the message to the question of the messenger. The logic of the attack is the logic that is worth stating: the notables who reject the message attack the character of the messenger, because the character of the messenger is the character that is the ground of the trust in the message. The man who attacks the character of the messenger is the man who is in the one who knows that the message cannot be refuted by the argument, and the man who knows that the message cannot be refuted by the argument is the man who is in the attack on the messenger rather than the message.

**The Notables Who Disbelieved**

The verse adds the detail that is the detail of the distinction, and the detail is the detail that is the notables who disbelieved. The notables of the people of Hūd who opposed Hūd were not the notables in general, but the notables who disbelieved, and some of the leading members of the people of Hūd were reportedly the believers in his message. The distinction is the distinction that is the teaching that the rejection of the message is not the rejection of the community, and the rejection of the community is not the rejection that is the necessity. The man who is in the belief among the notables is the man who is in the exception of the pattern of the rejection of the notables, and the exception of the pattern of the rejection of the notables is the exception that is the proof of the possibility of the acceptance of the message by the notables.

## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:67

> **He said, “O my people! There is no foolishness in me, but rather I am a messenger from the Lord of the worlds.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Denial and the Correction**

The verse is the reply of Hūd to the charge of the notables, and the reply is the reply that is in the form of the two parts, on the same form as the reply of Noah in verse 61. The first part is the denial of the charge of the foolishness: *There is no foolishness in me*. The second part is the correction by the word *bal*: *I am a messenger from the Lord of the worlds*. The two parts are the two parts that are the denial of the charge and the assertion of the mission, on the form of the teaching of the previous verse. The logic of the reply is the logic that is worth stating: the reply of the prophet is the reply that is the transfer of the authority from the self to the source, and the transfer of the authority from the self to the source is the transfer that is the declaration of the mission from the Lord of the worlds. The repetition of the same reply by the two prophets is the repetition that is the unity of the profile of the messengers, and the unity of the profile of the messengers is the unity that is the teaching of the fundamental unity of the missions of the prophets.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:68

> **I deliver unto you the messages of my Lord, and truly I am a trustworthy adviser unto you.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Delivery and the Trustworthy Advice**

The verse completes the reply of Hūd, and the reply is the reply that is in the form of the two declarations, on the same form as the reply of Noah in verse 62. The first declaration is the declaration of the duty: *I deliver unto you the messages of my Lord*. The word for "deliver" is the same word that Noah used, and the duty of the delivery is the duty that is the conveyance of the message and nothing more, on the form of the teaching of the previous verses. The second declaration is the declaration of the advice: *and truly I am a trustworthy adviser unto you*. The word for "trustworthy adviser" is *wakiʿan adīqan*, and the two words are the two words that are the sincerity of the advice and the truthfulness of the adviser. The declaration of the trustworthy advice of Hūd is the declaration that is the contrast with the false claim of the sincere advice of Satan in verse 21, on the form of the teaching of verse 62. The logic of the contrast is the logic that is worth stating: the trustworthy advice of the prophet is the trustworthy advice that ends in the benefit of the one who is advised, and the false claim of the sincere advice of Satan is the false claim that ends in the ruin of the one who is deceived, and the two claims are the two claims that are the criterion of the outcome.

## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:69

> **Or do you marvel that a reminder from your Lord should come to you by means of a man from among yourselves, so as to warn you? Remember when He made you vicegerents after the people of Noah, and increased you amply in stature. So remember the boons of God, that haply you may prosper.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Challenge of the Human Messenger Repeated**

The verse is the challenge that Hūd addresses to his people, and the challenge is the challenge that is on the same form as the challenge of Noah in verse 63: *Or do you marvel that a reminder from your Lord should come to you by means of a man from among yourselves, so as to warn you?* The repetition of the challenge by the two prophets is the repetition that is the commonness of the objection of the human messenger, and the commonness of the objection of the human messenger is the commonness that is the human tendency of the rejection of the human messenger, on the form of the teaching of verse 63.

**The Vicegerency After the People of Noah**

The challenge of Hūd adds the statement that is the history of the people of ʿĀd: *Remember when He made you vicegerents after the people of Noah, and increased you amply in stature*. The word for "vicegerents" is *khulafāʾ*, from the root *k-h-l-f*, and the root carries the sense of the succession, the inheritance, the coming after the one who was before. The people of ʿĀd are the people who were made the vicegerents after the people of Noah, and the state of the vicegerency is the state that is the succession to the sovereignty on the earth. The statement of the increase in stature is the statement that is the great size of the people of ʿĀd. The legendary accounts say that the smallest of the people of ʿĀd was sixty cubits in height, and the largest was one hundred cubits, and the two measures are the two measures that are the increase of the stature in the form of the tradition. The boons of God that Hūd reminds his people of are the boons of the succession and the sovereignty on the earth, and the boons of the great size, and the two boons are the two boons that are the favor of God that the people of ʿĀd received.

The logic of the reminder of the boons is the logic that is worth stating: the prophet who reminds the people of the boons of God is the prophet who is the appeal to the gratitude of the people, and the appeal to the gratitude of the people is the appeal that is the connection of the worship of God with the thanksgiving for the boons of God. The people who forget the boons of God are the people who are the ingratitude, and the state of the ingratitude is the state that is the worship of the boons rather than the worship of the giver of the boons. The verse, in its form, is the form of the challenge of Hūd and the reminder of the boons, and the challenge of Hūd and the reminder of the boons are the challenge of Hūd and the reminder of the boons that are in the form of the introduction of the response of the people of ʿĀd in the next verse.

## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:70

> **They said, “Have you come unto us so that we may worship God alone, and leave aside what our fathers worshipped? Then bring upon us that wherewith you have threatened us, if you are truthful.”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Two Responses of the Rejection**

The verse states the response of the people of ʿĀd to the call of Hūd, and the response is the response that is in the form of the two parts that the Qur'an records in the responses of the other communities of the disbelievers. The first part is the statement of the unwillingness to abandon the gods of the fathers: *Have you come unto us so that we may worship God alone, and leave aside what our fathers worshipped?* The second part is the challenge of the hastening of the punishment: *Then bring upon us that wherewith you have threatened us, if you are truthful*. The two parts are the two forms of the rejection, and the two forms of the rejection are the two forms that are the refusal of the message and the provocation of the warning.

**The Excuse of the Fathers**

The first response is the excuse that is the following of the fathers, and the excuse of the following of the fathers is the excuse that the Qur'an never accepts in any context. The Qur'an records the form of the excuse in the other verses, and the statement is the statement that is in the form of the confirmation of the teaching:

> *"And when it is said to them: Follow that which God has sent down, they say: Rather, we will follow that upon which we found our fathers. Or even if their fathers understood nothing, and were not guided?"* (2:170, in the form of the statement of the excuse of the fathers)

The logic of the non-acceptance of the excuse is the logic that is worth stating in full: the excuse of the fathers is the excuse that is the transfer of the responsibility from the self to the ancestor, and the transfer of the responsibility from the self to the ancestor is the transfer that is the removal of the personal responsibility. The Qur'an refutes the excuse in the form of the covenant of the pretemporal witnessing, in which all the humanity acknowledged the lordship of God before the birth of the fathers (7:172–173), and the covenant of the pretemporal witnessing is the covenant that is the proof that the worship of God is the worship that is known to the humanity before the worship of the fathers, and the worship of the fathers is the worship that is the addition to the original worship, not the replacement of the original worship. The man who follows the fathers in the worship is the man who is in the following of the addition rather than the following of the original, and the following of the addition rather than the following of the original is the following that is the error of the religion.

**The Challenge of the Hastening**

The second response is the challenge that is the seeking of the hastening of the punishment, and the seeking of the hastening of the punishment is the seeking that is the complete lack of the faith. The Qur'an records the form of the challenge in the other verses, and the statement is the statement that is in the form of the confirmation of the teaching: the same challenge is issued to Noah (11:32), and a nearly identical statement is made to Ṣāli in verse 77 of the sūrah. The Qur'an indicates the danger of the seeking of the hastening of the punishment in the other verses (10:48–50; 21:37–38; 27:71–72), and the danger of the seeking of the hastening is the danger that is the bringing of the punishment upon the one who seeks the hastening of the punishment. The verse in Sūrah Ḥāj states the form of the teaching in the same way:

> *"And they said: When will this promise be fulfilled, if you are truthful? They said: When it comes, you will see the cloud not containing any rain. And they said: This is a cloud that brings us rain. God said: It is what you were seeking to hasten — a wind within it is a painful punishment."* (46:22–24, in the form of the statement of the hastening)

The verse is the form of the confirmation of the teaching: the one who seeks the hastening of the punishment is the one who receives the punishment that he sought to hasten, and the punishment that he sought to hasten is the punishment that is the cloud of the destructive wind of the people of ʿĀd. The verse, in its form, is the form of the two responses of the rejection, and the two responses of the rejection are the two responses that are in the form of the introduction of the reply of Hūd in the next verse.

## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:71

> **He said, “Defilement and wrath have already come upon you from your Lord. Do you dispute with me over names that you have named — you and your fathers — for which God has sent down no authority? Then wait! Truly I am waiting along with you.”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Defilement That Has Already Come**

The verse is the reply of Hūd to the two responses of the people of ʿĀd, and the reply begins with the statement of the defilement: *Defilement and wrath have already come upon you from your Lord*. The word for "defilement" is *rijs*, and the word overlaps in meaning with the similar Quranic term *rijz*, which means both the filth or the defilement and the punishment. In the context of the verse, the defilement is the defilement that is the imminent punishment, and the imminent punishment is the punishment that has already been engendered by the rejection of Hūd, and that will soon be visited upon the people of ʿĀd. The statement of the "already come" is the statement that is the teaching that the punishment is not the punishment that is the future that is far, but the punishment that is the present that is near, and the present that is near is the present that is the engagement of the punishment.

**The Names That Have No Authority**

The verse then moves to the refutation of the gods of the fathers, and the refutation is the refutation that is in the form of the statement of the names: *Do you dispute with me over names that you have named — you and your fathers — for which God has sent down no authority?* The idols of the people of ʿĀd are the idols that are dismissed by Hūd as the mere names that the people and their fathers have named, and the mere names are the mere names that are the names without the authority of God. The logic of the refutation is the logic that is worth stating in full: the name of the thing is the name that is the label that the man puts upon the thing, and the label that the man puts upon the thing is the label that is the thing without the reality. The gods of the people of ʿĀd are the gods that are the names without the ontological reality, and the names without the ontological reality are the names that are the things that can bring neither harm nor benefit. The worship of the names is the worship that is the worship of the labels, and the worship of the labels is the worship that is the futility of the worship.

The Qur'an states the form of the teaching in the same way in the verse of the Yūsuf, where the prison companions ask Yūsuf about their gods, and Yūsuf replies that they worship, apart from God, nothing but names that you have named — you and your fathers — for which God has sent down no authority (12:40). And the verse in Sūrah al-Najm states the form of the names in the same way, in the statement of the names of the three idols of the Quraysh: *And indeed, the stars are but great luminaries, and they are not averters of the evil except that they are named* (53:21–23, in the form of the statement of the names). The two verses are the two forms of the same teaching: the idols are the names, and the names are the names that are the things without the power, and the things without the power are the things that are the futility of the worship.

**The Mutual Waiting**

The verse closes with the statement of the waiting: *Then wait! Truly I am waiting along with you*. The waiting of Hūd is the waiting that is the waiting for the judgment of God between the prophet and his people, and the waiting for the judgment of God is the waiting that is the confidence of the prophet in the victory of the truth. The statement of the mutual waiting is the statement that is the teaching that the prophet is not the prophet who is the fear of the people, and the prophet who is not the fear of the people is the prophet who is the waiting for the judgment of God with the confidence. The Prophet ﷺ is instructed to respond to his people in the same manner in the other verses (6:158; 9:52; 10:20, 102; 11:122; 52:31), and the response of the mutual waiting is the response that is the standing of the prophet in the face of the challenge of the people. The verse, in its form, is the form of the complete reply of Hūd, and the complete reply of Hūd is the complete reply that is in the form of the introduction of the statement of the destruction of the people of ʿĀd in the next verse.

## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:72

> **So We saved him and those who were with him through a mercy from Us, and We cut off the last remnant of those who denied Our signs and were not believers.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Mercy of the Salvation and the Cutting Off of the Remnant**

The verse states the result of the story of Hūd, and the result is the result that is in the form of the two acts, on the same form as the result of the story of Noah. The first act is the act of the salvation: *So We saved him and those who were with him through a mercy from Us*. The salvation is the salvation that is the mercy of God, and the mercy of God is the mercy that is the sparing of the prophet and his believers from the destruction. The second act is the act of the cutting off: *and We cut off the last remnant of those who denied Our signs and were not believers*. The cutting off of the last remnant is the cutting off that is the complete destruction of the people who denied the signs and were not believers, and the complete destruction of the people who denied the signs is the complete destruction that is the fulfillment of the warning of Hūd.

**The Wind of the Destruction**

The tradition gives the details of the destruction of the people of ʿĀd, and the details are the details that are worth stating. The destruction of the people of ʿĀd was the destruction by the fierce wind that destroyed all but Hūd and those who were with him (41:16; 54:19; 69:6–7). The wind of the destruction is the wind that is described in the other verse as the wind that tore out the people as if they were uprooted palm trunks (54:20). According to some of the accounts, when the people of ʿĀd rejected the call of Hūd, God punished them with the drought for three years, and a group of them went to Makkah to pray and to beseech God to relieve them, although they continued to disbelieve in Hūd and his warning. In response to their prayers, three clouds appeared, one white, one red, and one black, and a heavenly herald asked them to choose among them, and they chose the black cloud, which God then drove until it reached their town. The inhabitants of the town initially took the cloud to be the good sign and the harbinger of the life-giving rain, and the cloud soon brought down upon them the violent and destructive wind. The story of the three clouds is the story that is the choice of the people the choice of the black cloud, and the choice of the black cloud is the choice that is the choice of the destruction rather than the choice of the mercy. The logic of the teaching is the logic that is worth stating: the people who are in the choice of the black cloud are the people who are in the choice of the destruction the appearance of the mercy, and the choice of the destruction the appearance of the mercy is the choice that is the blindness of the people.

The verse, in its form, is the form of the end of the story of Hūd, and the end of the story of Hūd is the end that is in the form of the introduction of the story of Ṣāli and his people of Thamūd in the next verse.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:73

> **And unto Thamūd, their brother, Ṣāliḥ. He said, “O my people! Worship God! You have no god other than Him. There has come unto you a clear proof from your Lord. This she-camel of God is a sign unto you. Leave her to graze freely on God’s earth, and cause her no harm, lest a painful punishment seize you.”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Third Link of the Procession**

The verse is the third link in the procession of the destroyed peoples, and the link is on the same form as the previous two: *And unto Thamūd, their brother, Ṣāliḥ*. The brotherhood of the prophet with his people is the brotherhood that is the kinship and the origin, on the form of the teaching of the previous verses. Ṣāli is the prophet of the Arabian people of Thamūd, and the people of Thamūd are the people that the Islamic tradition identifies as the descendants of Noah through the son of Noah, Shem, on the same form as the people of ʿĀd. The tradition gives the full name of Ṣāli as Ṣāli ibn ʿUbayd ibn Asif ibn Māsikh ibn ʿUbayd ibn Khādir ibn Thamūd. Ṣāli is described as having been among the noblest and most well-respected members of Thamūd before his mission, and his mission began when he was still a youth. The people of Thamūd are said to have inhabited the high rocky plain known as al-Ḥijr, in the western Arabia, between the Ḥijāz and Syria, and al-Ḥijr is the title of Sūrah 15, which recounts the story of the Thamūd in the form of the short account.

**The Clear Proof and the She-Camel of God**

The call of Ṣāli is the call that is on the same form as the calls of the previous prophets, and the call is the call that is the worship of the One God. The call of Ṣāli adds the statement that is the clear proof: *There has come unto you a clear proof from your Lord*. The word for "clear proof" is *bayyina*, and the word is used throughout the Qur'an for the scriptural revelations, the prophetic warnings, and the miracles given to the prophets to convince their people. In the case of Ṣāli, the clear proof is the pregnant she-camel that he miraculously brought forth from a large rock, after his people had requested a sign confirming his prophethood. The logic of the sign is the logic that is worth stating in full: the sign is given in response to the request of the people, and the people who request the sign are the people who are the disbelief, and the people who are the disbelief are the people who are the danger of the request of the sign. The story of Ṣāli serves as one of the warnings of the Qur'an about those who treat the signs of God with disdain, and as a particular warning about the danger of asking for signs and the necessity of believing in them once they have been granted.

**The Command of the Camel**

The verse states the command concerning the she-camel, and the command is the command that is in the form of the two parts: the permission of the grazing, and the prohibition of the harm. The first part is the permission: *Leave her to graze freely on God’s earth*. The word for "graze" is *tarʿa*, from the root *t-r-ʿ*, and the root carries the sense of the eating of the grass, the feeding on the earth. The she-camel is to graze freely on the earth of God, and the earth of God is the earth that is the ownership of the earth by God. The second part is the prohibition: *and cause her no harm*. The prohibition of the harm is the prohibition that is the protection of the sign, and the protection of the sign is the protection that is the warning of the painful punishment: *lest a painful punishment seize you*. The word for "painful" is *alīm*, from the root *ʿ-l-m*, and the root carries the sense of the knowledge, and the painful punishment is the punishment that is the punishment that is known in its pain.

The tradition gives the details of the state of the she-camel, and the details are the details that are worth stating. The Thamūd were instructed to allow the she-camel sole access to the drinking well every other day, and to alternate the access to the well between themselves and the camel. The she-camel, after having drunk from the well, would produce abundant milk to supply all the Thamūd, and the abundant milk was the abundant milk that was the provision of God for the people through the sign. But the people grew resentful of sharing the well, and they complained that the enormous size of the camel tended to frighten off their other livestock. The resentment of the people is the resentment that is the refusal of the sign, and the refusal of the sign is the refusal that is the path to the destruction.

The command of the camel bears the similarities to the pagan Arab practices of allowing certain camels to roam and graze without interference, while forbidding their use or slaughter, and the practice of the pagan Arabs is the practice that the Qur'an criticizes for the arbitrary manner in which the sacrosanct nature of such animals was assigned to them by the pagan Arabs themselves (5:103; 6:138). The difference between the two cases is the difference that is worth stating: in the case of the pagan Arabs, the sacrosanctity of the animal is the sacrosanctity that is the arbitrary assignment of the people, and the arbitrary assignment of the people is the arbitrary assignment that is the religion of the invention. In the case of the she-camel of Ṣāli, the sacrosanctity of the animal is the sacrosanctity that is the sign of God, and the sign of God is the sign that is the authority of God. The two cases are the two forms of the same teaching: the sacrosanctity of the thing is the sacrosanctity that is the authority of the one who makes the thing sacrosanct, and the authority of the one who makes the thing sacrosanct is the authority of God, not the authority of the people. The verse, in its form, is the form of the call of Ṣāli and the command of the camel, and the call of Ṣāli and the command of the camel are the call of Ṣāli and the command of the camel that are in the form of the introduction of the reminder of the boons of the next verse.
