## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:74

> **Remember when He made you vicegerents after ʿĀd and settled you on the earth: you build castles for yourselves on the open plain and hew dwellings in the mountains. So remember the boons of God, and behave not wickedly upon the earth, working corruption.”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Succession of the Peoples**

The verse is the reminder that Ṣāli addresses to his people, and the reminder is the reminder that is the succession: *Remember when He made you vicegerents after ʿĀd*. The word for "vicegerents" is the same word that Hūd used for his people in verse 69, and the word is the word that carries the sense of the succession and the inheritance. The chain of the succession is the chain that is the people of Noah, then the people of ʿĀd, then the people of Thamūd, and the chain of the succession is the chain that is the history of the sovereignty of the peoples on the earth. The logic of the reminder is the logic that is worth stating: the prophet who reminds the people of the succession is the prophet who is the appeal to the consciousness of the people of the position that they hold, and the position that they hold is the position that is the sovereignty that was given to them after the destruction of the people before them. The people who hold the position of the sovereignty are the people who are the ones who have seen the fate of the people before them, and the people who have seen the fate of the people before them are the people who are the ones who know the cost of the rejection of the prophet.

**The Castles and the Dwellings**

The verse then states the details of the settlement of the people of Thamūd, and the details are the details that are the building: *you build castles for yourselves on the open plain and hew dwellings in the mountains*. The two acts of the building are the two acts that are the famous building ability of the Thamūd, and the building ability of the Thamūd is the building ability that is the construction of the dwellings in the mountains, the vestiges of which were known to the Arabs of the time of the Prophet ﷺ, and which remain to the present day. The dwellings of the Thamūd in the mountains are the dwellings that are the rock-cut architecture of al-Ḥijr, and the rock-cut architecture of al-Ḥijr is the rock-cut architecture that is the visible evidence of the civilization of the Thamūd.

The tradition gives the detail that is the detail of the connection of the dwellings of the Thamūd with the life of the Prophet ﷺ, and the detail is the detail that is worth stating. The Prophet ﷺ and his army stopped near the remains of the mountain abodes of the Thamūd on their way to the anticipated military campaign of Tabūk in the year 9 of the Hijra. The Prophet ﷺ forbade his followers from drinking or using the water from the wells of the Thamūd, and he would not allow them to approach the well used by the sacred she-camel, lest they suffer the same fate as the Thamūd. The prohibition of the Prophet ﷺ is the prohibition that is the teaching that the place of the destruction of the people is the place that is the place that is to be avoided, and the place that is to be avoided is the place that is the reminder of the fate of the people.

**The Boons and the Non-Corruption**

The verse closes with the command that is the two parts: the remembrance of the boons, and the non-corruption of the earth. The first part is the command of the remembrance: *So remember the boons of God*. The word for "boons" is *niʿam*, the plural of *niʿma*, and the boons of the verse are the boons of the succession and the sovereignty, and the boons of the settlement on the earth, and the boons of the building ability. The command of the remembrance of the boons is the command that is the appeal to the gratitude of the people, on the form of the teaching of the previous verses. The second part is the command of the non-corruption: *and behave not wickedly upon the earth, working corruption*. The command of the non-corruption is the same command that the sūrah used in verse 56, and the repetition of the command is the repetition that is the importance of the command, and the importance of the command of the non-corruption of the earth is the importance that is the central position of the command in the teaching of the sūrah. The logic of the two commands is the logic that is worth stating: the remembrance of the boons is the remembrance that is the cause of the non-corruption, and the non-corruption is the non-corruption that is the fruit of the remembrance of the boons. The verse, in its form, is the form of the reminder of Ṣāli, and the reminder of Ṣāli is the reminder that is in the form of the introduction of the statement of the division of the people in the next verse.

## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:75

> **The notables among his people who were arrogant said to those among them who believed and whom they deemed weak, “Do you know that Ṣāliḥ has been sent by his Lord?” They said, “Truly we believe in that wherewith he has been sent.”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Arrogant and the Weak**

The verse states the division of the people of Thamūd, and the division is the division that is the two groups: the notables who were arrogant, and the ones who believed and whom they deemed weak. The two groups are the two groups that are the social structure of the rejection, and the social structure of the rejection is the social structure that is the arrogance of the notables and the weakness of the believers. The word for "arrogant" is *al-mustaʿbibūn*, from the root *ʿ-b-b*, and the root carries the sense of the arrogance, the taking of the greatness for oneself. The word for "weak" is the word that the sūrah uses for the ones who are considered to be of the lower social and economic standing, and the lower social and economic standing of the believers is the lower social and economic standing that is the weakness of the believers in the eyes of the notables.

The pattern of the division is the pattern that is the punishment narratives of the Qur'an: the notables are the notables who reject the prophet, and the believers are the believers who are the weak of the people. The reports indicate that some of the leading figures among the Thamūd believed in the message of Ṣāli, but they were overwhelmed by the others, including the keeper of their idols, who strongly rejected Ṣāli. The state of the leading believers who were overwhelmed is the state that is the teaching that the belief is not the belief that is the privilege of the notables, and the belief is the belief that is the state of the weak as well as the state of the notables.

**The Question That Is the Taunt**

The verse states the act of the arrogant notables, and the act is the act that is the question: *Do you know that Ṣāliḥ has been sent by his Lord?* The word for "sent" is the word that carries the sense of the authority, the authority from the Lord. The question of the notables is the question that is the taunt rather than the question, and the taunt is the taunt that is the attempt of the notables to undermine the faith of the weak. The logic of the taunt is the logic that is worth stating: the notables who cannot refute the message by the argument attack the faith of the weak by the taunt, and the taunt is the taunt that is the question that is in the condition of the one who is in the form of the undermining of the confidence of the weak in the truth of the message. The man who is in the taunt of the notables is the man who is in the one who is in the form of the attack on the ground of the social standing rather than the ground of the truth.

**The Answer of the Weak**

The verse closes with the answer of the weak, and the answer is the answer that is the confession of the faith: *Truly we believe in that wherewith he has been sent*. The answer of the weak is the answer that is the faith in the message rather than the faith in the messenger, and the faith in the message rather than the faith in the messenger is the faith that is the attachment to the source of the message. The logic of the answer is the logic that is worth stating: the weak who believe are the weak who are the faith that is in the condition of the one who is in the form of the strength of the faith in the weakness of the social standing, and the strength of the faith in the weakness of the social standing is the strength that is the teaching that the faith is the faith that is the strength of the heart rather than the strength of the social standing. The verse, in its form, is the form of the statement of the division of the people and the answer of the weak, and the statement of the division of the people and the answer of the weak are the statement of the division of the people and the answer of the weak that are in the form of the introduction of the response of the arrogant in the next verse.

## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:76

> **Those who were arrogant said, “Truly we believe not in that which you believe.”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Confession of the Division**

The verse is the response of the arrogant notables to the answer of the weak, and the response is the response that is the confession of the division: *Truly we believe not in that which you believe*. The statement of the notables is the statement that is the denial of the faith of the weak, and the denial of the faith of the weak is the denial that is the confirmation of the division of the people into the two groups.

The logic of the statement is the logic that is worth stating: the notables who are in the arrogance are the notables who are in the ones who cannot deny the faith of the weak in the form of the argument, and the notables who cannot deny the faith of the weak in the form of the argument are the notables who are in the denial of the faith of the weak in the form of the statement of the state. The statement of the state is the statement that is the "we do not believe in that which you believe," and the "we do not believe in that which you believe" is the statement that is the separation of the group from the group, and the separation of the group from the group is the separation that is the public confirmation of the division. The man who is in the statement of the division is the man who is in the one who makes the division of the people the state of the public, and the state of the public of the division is the state that is the beginning of the collective guilt of the group.

The verse, in its short form, is the form of the statement of the division of the people of Thamūd, and the statement of the division is the statement that is in the form of the introduction of the statement of the collective act of the people in the next verse, in which the people of Thamūd, in their collective form, hamstring the she-camel of God.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:77

> **So they hamstrung the she-camel and insolently defied the Command of their Lord. And they said, “O Ṣāli! Bring upon us that wherewith you have threatened us, if you are among those sent [by God].”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Hamstringing of the She-Camel**

The verse states the act of the people of Thamūd that is the act that is the culmination of the story: *So they hamstrung the she-camel and insolently defied the Command of their Lord*. The word for "hamstrung" is the word that carries the sense of the cutting of the sinews of the legs of the camel, and the hamstringing of the she-camel is the hamstringing that is the killing of the sign. The act of the hamstringing is the act that is the defiance of the command of God, and the defiance of the command of God is the defiance that is the "insolently" in the verse.

The commentators report that, despite the division of the people regarding the message of Ṣāli, all the Thamūd eventually consented to the plot to hamstring and kill the she-camel, and the collective consent to the plot is the collective consent that is the collective guilt and the liability to the divine punishment. The account gives the detail of the instigation of the killing, and the detail is the detail that is the two women, one of whom was the wife of Dhiʾāb ibn ʿAmr, one of the leading disbelievers among the Thamūd. She is said to have recruited a young man, who in turn recruited eight others, to carry out the slaughter, and the group of nine persons is the group that is referred to in the other verse as the group of nine who worked corruption among the Thamūd (27:48). After killing the she-camel, the young offspring of the camel ran off in distress and eventually disappeared, and the disappearance of the offspring is the disappearance that is the visible sign of the loss of the life of the camel.

**The Logic of the Sign That Is Killed**

The logic of the act of the hamstringing is the logic that is worth stating in full. The she-camel is the sign that the people requested, and the people who requested the sign are the people who are the ones who are given the sign the opportunity of the faith. The people who kill the sign that they requested are the people who are the destruction of the means of their own salvation, and the destruction of the means of their own salvation is the destruction that is the choice of the destruction rather than the choice of the faith. The logic of the teaching is the logic that is in the form of the warning of the sūrah: the man who is in the request of the sign is the man who is in the one who is bound to believe in the sign once it is granted, and the man who is not bound to believe in the sign is the man who is in the one who is in the form of the danger of the request of the sign. The sign that is refused is the sign that becomes the cause of the destruction, and the sign that is accepted is the sign that becomes the cause of the salvation.

**The Challenge of the Punishment**

The verse closes with the challenge of the people of Thamūd to Ṣāli, and the challenge is the challenge that is on the same form as the challenges of the people of Noah and the people of Hūd: *O Ṣāliḥ! Bring upon us that wherewith you have threatened us, if you are among those sent [by God]*. The challenge of the people of Thamūd is the challenge that is the seeking of the hastening of the punishment, and the seeking of the hastening of the punishment is the seeking that is the complete lack of the faith, on the form of the teaching of verse 70. The condition of the challenge is the condition that is the "if you are among those sent," and the "if you are among those sent" is the condition that is the doubt of the people about the mission of the prophet after the complete reply of the prophet and the giving of the sign. The man who doubts the mission of the prophet after the giving of the sign is the man who is in the one who has no ground for the doubt, and the man who has no ground for the doubt is the man who is in the one who is in the hardness of the heart.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:78

> **So the earthquake seized them, and morning found them lying lifeless in their abode.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Earthquake and the Sound**

The verse states the destruction of the people of Thamūd, and the destruction is the destruction that is the earthquake: *So the earthquake seized them*. The word for "earthquake" is *rajfah*, from the root *r-j-f*, and the root carries the sense of the shaking, the trembling, the shaking of the earth. The tradition gives the details of the destruction, and the details are the details that are worth stating. The destructive event was said to have entailed not only the violent shaking, but also a loud sound, like the crack of lightning accompanied by the cry of all living things on earth, which stopped the hearts of the people instantly in their breasts. The two forms of the destruction are the two forms that are the shaking of the earth and the stopping of the hearts, and the shaking of the earth and the stopping of the hearts are the shaking of the earth and the stopping of the hearts that are the destruction of the body and the destruction of the life.

**The Warnings of the Three Days**

The tradition gives the detail of the warnings that preceded the destruction, and the detail is the detail that is the three days. After killing the she-camel, Ṣāli realized that the punishment of which he had been warning was imminent, and he told his people that it would be visited upon them in three days, and that on each successive morning until that time, they would wake with their faces having turned yellow, then red, and finally black before their final destruction. The three colors of the faces are the three colors that are the visible sign of the approaching of the destruction, and the visible sign of the approaching of the destruction is the visible sign that is the last warning of the prophet to his people. The people who are in the three days of the warning are the people who are in the ones who are given the three days of the opportunity of the repentance, and the three days of the opportunity of the repentance are the three days that are the refusal of the repentance even in the hour of the visible sign of the destruction.

**The Morning That Found Them Lifeless**

The verse closes with the statement of the result, and the result is the result that is the morning: *and morning found them lying lifeless in their abode*. The morning is the morning that is the third morning, and the third morning is the third morning that is the morning of the blackened faces, and the morning of the blackened faces is the morning that is the morning of the destruction. The people who are found lifeless in their abode are the people who are the ones who laid themselves out on the floor in expectation of their demise, and the laying out on the floor in expectation of the demise is the laying out that is the preparation of the people for the destruction. The logic of the teaching is the logic that is worth stating: the people who are found lifeless in their abode are the people who are the ones who were in the acknowledgment of the certainty of the destruction, and the acknowledgment of the certainty of the destruction is the acknowledgment that is the confession that is too late, on the form of the teaching of verse 5. The same fate is said to have befallen the people of Midian in the other verses of the sūrah (7:91; 11:94; 29:37), and the same fate of the people of Midian is the same fate that is the repetition of the pattern of the destruction of the peoples of the prophets. The verse, in its form, is the form of the statement of the destruction of the people of Thamūd, and the statement of the destruction is the statement that is in the form of the introduction of the statement of the turning away of Ṣāli in the next verse.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:79

> **So he turned away from them and said, “O my people! I indeed delivered unto you the message of my Lord, and advised you sincerely, but you love not sincere advisers.”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Turning Away**

The verse is the act of Ṣāli after the destruction of his people, and the act is the act that is the turning away: *So he turned away from them*. The commentators gave the two readings of the turning away, and the two readings are the two readings that are worth stating. The first reading is the reading that the turning away is the abandonment of the Thamūd in advance of their punishment, leaving their settlement along with a small number of believers. The second reading is the reading that the turning away is the reaction of Ṣāli to the destruction of his people, either when he turned back and saw the smoke rising from their residences, or after returning to find them lying lifeless in their abode. The two readings are the two forms of the same teaching: the prophet who turns away from his people is the prophet who is the completion of the duty of the delivery and the advice, and the completion of the duty of the delivery and the advice is the completion that is the leaving of the people to the result of their choice.

**The Two Essential Vocations**

The verse states the two declarations of Ṣāli, and the two declarations are the two declarations that are on the same form as the declarations of the previous prophets: *I indeed delivered unto you the message of my Lord, and advised you sincerely*. The two declarations are the two essential vocations of the prophets of the Qur'an: the delivery of the message, and the sincere advice. The repetition of the two declarations by the three prophets of the section (Noah, Hūd, Ṣāli) is the repetition that is the unity of the profile of the messengers, on the form of the teaching of the previous verses.

The difference between the statement of Ṣāli and the statements of the previous prophets is the difference that is worth stating. Noah and Hūd make their statements in the mood of the warning, and Ṣāli says his statement with the sadness and the regret that, for all his efforts, he could not persuade his people to mend their ways. The sadness and the regret of Ṣāli are the sadness and the regret that are the love of the prophet for his people, and the love of the prophet for his people is the love that is the grief of the prophet for the loss of the people. The other reading is the reading that Ṣāli says his statement by way of the posthumous chastisement of his people for their stubborn disbelief, and the posthumous chastisement is the chastisement that is the address of the prophet to the dead. The report that the Prophet ﷺ addressed the dead at the Battle of Badr, asking them if they had found the promise of God to be true, and the reply of the Prophet ﷺ to the surprise of ʿUmar, that the dead can hear as well as the living even if they cannot respond, is the report that supports the possibility of the address of the prophet to the dead.

**The People Who Do Not Love the Sincere Advisers**

The verse closes with the statement of the state of the people, and the state is the state that is in the form of the hatred of the sincere advisers: *but you love not sincere advisers*. The logic of the statement is the logic that is worth stating in full. The sincere adviser is the sincere adviser who is the one who tells the truth to the people, and the one who tells the truth to the people is the one who is the one who is hated by the people who do not want the truth. The people who do not love the sincere advisers are the people who are the ones who prefer the one who confirms them in their state to the one who calls them to the change of their state, and the preference of the one who confirms to the one who calls is the preference that is the psychology of the hatred of the advice. The logic of the psychology of the hatred of the advice is the logic that is worth stating: the advice is the advice that is the mirror that shows the state of the one who is advised, and the mirror that shows the state of the one who is advised is the mirror that is the thing that is hated by the one who does not want to see his own state. The man who does not love the sincere adviser is the man who is in the one who does not want to see his own state, and the man who does not want to see his own state is the man who is in the one who is in the form of the hardness of the heart. The verse, in its form, is the form of the end of the story of Ṣāli, and the end of the story of Ṣāli is the end that is in the form of the introduction of the story of Lūt and his people in the next verse.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:80

> **And Lot, when he said to his people, “What! Do you commit an indecency such as none in the world committed before you?**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Fourth Link of the Procession**

The verse is the fourth link in the procession of the destroyed peoples, and the link is on the same form as the previous links: the prophet of the people, and the call of the prophet, and the rejection of the people, and the destruction of the people. The prophet of the fourth link is Lūt, and Lūt is the nephew of Abraham, and his people are the people of Sodom. Lūt reportedly settled in Sodom, but was unrelated to the native inhabitants of the town. The tradition gives the full name of Lūt as Lūṭ ibn Hārān ibn Āzar, and the name Hārān is the name that is identified with the Biblical name Terah, the father of Abraham. Another report identifies Lūt as the maternal cousin of Abraham, and as the brother of the wife of Abraham, Sārā. The other narrative accounts of Lūt and his people are in 11:77–83, 15:57–77, 26:160–73, 27:54–58, 29:28–35, 37:133–38, and 54:33–38, and the similar Biblical narrative is in Genesis 19. Lūt is said to have become a believer along with Abraham and to have traveled with him to Syria, and while Abraham went on to Palestine, Lūt turned toward the Jordan. He is said to have been sent by God as a messenger to Sodom and the surrounding towns, to call them to the worship of the One God, and to abandon their sinful and prodigal behavior.

**The Astonishment of the Prophet**

The call of Lūt opens with the rhetorical question that is in the form of the state of the astonishment of the prophet: *What! Do you commit an indecency such as none in the world committed before you?* The word for "indecency" is the same root that the sūrah used in verse 28 for the act of the wrongdoer, and the word for "What!" is the word that carries the sense of the astonishment, the wonder, the marvel at the thing. The astonishment of Lūt is the astonishment that is the sin that is unprecedented in the world, and the sin that is unprecedented in the world is the sin that is the invention of the people of Sodom rather than the inheritance of the people before them. The logic of the astonishment is the logic that is worth stating: the prophet who is astonished at the sin of his people is the prophet who is the one who knows the state of the humanity before the sin, and the prophet who knows the state of the humanity before the sin is the prophet who is the one who knows that the sin is the sin that is the new invention of the people. The statement of the unprecedented nature of the sin is the statement that is the removal of the excuse of the fathers, on the form of the teaching of verse 28 and verse 70: the people of Sodom cannot claim the excuse of the following of the fathers, because the sin is the sin that none of their fathers committed, and the sin that none of their fathers committed is the sin that is the pure invention of the people.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:81

> **Verily you come with desire unto men instead of women. Indeed, you are a prodigal people!”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Sin That Is Named**

The verse names the sin of the people of Sodom, and the naming is the naming that is the act: *Verily you come with desire unto men instead of women*. The traditional commentators understood the act as the practice of homosexuality, and some specified the act of the sodomy. The verse indicates that the practice originated with the people of Sodom in the time of Lūt, and the origin of the practice with the people of Sodom is the origin that is the confirmation of the statement of the unprecedented nature of the sin in the previous verse. The account of the Qur'an in the other verses (11:77–80; 15:61–70) gives the detail of the aggressive behavior of the men of Sodom, and the detail of the aggressive behavior is the detail that is the seeking of the desire in the form of the violence. The report indicates that the people of Sodom engaged in the practice only with those who were the outsiders in their town, and the report is consistent with the fact that the people of Sodom demanded access to the angels who had visited Lūt, whom they clearly perceived as the foreigners. The other commentators suggest that the men of Sodom preferred the act on a regular basis, and the two reports are the two forms of the same teaching: the sin of the people of Sodom is the sin that is the act that is against the order of the creation.

**The Prodigality That Is the Root of the Evil**

The verse closes with the statement of the state of the people, and the state is the state that is in the form of the prodigality: *Indeed, you are a prodigal people*. The word for "prodigal" is *musrifīn*, from the root *s-r-f*, and the root is the same root that the sūrah used in verse 31 for the prohibition of the excess in the eating and the drinking. The prodigality of the people of Sodom is the prodigality that is understood to mean the transgression of the limits of what is lawful to enjoy, by having the act that is against the order of the creation rather than the act that is lawful for them, namely the women who were made lawful for them. The commentary of al-Zamakhsharī describes the prodigality as the root of all the evil, and the root of all the evil is the root that is the transgression of the limits of the things, and the transgression of the limits of the things is the transgression that is the excess in the worship and the excess in the act and the excess in the enjoyment. The two verses of the sūrah (7:31, 7:81) are the two forms of the same teaching: the prohibition of the excess in the eating and the drinking, and the prohibition of the excess in the act of the desire, and the two prohibitions are the two forms of the keeping of the balance in the life of the man. The verse, in its form, is the form of the naming of the sin of the people of Sodom, and the naming of the sin is the naming that is in the form of the introduction of the reply of the people in the next verse.

## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:82

> **And the reply of his people was but to say, “Expel them from your town! Truly they are a people who keep themselves pure.”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Reply of the Expulsion**

The verse states the reply of the people of Sodom to the call of Lūt, and the reply is the reply that is the demand of the expulsion: *Expel them from your town*. The "them" of the verse is the family of Lūt, and the threat or plan to expel them extended, according to some of the commentators, to a group of the believing followers of Lūt as well. The demand of the expulsion is the demand that is the rejection of the presence of the pure in the town, and the rejection of the presence of the pure in the town is the rejection that is the teaching that the town of the sin is the town that cannot bear the presence of the purity.

**The Purity That Is Made the Charge**

The verse closes with the statement that is the form of the reversal of the teaching: *Truly they are a people who keep themselves pure*. The "purity" of Lūt and his followers is the purity that is the refusal to participate in the sexual practices of the people of Sodom, on the report of some of the commentators. The people of Sodom use the word of the purity as the charge, and the use of the word of the purity as the charge is the use that is the reversal of the value. The logic of the reversal is the logic that is worth stating in full: the society of the sin is the society that is the making of the virtue into the offense, and the making of the virtue into the offense is the making that is the teaching that the state of the society is the state that is in the form of the measure of the value of the acts. The man who is in the purity in the society of the sin is the man who is in the one who is marked by the society of the sin, and the man who is marked by the society of the sin is the man who is in the one who is expelled from the society of the sin. The modern form of the reversal is the modern form of the statement of the society that mocks the one who is clean, and the society that mocks the one who is clean is the society that is the state of the people of Sodom. The verse, in its form, is the form of the reply of the people of Sodom, and the reply of the people of Sodom is the reply that is in the form of the introduction of the statement of the salvation of Lūt in the next verse.

## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:83

> **So We saved him and his family, except for his wife; she was among those who lagged behind.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Salvation of the Family**

The verse states the result of the story of Lūt, and the result is the result that is on the same form as the results of the previous stories: *So We saved him and his family*. The salvation of Lūt and his family is the salvation that is the mercy of God, and the family of Lūt is widely understood as including the believers who followed Lūt as well as his immediate family. The pattern of the salvation of the prophets and their believers from the destruction of their people is the pattern that is the constant rule of the punishment narratives of the Qur'an, on the form of the teaching of verse 64.

**The Wife Who Lagged Behind**

The verse adds the exception that is the exception that is the wife of Lūt: *except for his wife; she was among those who lagged behind*. The word for "lagged behind" is *qāʿidat*, from the root *q-w-d*, and the root carries the sense of the remaining, the sitting, the one who remains while the others go. The wife of Lūt is the wife who is the one who followed the religion of Lūt at least outwardly, but who was inwardly a disbeliever. The outward following and the inward disbelief of the wife of Lūt are the outward following and the inward disbelief that are the teaching that the name of the family is not the name that saves, and the name of the family is not the name that saves is the teaching that is the individual responsibility in the state of the family. The logic of the teaching is the logic that is worth stating: the man who is in the family of the prophet is the man who is in the one who is not saved by the family, and the man who is not saved by the family is the man who is in the one who is saved by his own faith. The wife of Lūt is the wife who is the one who was in the family of the prophet and who was not in the state of the faith, and the wife who was in the family of the prophet and who was not in the state of the faith is the wife who lagged behind, and the lagging behind is the lagging behind that is the remaining in the town of the destruction.

The Biblical account gives the detail of the looking back of the wife of Lūt, and the detail is the detail that is the turning to look back and becoming the pillar of the salt. A similar version of the events is mentioned by some of the commentators, and is suggested in the other verse of the Qur'an, where Lūt is commanded to set out with his family during the night, and to follow behind them, and to let none of them turn around (15:65). The command of the non-turning around is the command that is the teaching that the one who is saved is the one who does not turn around to the state that was destroyed, and the one who turns around is the one who is the attachment to the state that was destroyed. The verse, in its form, is the form of the statement of the salvation of Lūt and the exception of the wife, and the statement of the salvation of Lūt and the exception of the wife are the statement of the salvation of Lūt and the exception of the wife that are in the form of the introduction of the statement of the destruction of the next verse.

## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:84

> **And We sent down a rain upon them; so behold how the guilty fared in the end.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Rain of the Stones**

The verse states the destruction of the people of Sodom, and the destruction is the destruction that is the rain: *And We sent down a rain upon them*. The commentators described the rain as the rain of the stones, and the rain of the stones is the rain that is the stones of the baked clay, on the statement of the other verses (11:82; 15:74; 26:173; 27:58). The other verses describe the destruction in the forms of the torment from the heaven (29:34), and the torrent of the stones (54:34), and the two forms of the destruction are the two forms that are the same event. The logic of the image of the rain of the stones is the logic that is worth stating: the rain is the rain that is the mercy of God, on the form of the teaching of verse 57, and the rain of the stones is the rain that is the punishment of God, and the rain of the stones is the rain that is the reversal of the mercy into the punishment, and the reversal of the mercy into the punishment is the reversal that is the state of the people who turned from the mercy to the punishment.

**The Command of the Beholding**

The verse closes with the command that is repeated in the sūrah and in the other sūrahs: *so behold how the guilty fared in the end*. The word for "guilty" is *al-mujrimīn*, from the root *j-r-m*, and the root carries the sense of the crime, the guilt, the one who commits the crime. The command of the beholding is the command that is often preceded by the mention of the journeying upon the earth, and the journeying upon the earth is the journeying that is the seeing of the ruins of the civilizations of the earlier peoples. The Arabs of the time of the Prophet ﷺ were able not only to reflect on the stories of these earlier peoples, but in some cases could see for themselves the ruins of their civilizations that remained in parts of Arabia. The command of the beholding is addressed to the Prophet ﷺ, enjoining him to reflect on the fate of those who had rejected their messengers, even as he faced the rejection by many of his own people, and the reflection on the fate of the rejected peoples is the reflection that is the warning of the people of Makkah that they could expect the same fate, should they persist in the denial of the prophethood of the Prophet ﷺ. The verse, in its form, is the form of the end of the story of Lūt, and the end of the story of Lūt is the end that is in the form of the introduction of the story of Shuʿayb and his people of Midian in the next verse.

## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:85

> **And unto Midian, their brother, Shuʿayb. He said, “O my people! Worship God! You have no god other than Him. There has come unto you a clear proof from your Lord. So observe fully the measure and the balance and diminish not people’s goods, nor work corruption upon the earth after it has been set aright. That is better for you, if you are believers.”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Fifth Link of the Procession**

The verse is the fifth link in the procession of the destroyed peoples, and the link is on the same form as the previous links. The tradition gives the full name of Shuʿayb as Shuʿayb ibn Mīkīl ibn Yashjar, on the report of the early historian Ibn Isḥāq, and the other reports identify him as Shuʿayb ibn Tawbah ibn Midian. Shuʿayb is referred to in the Islamic tradition as the "Orator of the Prophets," *khaṭīb al-anbiyāʾ*, because of the eloquence and the rhetorical power of his preaching. He is considered an Arab prophet, although the people of Midian are known in the Bible, and Shuʿayb is usually identified with the Biblical figure Jethro, the father-in-law of Moses, since the Qur'an mentions that Moses met his future wife and his future father-in-law in the area of Midian (26:22–23). The people of Midian are said to have resided in the northwestern Arabia, near Maʿān in Jordan, and to have descended from Midian, a son of Abraham, on the report of the verse of Genesis. Midian is said to have married the daughter of Lot, thus making the Midianites the descendants of both Lot and Abraham. According to some of the commentators, Shuʿayb was sent to both the people of Midian and the people of al-Aykah, "the Thicket," while the others consider the two to be the references to the same people. The other narrative accounts of Shuʿayb are in 11:84–95 and 26:176–89.

**The Measure and the Balance**

The call of Shuʿayb is the call that is on the same form as the calls of the previous prophets, in the statement of the worship of the One God and the statement of the clear proof from God. The call of Shuʿayb adds the injunction that is the particularity of the mission of Shuʿayb among the prophets of the Qur'an: *So observe fully the measure and the balance and diminish not people’s goods*. The injunction to observe fully the measure and the balance is the call to the integrity and the honesty in the commercial transactions, and by extension in the other dealings, and the injunction is also found in the other passages (6:151–52; 17:35; 18:1–3; 83:1–4). The statement of the non-diminishing of the goods of the people is the statement that they should render to the people in full the goods for which they had paid. Among the prophets of the Qur'an, it is Shuʿayb whose mission is particularly associated with the call to avoid the fraudulent commercial practices, and the identical call is in the other verses, where he addresses the people of Midian and the people of al-Aykah. In the Bible and in the Biblical commentaries, the Midianites were considered to be the merchants who worked along the incense routes, and the idea is consistent with the particular call of Shuʿayb to them.

The idea of the measure and the balance is the prominent theme of the Qur'an, having not only the commercial but also the cosmological significance. Several passages indicate that God measures out all the created existence and sets the created order in balance (13:8; 15:19–21; 23:18; 25:2; 42:27; 54:49; 65:3; 73:20). In some of the passages, the relationship is suggested between the measuring and the balancing of God, and the moral requirement that the human beings observe the proper measure and the balance (55:7–9). The logic of the teaching is the logic that is worth stating in full: the measure of the transaction is the measure that is in the form of the image of the measure of the creation, and the measure of the creation is the measure that is the balance that God set in the created order. The man who observes the measure in the transaction is the man who is in the correspondence to the balance of the creation, and the man who diminishes the measure in the transaction is the man who is in the disturbance of the balance of the creation the scale of the transaction. The tawḥīd of the call of Shuʿayb and the justice of the call of the measure are the two parts of the same teaching: the worship of the One God and the honesty in the dealing with the goods of the people are the two forms of the obedience to the order of God.

**The Non-Corruption of the Earth That Is Set Aright**

The call of Shuʿayb closes with the command of the non-corruption of the earth, and the command is the same command that the sūrah used in verse 56 and verse 74. The phrase "after it has been set aright" of the verse is the phrase that is worth stating: the setting aright of the earth indicates not only that God established the balance and the harmony in the natural order of the earth, but also that He provided the means of the setting of the matters aright in the human society, through His prophets and the commands and the prohibitions that they establish. The earth that is set aright is the earth that is the natural order and the social order, and the man who works the corruption in the earth that is set aright is the man who is in the disturbance of the two orders. The statement of the "better for you, if you are believers" is the statement that is the connection of the call of the measure with the faith, and the connection of the call of the measure with the faith is the connection that is the teaching that the call of the measure is the call of the faith the practice of the dealing. The verse, in its form, is the form of the call of Shuʿayb, and the call of Shuʿayb is the call that is in the form of the introduction of the continuation of the call in the next verse.

## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:86

> **And do not lie in wait on every path, threatening and turning away those who believe in Him from the way of God, and seeking to make it crooked. And remember when you were few, and He made you many. And behold how the workers of corruption fared in the end!**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Lying in Wait on Every Path**

The verse continues the call of Shuʿayb, and the continuation is the continuation that is the prohibition of the lying in wait: *And do not lie in wait on every path, threatening and turning away those who believe in Him from the way of God, and seeking to make it crooked*. The word for "path" is *ṣirāṭ*, and the word in the Qur'an refers to the path to the truth, and serves as the symbol for the life lived according to the divine guidance (1:6–7). The path of the verse is essentially synonymous with the way of God, *sabīl Allāh*, mentioned in the same verse. Although the commentators assert that the path to God is the single "straight path," *ṣirāṭ mustaqīm*, the expression "on every path" of the verse seems to suggest that there is more than one straight path. The explanation of al-Zamakhsharī is the explanation that is the path that is one, and that has the many branches, and it is this path with its branches to which the reference is made in the verse.

The course of action of the verse is the course of action that is the program of Satan, and the program of Satan is the program that he declared after his expulsion from the divine presence in verse 16, and the program of Satan is the program of the turning of the people from the way of God. The people of Midian are the people who follow the program of Satan the turning of the people from the way of God. The specific case of the people of Midian may mean that they turned the people away from the right path by denouncing Shuʿayb as a liar, or by threatening or otherwise hindering those who came along the road seeking to become the followers of Shuʿayb. The lying in wait may also mean that they inhibited the people journeying along the physical road by blocking it or collecting the tithes, perhaps in an exploitative or threatening manner, given their apparent penchant for the unethical commercial practices and their reported location along the important trade routes.

**The Boon of the Multiplication**

The verse then states the reminder of the boon, and the reminder is the reminder that is the multiplication: *And remember when you were few, and He made you many*. The logic of the reminder is the logic that is on the form of the teaching of the previous verses: the prophet who reminds the people of the boon that God gave them is the prophet who appeals to the gratitude of the people. The boon of the multiplication is the boon that is the increase of the number of the people, and the increase of the number of the people is the increase that is the strength of the people and the extension of the life of the people. The logic of the teaching is the logic that is worth stating: the people who are made many by God are the people who are in the ones who are bound to the gratitude for the multiplication, and the gratitude for the multiplication is the gratitude that is the obedience to the command of the one who multiplied them.

**The Command of the Beholding**

The verse closes with the command that is the beholding: *And behold how the workers of corruption fared in the end*. The command of the beholding is the same command that the sūrah used in verse 84, and the repetition of the command is the repetition that is the importance of the command, on the form of the teaching of the previous verses. The word for "workers of corruption" is the word that carries the same root as the root of the corruption of the earth in the other verses of the sūrah, and the workers of corruption are the workers of corruption that are the people of the destroyed towns. The verse, in its form, is the form of the continuation of the call of Shuʿayb, and the continuation of the call of Shuʿayb is the continuation that is in the form of the introduction of the statement of the division of the people and the patience of the prophet in the next verse.

## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:87

> **If a group of you believe in that wherewith I have been sent, and a group of you believe not, then be patient till God shall judge between us, and He is the best of judges.”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Division of the People**

The verse is the continuation of the speech of Shuʿayb, and the continuation is the continuation that is the statement of the division of the people of Midian: *If a group of you believe in that wherewith I have been sent, and a group of you believe not*. The word for "group" is the word that carries the sense of the party, the group, the division of the people into the two groups. The statement of the division is the statement that is on the form of the teaching of the previous verses: the people of the prophet are divided into the believers and the disbelievers, and the division of the people into the believers and the disbelievers is the division that is the constant state of the towns of the prophets.

**The Patience Until the Judgment**

The verse states the command of the patience, and the command is the command that is the waiting for the judgment of God: *then be patient till God shall judge between us*. The word for "patient" is *ṣbirū*, from the root *ṣ-b-r*, and the root carries the sense of the patience, the endurance, the holding of the state in the face of the contrary of the state. The command of the patience of Shuʿayb is the command that is addressed to the believers of the people of Midian, and the command is the command that is the encouragement of the believers, on the report of some of the commentators, and it is also the form of the warning of the disbelievers, on the report of the other commentators. The judgment of God between the believers and the disbelievers is the judgment that is the aiding of the believers and the affirmers of the truth over the disbelievers and the champions of falsehood.

The form of the command of the patience is the form that is on the teaching of the verse 71, in which Hūd commanded the people of ʿĀd to wait, and the form of the command is also in the other verses, in which the Prophet ﷺ is told to respond to the continued denial of the Quraysh in the similar manner (6:158; 9:52; 10:20, 102; 11:122; 20:135; 52:31). The statement of "the best of judges" of the verse is the statement that is in the other verses (10:109; 11:45; 12:80; 95:8), and the statement of "the best of deciders" is in the other verses (6:57; 7:89), and the two statements are the two forms of the same teaching: the judgment of God is the judgment that is the best of the judgments, and the best of the judgments is the best that is the justice of the judgment of God. The logic of the command of the patience is the logic that is worth stating: the believer who is in the minority in the town of the disbelievers is the believer who is in the one who is commanded to the patience, and the patience of the believer is the patience that is the waiting for the judgment of God the confidence of the believer in the victory of the truth. The verse, in its form, is the form of the statement of the division of the people and the command of the patience, and the statement of the division of the people and the command of the patience are the statement of the division of the people and the command of the patience that are in the form of the introduction of the threat of the notables in the next verse.
