## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:88

> **The notables among his people who were arrogant said, “We shall surely expel you, O Shuʿayb, and those who believe along with you from our town, unless you revert to our creed.” He said, “What! Even though we are unwilling?**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Threat of the Expulsion**

The pattern of the notables is the pattern that is on the form of the teaching of the previous verses: the notables who are the arrogant are the notables who are the opposition to the prophet, and the opposition of the notables to the prophet is the opposition that is the threat of the expulsion. The threat of the expulsion of Shuʿayb is the threat that is on the form of the threat of the expulsion of Lūt in verse 82, and the two threats are the two forms of the same teaching: the town of the notables is the town that is the expulsion of the prophet and his believers, and the expulsion of the prophet and his believers is the expulsion that is the last resort of the notables when they cannot win by the argument.

**The Condition of the Reverting**

The threat of the notables is the threat that is the condition: *unless you revert to our creed*. The condition of the reverting is the condition that is the demand of the returning to the creed of the people, and the demand of the returning to the creed of the people is the demand that is the attempt of the notables to make the creed of the people the creed of the prophet. The notables who demand the reverting of the prophet to the creed of the people are the notables who are in the ones who want the prophet to abandon the mission, and the abandonment of the mission of the prophet is the abandonment that is the return to the state of the disbeliever.

**The Astonishment of the Prophet**

The verse closes with the reply of Shuʿayb, and the reply opens with the exclamation of the astonishment: *What! Even though we are unwilling?* The exclamation of the astonishment is the exclamation that is on the form of the exclamation of the astonishment of Lūt in verse 80, and the exclamation is the exclamation that is the prophet who is astonished at the demand of the notables. The phrase "even though we are unwilling" is the phrase that is the statement of the non-willingness of the prophet and his believers to the reverting to the creed of the people, and the non-willingness of the prophet and his believers to the reverting is the non-willingness that is the confirmation of the firmness of the faith of the prophet and his believers. The exclamation of the astonishment is the form of the introduction of the reply of the prophet in the next verse, and the reply of the prophet is the reply that is the complete refutation of the demand of the notables.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:89

> **We would be fabricating a lie against God were we to revert to your creed after God had delivered us from it. It is not for us to revert thereto unless God, our Lord, should will. Our Lord encompasses all things in knowledge. In God do we trust. Our Lord! Decide between us and our people in truth, and Thou art the best of deciders.”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Fabrication of the Lie Against God**

The verse is the reply of Shuʿayb to the demand of the notables, and the reply is the reply that is the complete refutation: *We would be fabricating a lie against God were we to revert to your creed after God had delivered us from it*. The word for "fabricating a lie" is the phrase that the Qur'an identifies as among the greatest of the sins, and the greatest of the sins is the greatest that is the one who does the greater wrong than the one who fabricates a lie against God (6:21, 93, 144; 7:37; 10:17; 11:18; 18:15; 19:68; 61:7). The reverting of Shuʿayb and his believers to the creed of the people, after God had delivered them from it, would be the reverting that is the attribution of the error to God, because they would be the attribution of the error to God the statement that God delivered them from the creed and then delivered them to the creed, and the statement that God delivered them from the creed and then delivered them to the creed is the statement that is the lie against God.

The reverting in this form would also make Shuʿayb and his followers effectively the "apostates," and the apostates are, according to some of the commentators, worse than the disbelievers, because such people are capable of discerning the true religion from the false religion, since they were the followers of the true religion, but they nonetheless have now renounced it and follow what they know to be false. The state of the apostate who is the knowledge of the truth and the following of the falsehood is the state that is the double state of the betrayal, and the double state of the betrayal is the double state that is the betrayal of the truth after the knowledge of the truth.

**The Knowledge of the Lord and the Trust in God**

The reply of Shuʿayb continues with the statement of the knowledge of God, and the statement is the statement that is the encompassing of the knowledge: *Our Lord encompasses all things in knowledge*, on the statement of the other verses (6:80; 20:98; 65:12). The statement of the encompassing of the knowledge is the statement that is the teaching that God is the one who knows all the things, and the one who knows all the things is the one who is the one who is the best of the deciders.

The reply of Shuʿayb continues with the statement of the trust, and the statement is the statement that is the trust in God: *In God do we trust*. The phrase of the trust is the phrase that demonstrates the confidence of Shuʿayb that God would protect and deliver him and his followers from the threats of the disbelievers, and the phrase points to the central spiritual virtue of the trust in God, *tawakkul* (14:11–12; 39:38). The trust in God of the prophet and his believers is the trust that is the response to the threat of the notables, and the response to the threat of the notables is the response that is the teaching that the one who is threatened is the one who is the trust in God rather than the fear of the threat.

**The Request of the Decision**

The reply of Shuʿayb closes with the request of the decision, and the request is the request that is on the form of the statement of verse 87: *Our Lord! Decide between us and our people in truth, and Thou art the best of deciders*. The request of the decision of God between the believers and the disbelievers is the request that is the prayer of the prophet for the judgment of God, and the prayer of the prophet for the judgment of God is the prayer that is the confidence of the prophet in the victory of the truth. The statement of "the best of deciders" of the verse is the statement that is in the other verse (6:57), and the statement is the same statement as the statement of "the best of judges" of verse 87, and the two statements are the two forms of the same teaching, on the form of the teaching of the previous verse. The reply of Shuʿayb is the reply that is in the form of the eloquence of the prophet who is the orator of the prophets, and the eloquence of the reply is the eloquence that is the chain of the statements: the refutation of the demand, the statement of the knowledge of God, the statement of the trust in God, and the request of the decision of God. The verse, in its form, is the form of the reply of Shuʿayb, and the reply of Shuʿayb is the reply that is in the form of the introduction of the warning of the notables to the weak in the next verse.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:90

> **The notables among his people who disbelieved said, “Verily if you follow Shuʿayb, you shall surely be the losers.”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Warning of the Loss**

The verse states the act of the notables who disbelieved, and the act is the act that is the warning of the weak believers: *Verily if you follow Shuʿayb, you shall surely be the losers*. The word for "losers" is *al-khāsirūn*, from the root *k-h-s-r*, and the root carries the sense of the loss, the diminishment, the one who loses the thing. The notables warn that those who follow Shuʿayb will be the losers, meaning those who are deceived with regard to their religion, and that abandoning their native religious practice to follow Shuʿayb will lead to the destruction. The logic of the warning is the logic that is worth stating: the notables who cannot win by the argument of the truth resort to the argument of the loss, and the argument of the loss is the argument that is the application of the language of the commerce to the religion, and the application of the language of the commerce to the religion is the application that is the teaching that the religion is the commerce in the eyes of the notables. The man who follows Shuʿayb in the eyes of the notables is the man who is in the one who makes the loss in the commerce of the religion, and the man who makes the loss in the commerce of the religion is the man who is in the loser.

**The Reversal of the Loss**

The verse is the verse that is the declaration of the loss of the notables, and the declaration of the loss of the notables is the declaration that is reversed in the result of the story. In verse 92, it is those who denied Shuʿayb who were the losers, and the reversal of the loss is the reversal that is the teaching that the man who calls the others the losers is the man who is the loser, and the man who is the loser is the man who is in the one who loses the religion. The logic of the reversal is the logic that is worth stating: the notables who declare the loss of the believers are the notables who are in the ones who lose the religion, and the loss of the religion is the loss that is the true loss, on the form of the teaching that the true loss is the loss of the religion. The verse, in its form, is the form of the warning of the notables, and the warning of the notables is the warning that is in the form of the introduction of the statement of the destruction of the next verse.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:91

> **So the earthquake seized them, and morning found them lying lifeless in their abode.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Same Earthquake**

The verse states the destruction of the people of Midian, and the destruction is the destruction that is identical to the destruction of the people of Thamūd in verse 78. The description of the destruction of the Midianites is identical to the description of the destruction of the Thamūd, and the identity of the descriptions is the identity that is the repetition of the pattern of the destruction of the peoples of the prophets. The earthquake of the verse is the same earthquake, *rajfah*, that seized the people of Thamūd, and the identity of the earthquake is the identity that is the teaching that the destruction of the peoples of the prophets is the destruction that is the repetition of the forms.

**The Heat, the Cloud, and the Cry**

The tradition gives the details of the destruction of the people of Midian, and the details are the details that are worth stating. When the people of Midian denied Shuʿayb, God first sent upon them such a terrible heat that even the shade and the water could provide no relief. God then sent upon them a cloud, bearing the cool, refreshing breezes. When all the people of Midian had gathered beneath this cloud, it unleashed upon them the flame and the fire, followed by a terrible cry and the earthquake that instantly extinguished their spirits and left them suddenly lifeless. The three stages of the destruction are the three stages that are the heat, and the cloud that brings the fire, and the cry and the earthquake. The logic of the cloud that brings the fire is the logic that is on the form of the teaching of the cloud of the people of ʿĀd in the commentary of verse 72: the cloud is the cloud that appears in the form of the mercy, and the cloud that appears in the form of the mercy is the cloud that brings the punishment, and the cloud that brings the punishment is the cloud that is the teaching that the mercy that is sought by the disbeliever is the mercy that is the appearance of the punishment. The two clouds of the people of ʿĀd and the people of Midian are the two forms of the same teaching, and the two forms of the same teaching are the two forms that are the warning of the seeking of the mercy without the faith. The verse, in its form, is the form of the statement of the destruction of the people of Midian, and the statement of the destruction is the statement that is in the form of the introduction of the statement of the reversal of the loss in the next verse.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:92

> **Those who denied Shuʿayb, it was as though they had never dwelt there. Those who denied Shuʿayb, they themselves were the losers.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Complete Effacement**

The verse states the state of the result of the destruction, and the state is the state that is the complete effacement: *Those who denied Shuʿayb, it was as though they had never dwelt there*. The people of Midian were so thoroughly effaced by the earthquake that it was as though they had never dwelt there, and the complete effacement of the people of Midian is the complete effacement that is the teaching that the destruction of the people is the destruction that is the removal of the trace of the people. The logic of the teaching is the logic that is worth stating: the people who are effaced are the people who are in the ones who are the trace of the denial, and the trace of the denial is the trace that is removed by the destruction, and the removal of the trace by the destruction is the removal that is the teaching that the state of the denial does not leave the trace the permanence.

**The Reversal of the Loss**

The verse closes with the statement that is the reversal of the declaration of the notables in verse 90, and the reversal is the reversal that is the spelling out of the irony: *Those who denied Shuʿayb, they themselves were the losers*. The repetition of "those who denied Shuʿayb" is the repetition that is the emphasis of the state of the denial, and the emphasis of the state of the denial is the emphasis that is the statement of the cause of the loss. The logic of the reversal is the logic that is worth stating: the notables who declared the believers the losers are the notables who are in the ones who are the losers, and the state of the losers of the notables is the state that is the loss of the religion, and the loss of the religion is the loss that is the true loss. The man who calls the believers the losers is the man who is in the one who is the loser, and the man who is the loser is the man who is in the one who is destroyed in the destruction. The verse, in its form, is the form of the end of the story of Shuʿayb, and the end of the story of Shuʿayb is the end that is in the form of the introduction of the statement of the turning away of Shuʿayb in the next verse.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:93

> **So he turned away from them and said, “O my people! I indeed delivered unto you the messages of my Lord, and advised you sincerely. So how can I grieve for a disbelieving people?”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The End of the Fifth Story**

The verse is the end of the story of Shuʿayb, and the end is the end that is on the form of the end of the story of Ṣāli in verse 79. The address of Shuʿayb to his people, except for the last sentence, is identical to the address of Ṣāli, and the identity of the addresses is the identity that is the unity of the profile of the prophets. The same question that arose for the address of Ṣāli arises for the address of Shuʿayb, and the question is the question of whether the address was delivered after the people had been thoroughly destroyed, or only after the prophet became certain that their destruction was imminent and unavoidable. The two readings are the two readings that are worth stating, on the form of the teaching of verse 79.

**The Two Declarations and the Question of the Grief**

The verse states the two declarations of Shuʿayb, and the two declarations are the two essential vocations of the prophets of the Qur'an, on the form of the teaching of the previous verses: the delivery of the messages of God, and the sincere advice. The verse adds the question that is the particularity of the address of Shuʿayb: *So how can I grieve for a disbelieving people?* The question of the grief is the rhetorical question that is the statement of the non-grief of the prophet for the disbelieving people. The logic of the question is the logic that is worth stating in full: the prophet who has completed the duty of the delivery and the advice is the prophet who is in the one who has discharged the obligation, and the prophet who has discharged the obligation is the prophet who is in the one who has no remaining duty to the disbelieving people. The grief of the prophet for the disbelieving people is the grief that is the grief of the mercy, and the grief of the mercy of the prophet is the grief that remains with the prophet even after the completion of the duty, but the duty of the prophet is the duty of the delivery and the advice, not the duty of the grief. The question of the verse is the question that is the statement of the completion of the duty, and the statement of the completion of the duty is the statement that is the release of the prophet from the state of the disbelieving people. The verse, in its form, is the form of the end of the story of Shuʿayb, and the end of the story of Shuʿayb is the end that is in the form of the introduction of the new section of the sūrah in the next verse.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:94

> **We sent no prophet to a town but that We seized its people with misfortune and hardship, that haply they would humble themselves.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Opening of the Generalizing Section**

The verse opens the new section of the sūrah, verses 94 through 102, and the section serves as the commentary on and the conclusion to the prophetic narratives of Noah, Hūd, Ṣāliḥ, Lot, and Shuʿayb. The section of the generalization is the section that moves from the particular stories of the particular peoples to the general law of the towns of the prophets, and the movement from the particular to the general is the movement that is the summary of the sūrah of the stories. The statement of the verse is the statement of the law that the Qur'an states the sending of the prophets: *We sent no prophet to a town but that We seized its people with misfortune and hardship, that haply they would humble themselves*.

**The Adversity as the Test**

The logic of the verse is the logic that is worth stating in full. The sending of the prophetic messenger is often accompanied by some form of the adversity for his people as the moral test, and the adversity of the verse is the adversity that is the misfortune and the hardship, and the misfortune and the hardship refer to the poverty, the hunger, the illness, and the various forms of the loss (6:42–44). The purpose of the adversity is the purpose that is stated in the phrase "that haply they would humble themselves," and the purpose of the humility is the purpose that is the turning of the people to God in the state of the adversity. The logic of the teaching is the logic that is worth stating: the people who are seized with the adversity are the people who are in the ones who are given the opportunity of the humility, and the opportunity of the humility is the opportunity that is the turning of the people to God, and the turning of the people to God in the state of the adversity is the turning that is the acknowledgment of the dependence of the people on God. The people who are in the acknowledgment of the dependence on God in the state of the adversity are the people who pass the test of the adversity, and the people who pass the test of the adversity are the people who are in the ones who are guided by the adversity. The verse, in its form, is the form of the opening of the generalizing section, and the opening of the generalizing section is the opening that is in the form of the introduction of the statement of the alternation of the adversity and the ease in the next verse.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:95

> **Then We replaced evil [circumstances] with good, till they multiplied and said, “Hardship and ease visited our fathers [as well].” Then We seized them suddenly, while they were unaware.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Alternation of the Hardship and the Ease**

The verse states the second stage of the general law of the towns of the prophets, and the stage is the stage that is the replacement of the evil with the good: *Then We replaced evil [circumstances] with good*. The alternation of the adversity and the ease is the alternation that is the test of God, and the test of God is the test that is the trying of the people with the good and the bad fortune, in the form of the teaching of verse 168. The good fortune that replaced the earlier adversity continued till the people multiplied, and the multiplication of the people is the multiplication that is the increase in both the wealth and the children, on the report of the commentators, and the two worldly goods of the wealth and the children are the two worldly goods that give the people the false sense of the security with regard to both the vicissitudes of this life and the punishment in the next (34:34–35).

**The Excuse of the Fathers in the New Form**

The verse states the response of the people to the alternation of the hardship and the ease, and the response is the response that is the excuse of the fathers: *Hardship and ease visited our fathers [as well]*. Some of the people fail the test by attributing both the adversity and the ease to the normal course of the earthly life, rather than understanding them as the test or the gift from God, and the attribution of the adversity and the ease to the normal course of the earthly life is the attribution that is the form of the excuse of the fathers in the new form, on the form of the teaching of verse 28 and verse 70. The people who attribute the adversity and the ease to the normal course of the earthly life are the people who are in the ones who become the heedless and the complacent, and the heedlessness and the complacency of the people are the heedlessness and the complacency that are the removal of the consciousness of the test from the state of the life. The logic of the teaching is the logic that is worth stating: the people who are in the excuse of the fathers in the new form are the people who are in the ones who fail the test of the alternation, and the people who fail the test of the alternation are the people who are in the ones who are seized by the punishment in the state of the ease.

**The Sudden Seizing of the Unaware**

The verse closes with the statement of the result, and the result is the result that is the sudden seizing: *Then We seized them suddenly, while they were unaware*. The suddenness of the seizing is the suddenness that is the punishment that comes in the state of the unawareness of the people, and the state of the unawareness of the people is the state that is the heedlessness of the people in the state of the ease. The logic of the suddenness is the logic that is worth stating: the punishment that comes in the state of the unawareness of the people is the punishment that is the punishment that finds the people in the state of the complacency, and the state of the complacency of the people is the state that is the removal of the preparation of the people for the punishment. The people who are seized suddenly while they are unaware are the people who are in the ones who did not prepare for the punishment, and the people who did not prepare for the punishment are the people who are in the ones who are seized by the punishment in the state of the complacency. The section of the generalization of the sūrah continues in the next verse, in the statement of the reward of the grateful people and the punishment of the disbelieving people.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:96

> **Had the people of the towns believed and been reverent, We would surely have opened unto them blessings from Heaven and earth. But they denied, so We seized them for that which they used to earn.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Condition of the Opening of the Blessings**

The verse states the contrast that is the contrast that is the condition of the belief and the taqwā: *Had the people of the towns believed and been reverent, We would surely have opened unto them blessings from Heaven and earth*. The condition of the belief and the taqwā is the condition that is on the form of the condition of verse 2 and verse 57, and the condition is the condition that is the connection of the belief with the taqwā, and the connection of the belief with the taqwā is the connection that is the teaching that the belief is the belief that is the belief with the practice, and the belief with the practice is the belief that is the belief with the taqwā.

**The Blessings of the Heaven and the Earth**

The verse states the result of the condition, and the result is the result that is the opening of the blessings: *We would surely have opened unto them blessings from Heaven and earth*. The commentators understood the opening of the blessings from the heaven and the earth in the forms that are worth stating. The first form is the form that the opening of the heaven is the opening of the heaven to send down the rain, so that the crops would grow abundantly on the earth. The second form is the form that the opening of the blessings is the opening that is metaphorical and by extension, in which God provides them with all manner of good things, including the security and the peace, which can also be understood spiritually to mean the security and the peace for the soul. The two forms of the opening of the blessings are the two forms that are the teaching that the blessing of God is the blessing that is the blessing of the world and the blessing of the soul, and the blessing of the world and the blessing of the soul are the two forms of the blessing that are the fruit of the belief and the taqwā.

**The Seizing for the Earning**

The verse closes with the statement of the result of the denial, and the result is the result that is the seizing: *But they denied, so We seized them for that which they used to earn*. The statement of the "for that which they used to earn" is the statement that is the principle of the proportionality of the punishment to the earning, and the principle of the proportionality of the punishment to the earning is the principle that is the justice of the punishment of God. The logic of the contrast of the verse is the logic that is worth stating: the people of the towns are the people who are in the ones who were given the condition of the belief and the taqwā, and the people who were given the condition of the belief and the taqwā are the people who are in the ones who denied, and the people who denied are the people who are in the ones who are seized for that which they used to earn. The man who is seized for that which he used to earn is the man who is in the one who receives the reward of his own act, and the man who receives the reward of his own act is the man who is in the one who is the object of the justice of God. The verse, in its form, is the form of the statement of the contrast of the condition and the result, and the statement of the contrast is the statement that is in the form of the introduction of the series of the rhetorical questions of the next verses.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:97

> **Did the people of the towns feel secure from Our Might coming upon them by night, while they were sleeping?**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Question of the Security**

The verse opens the series of the rhetorical questions that the sūrah addresses to the people of the destroyed towns, and the series of the questions is the verses 97 through 99. The questions of the series emphasize the foolishness of feeling "secure" in the life of this world, or "secure" from the punishment of God, which may come in either this world or the next. The idea of the questions is found in the other verses, such as 10:7–8, in which the verse states that those who anticipate not the meeting with God, and who are content with the life of this world and feel secure therein, and who are heedless of the signs of God, it is they whose refuge shall be the Fire for that which they used to earn, and 16:45, in which the verse asks whether those who have plotted the evil deeds feel secure that God will not cause the earth to engulf them, or that the punishment will not come upon them whence they are not aware. The two verses are the two forms of the same teaching that the series of the questions states, and the teaching of the two verses is the teaching that is the foolishness of the feeling of the security from the punishment of God.

**The Night of the Sleep**

The question of the verse is the question that is the punishment that comes by the night: *Did the people of the towns feel secure from Our Might coming upon them by night, while they were sleeping?* The punishment that comes by the night is the punishment that is the punishment that finds the people in the state of the sleep, and the state of the sleep of the people is the state that is the unconsciousness and the unawareness of the people. The logic of the question is the logic that is worth stating: the people who feel secure from the punishment by the night are the people who are in the ones who are not prepared for the punishment in the state of the sleep, and the people who are not prepared for the punishment in the state of the sleep are the people who are in the ones who are seized by the punishment in the state of the unconsciousness. The punishment that comes by the night is the punishment that is the punishment that finds the people in the state of the removal of the preparation, and the state of the removal of the preparation of the people is the state that is the heedlessness of the people. The verse, in its form, is the form of the first question of the series, and the first question of the series is the question that is in the form of the introduction of the second question of the next verse.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:98

> **Or did the people of the towns feel secure from Our Might coming upon them in broad daylight, while they were playing?**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Day of the Play**

The verse is the second question of the series, and the question is the question that is the punishment that comes in the broad daylight: *Or did the people of the towns feel secure from Our Might coming upon them in broad daylight, while they were playing?* The punishment that comes in the broad daylight is the punishment that is the punishment that finds the people in the state of the play, and the state of the play of the people is the state that is the distraction of the people by their engagement with the affairs of this world. The play of the people is the play that is the affairs of this world, which is itself described as the mere play and the diversion in the other verses (6:32; 29:64; 47:36; 57:20). The teaching of the play of the verse is the teaching that is on the form of the teaching of verse 51 of the sūrah, in which the life of this world is described as the play and the diversion, and the two teachings are the two forms of the same teaching: the play of the life of this world is the play that is the distraction of the people from the remembrance of the punishment.

**The Two States of the Heedlessness**

The two questions of the series are the two questions that are the two states of the heedlessness of the people: the state of the sleep of the night, and the state of the play of the day. The commentary of al-Rāzī states that God will send down the punishment upon such disbelievers when they are in the greatest state of the heedlessness, and the greatest state of the heedlessness of the people is the greatest state that is the state of the sleep and the state of the play, and the state of the sleep and the state of the play are the two states that are the removal of the consciousness of the people from the punishment of God. The logic of the teaching is the logic that is worth stating: the people who are in the state of the greatest heedlessness are the people who are in the ones who are seized by the punishment in the state of the greatest heedlessness, and the people who are seized by the punishment in the state of the greatest heedlessness are the people who are in the ones who are the objects of the wisdom of the punishment of God, and the wisdom of the punishment of God is the wisdom that is the punishment that comes in the state of the unawareness of the people. The verse, in its form, is the form of the second question of the series, and the second question of the series is the question that is in the form of the introduction of the third question of the next verse.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:99

> **Did they feel secure from God’s plotting? None feels secure from God’s plotting, save the people who are losers.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Plotting of God**

The verse is the third question of the series, and the question is the question that is the plotting of God: *Did they feel secure from God’s plotting?* The word for "plotting" is *makr*, and the word in the Qur'an refers to the ultimate control of God over the outcome of all the events, and the word is frequently juxtaposed with the futility of the human "plotting" by the comparison (3:54; 10:21; 13:42; 14:46; 16:26; 27:50). The plotting of God of the verse may refer specifically to His seizing them with the punishment while they were unaware, on the statement of verse 95. The logic of the question is the logic that is worth stating: the people who feel secure from the plotting of God are the people who are in the ones who do not acknowledge the control of God over the events of their lives, and the people who do not acknowledge the control of God over the events of their lives are the people who are in the ones who are the objects of the plotting of God. The man who does not acknowledge the control of God over the events of his life is the man who is in the one who is seized by the punishment in the state of the unawareness, and the man who is seized by the punishment in the state of the unawareness is the man who is in the one who is the object of the plotting of God.

**The Losers Who Feel Secure**

The verse closes with the statement that is the form of the reversal of the teaching: *None feels secure from God’s plotting, save the people who are losers*. The word for "losers" is the same word that the sūrah used in verse 90 and verse 92, and the repetition of the word is the repetition that is the emphasis of the state of the loss. The logic of the reversal is the logic that is worth stating in full: the feeling of the security from the plotting of God is the feeling that is the mark of the losers, and the mark of the losers is the mark that is the feeling of the security from God. The man who feels secure from the plotting of God is the man who is in the one who makes himself the loser by the feeling of the security from God, and the man who makes himself the loser by the feeling of the security from God is the man who is in the one who is the loser. The three questions of the series are the three questions that are the teaching that the feeling of the security from the punishment of God is the feeling that is the state of the losers, and the state of the losers is the state that is the loss of the religion and the loss of the world. The verse, in its form, is the form of the end of the series of the questions, and the end of the series of the questions is the end that is in the form of the introduction of the statement of the inheritance of the earth in the next verse.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:100

> **Does it not serve as guidance unto those who inherited the earth after its [earlier] inhabitants that, if We willed, We could smite them for their sins and set a seal upon their hearts such that they would not hear?**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Inheritance of the Earth**

The verse states the application of the stories of the destroyed peoples to the people who inherit the earth after them, and the application is the application that is the warning: *Does it not serve as guidance unto those who inherited the earth after its [earlier] inhabitants?* The word for "inherited" is the word that carries the sense of the inheritance, the succession, the one who comes after the one who is destroyed. The people who inherit the earth after the earlier inhabitants are the people who are the chain of the succession of the peoples, on the form of the teaching of verse 69 and verse 74. The logic of the inheritance is the logic that is worth stating: the people who inherit the earth are the people who are in the ones who receive the earth in the state of the removal of the people before them, and the people who receive the earth in the state of the removal of the people before them are the people who are in the ones who are the successors of the people before them, and the successors of the people before them are the successors who are the ones who are bound to the fate of the people before them, on the condition of the denial of the prophet. The stories of the past peoples and their destruction on account of their sins and their rejection of their messengers are meant as the warning for all the later peoples who might be similarly destroyed for their sins. In the context of the life of the Prophet ﷺ, the warning was perhaps directed specifically at the Quraysh, since, like the peoples of Noah, Hūd, Ṣāliḥ, Lot, and Shuʿayb before them, they rejected their prophet, Muhammad ﷺ, and drove him from Makkah, his native city.

**The Smite and the Seal**

The verse states the two acts that God states the willingness: *if We willed, We could smite them for their sins and set a seal upon their hearts such that they would not hear*. The first act is the act of the smiting for the sins, and the smiting for the sins is the smiting that is the punishment of the sins of the people. The second act is the act of the setting of the seal upon the hearts, and the setting of the seal upon the hearts is the setting that is the removal of the hearing and the understanding of the religious truth, and the removal of the hearing and the understanding of the religious truth is the punishment particularly for the wrongdoing or the disbelief, and the teaching is found in the other verses (2:7; 4:155; 6:46; 9:87, 93; 10:74; 16:108; 30:59; 40:35; 42:24; 45:23; 47:16; 63:3). The logic of the seal is the logic that is worth stating: the seal upon the heart is the seal that is the consequence of the denial of the people, not the cause of the denial of the people, and the consequence of the denial of the people is the consequence that is the removal of the ability of the people to hear and to understand the truth. The man who denies the truth is the man who is in the one who is sealed by the seal of the consequence of the denial, and the man who is sealed by the seal of the consequence of the denial is the man who is in the one who is removed from the hearing and the understanding of the truth. The verse, in its form, is the form of the statement of the application of the stories to the inheritors of the earth, and the statement of the application is the statement that is in the form of the introduction of the statement of the summary of the stories in the next verse.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:101

> **These are the towns whose stories We have recounted unto thee. Their messengers certainly brought them clear proofs, but they would not believe in what they had denied earlier. Thus does God set a seal upon the hearts of the disbelievers.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Summary of the Stories**

The verse is the summary of the section of the stories of the sūrah, verses 59 through 93, and the summary is the summary that is on the form of the similar verse (11:100), in which a nearly identical verse concludes a similar series of the prophetic narratives of the earthly destruction. The statement of the stories of the towns is the statement that is the five towns of the prophets of the section: the town of Noah, the town of Hūd, the town of Ṣāliḥ, the town of Lot, and the town of Shuʿayb. The statement of the "recounted unto thee" is the statement that is addressed to the Prophet ﷺ most specifically, and the statement is the statement that is the two purposes of the recounting. The first purpose is the purpose that the Prophet ﷺ would be assured that God aids His prophets and the believers against their enemies in this world, as well as rewarding them in the next. The second purpose is the purpose that the Prophet ﷺ might warn his own people, the Quraysh, of the fate that awaited them if they persisted in disbelief.

**The Clear Proofs and the Earlier Denial**

The verse states the two statements of the messengers and the people, and the two statements are the two statements that are the clear proofs and the denial. The statement of the clear proofs is the statement that is on the form of the statement of verse 73 and verse 85, and the clear proofs of the messengers are the clear proofs that are the evidences of the prophethood and the divine origin of the message. The statement of the denial of the people is the statement that is the "they would not believe in what they had denied earlier." The commentators gave the three readings of the statement, and the three readings are the three readings that are worth stating. The first reading is the reading that the statement refers to the making of the pretemporal covenant with all the children of Adam, in which all the human beings testified to the lordship of God, in the form of the teaching of verse 172, and the denial of the people was manifested even at the moment of the pretemporal covenant, in which they bore witness to the lordship of God only reluctantly and insincerely, or their denial was manifest at the pretemporal event insofar as their ultimate moral status as the believers or the disbelievers was already known to God at that time. The second reading is the reading that the statement refers to the stubborn and the unrelenting disbelief of most of the people of the towns, for whom no amount of the warning, the preaching, or even the evidentiary miracles could bring them to accept the messengers sent to them, and their persisting in their state of the denial and the disbelief until the death. The third reading is the reading that the statement might be read transhistorically, to mean that the disbelievers of any generation will not believe in what those like them among the previous peoples had denied earlier.

**The Seal of the Hearts**

The verse closes with the statement of the seal, and the statement is the statement that is on the form of the statement of verse 100: *Thus does God set a seal upon the hearts of the disbelievers*. The seal of the hearts of the disbelievers is the seal that is the teaching that some will never believe, on the form of the teaching of the other verses (2:7; 4:155). The logic of the seal of the verse is the logic that is the consequence of the denial, on the form of the teaching of the previous verse: the seal is the consequence of the denial, not the cause of the denial, and the consequence of the denial is the consequence that is the removal of the ability of the disbelievers to hear and to understand the truth. The verse, in its form, is the form of the summary of the section of the stories, and the summary of the section of the stories is the summary that is in the form of the introduction of the statement of the pact of the next verse.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:102

> **We did not find most of them [faithful to their] pact. Indeed, We found most of them to be iniquitous.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Pact That Was Not Kept**

The verse is the final statement of the summary of the section of the stories, and the statement is the statement that is the pact: *We did not find most of them [faithful to their] pact*. The phrase is the idiomatic rendering of the Arabic, which literally reads that God found no pact for most of them. The commentators understood the statement as the statement that most of the people were not faithful to their pact with God, and al-Rāzī noted that in being unfaithful to their pact with God, it is as if they had made no pact at all with God. The "most of them" of the verse may refer to most of those among the peoples of Noah, Hūd, Ṣāli, Lot, and Shuʿayb, namely the majority of them who rejected their messengers and the warnings they brought, and may also refer to most of the human beings in general. The "pact" of the verse may be understood as the reference to the pretemporal covenant that God makes with all of the humanity, requiring them to bear witness to His lordship, in the form of the teaching of verse 172. The statement of the non-keeping of the pact is the statement that is the teaching that the people did not continue to recognize the lordship of God and His claim to their obedience and worship during the course of their earthly lives. The logic of the teaching is the logic that is worth stating in full: the people who are in the acknowledgment of the lordship of God at the moment of the pretemporal covenant are the people who are in the ones who are bound to the keeping of the acknowledgment during the course of the earthly life, and the people who do not keep the acknowledgment during the course of the earthly life are the people who are in the ones who are not faithful to their pact, and the people who are not faithful to their pact are the people who are in the ones who are found iniquitous by God.

**The Iniquity of Most**

The verse closes with the statement of the iniquity, and the statement is the statement that is the finding of the iniquity of most of the people: *Indeed, We found most of them to be iniquitous*. The statement of the iniquity of most of the people is the statement that is consistent with the many other verses of the Qur'an that state that most of the people are ungrateful (2:243; 10:60; 12:38; 27:63; 40:61), and are unaware of the spiritual truths, and are not the believers. In Sūrah 26, which contains the discussion of the past prophets and their struggles with their peoples similar to the discussion found in the present sūrah, the statement that most of the humanity does not believe functions as something of a refrain throughout the sūrah. Even without the specific or the explicit reference to the pretemporal covenant, most of the commentators understand the statement of the non-keeping of the pact to mean that most of the people fail to act upon the intrinsic knowledge and the moral responsibility that God has given them regarding the necessity of worshipping only the One God and abandoning the idolatry, being obedient to His messengers, being reverent, obeying His laws, being thankful for one's blessings, and performing the virtuous deeds while avoiding the abominable ones. The logic of the summary of the section is the logic that is worth stating: the people who are found iniquitous by God are the people who are in the ones who fail the test of the life, and the people who fail the test of the life are the people who are in the ones who are the objects of the destruction of the towns of the prophets. The verse, in its form, is the form of the end of the section of the stories, and the end of the section of the stories is the end that is in the form of the introduction of the story of Moses and Pharaoh in the next verse.

## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:103

> **Then after them We sent Moses with Our signs unto Pharaoh and his notables, but they treated them wrongfully; so behold how the workers of corruption fared in the end.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Opening of the Story of Moses**

The verse opens the lengthy segment of the sūrah, verses 103 through 156, concerning the prophet Moses, who was sent after the previous prophets mentioned in verses 59 through 93. The phrase "after them" of the verse is the phrase that is the succession of the prophets, and the succession of the prophets of the verse is the succession that is the chain of the messengers of the sūrah. The other narrative accounts of the life of Moses are in 5:20–26, 10:75–93, 18:60–82, 20:9–97, 26:10–66, 27:7–14, 28:3–46, 40:23–45, 43:46–56, and 79:15–25, and the other accounts are the other forms of the story of Moses in the sūrahs of the Qur'an.

**The Notables of Pharaoh**

The verse states the act of the people of Pharaoh, and the act is the act that is on the form of the teaching of the previous stories: the opposition to Moses is led, in part, by the notables among the people to whom he was sent, on the form of the teaching of verse 60, verse 75, and verse 88–90. The notables are guilty of having treated the signs of God wrongfully, by disbelieving in them or by turning the others away from them, and the notables are also the workers of corruption, on the form of the teaching of verse 56. The command of the "behold" of the verse is addressed to the Prophet ﷺ, indicating that the prophetic narratives were meant to give him the confidence that God aids His messengers against their enemies, and the confidence of the Prophet ﷺ is the confidence that is the consolation of the prophet in the face of the rejection of his people. The verse, in its form, is the form of the opening of the story of Moses, and the opening of the story of Moses is the opening that is in the form of the introduction of the claim of the messenger in the next verse.

## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:104

> **And Moses said, “O Pharaoh! I am truly a messenger from the Lord of the worlds,**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Claim of the Messenger**

The verse states the claim of Moses to Pharaoh, and the claim is the claim that is the self-identification of the messenger: *O Pharaoh! I am truly a messenger from the Lord of the worlds*. The claim of Moses is the claim that is on the form of the claim of the other prophets of the sūrah, in which Noah and Hūd similarly identify themselves as the messengers from the Lord of the worlds (7:61; 7:68), and the claim is also on the form of the identical verse in the other sūrah (43:46). The title "the Lord of the worlds" is the title for God that is found throughout the Qur'an, beginning with the opening sūrah (1:2), and the title is the title that is the universal lordship of God over the worlds.

The logic of the claim is the logic that is worth stating in full: the claim of the messenger is the claim that is the establishment of the authority of the messenger, and the establishment of the authority of the messenger is the establishment that is the reference of the messenger to the Lord of the worlds. The man who claims to be the messenger of the Lord of the worlds is the man who claims to be the messenger of the lordship that is the lordship that exceeds the lordship of the king, and the lordship that exceeds the lordship of the king is the lordship that is the lordship of the worlds. The claim of Moses to Pharaoh is the claim that is the confrontation of the two lordships: the lordship of the prophet who is the messenger of the Lord of the worlds, and the lordship of Pharaoh who claims to be the lord of the people of Egypt. The two lordships are the two lordships that are the confrontation of the story of Moses and Pharaoh, and the confrontation of the two lordships is the confrontation that is the heart of the story of the sūrah. God also identifies Himself to Moses as the Lord of the worlds when the latter first encounters Him and receives his prophetic vocation (28:30), and the identification of God to Moses is the identification that is the confirmation of the claim of Moses in the next verse.

## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:105

> **obligated to speak naught about God save the truth. I have brought you a clear proof from your Lord; so send forth with me the Children of Israel.”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Truthfulness of the Messenger**

The verse continues the claim of Moses, and the continuation is the continuation that is the statement of the truthfulness: *obligated to speak naught about God save the truth*. According to one account, after Moses articulated his claim to be a messenger from the Lord of the worlds, Pharaoh replied that he was lying, and in the present verse Moses responds to that charge by indicating that he would speak naught about God save the truth. The statement of the truthfulness of the messenger is the statement that is on the form of the statement of the other verse (7:169), in which the speaking truthfully about God is the condition of the covenant of the Israelites with God. The logic of the statement is the logic that is worth stating: the messenger who speaks naught about God save the truth is the messenger who is the one who is bound to the truth in the speech about God, and the one who is bound to the truth in the speech about God is the one who is the one who is the object of the trust of the people, and the one who is the object of the trust of the people is the one who is the one who is the messenger.

**The Clear Proof and the Demand of the Release**

The verse states the claim of the clear proof, and the claim is the claim that is on the form of the claim of verse 73 and verse 85. The clear proof that Moses refers to is the miracles that he would subsequently perform, as well as the force of his call to Pharaoh to accept the message he brings and to send forth the Children of Israel, that is, to free them from servitude and allow them to return to the Holy Land. The demand of the release of the Children of Israel is the demand that is the central demand of the mission of Moses in the sūrah, and the demand of the release of the Children of Israel is the demand that is the liberation of the people from the servitude of Pharaoh, and the liberation of the people from the servitude of Pharaoh is the liberation that is the return of the people to the Holy Land. The logic of the demand is the logic that is worth stating: the messenger who demands the release of the people is the messenger who is the one who demands the obedience to God rather than the obedience to the king, and the demand of the obedience to God rather than the demand of the obedience to the king is the demand that is the confrontation of the two lordships, on the form of the teaching of the previous verse. The verse, in its form, is the form of the claim of the messenger, and the claim of the messenger is the claim that is in the form of the introduction of the request of the sign of Pharaoh in the next verse.

## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:106

> **He said, “If you have brought a sign, then bring it forth, if you are among the truthful.”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Request of the Sign**

The verse states the reply of Pharaoh to the claim of Moses, and the reply is the reply that is the request of the sign: *If you have brought a sign, then bring it forth, if you are among the truthful*. Pharaoh asks Moses for the sign, that is, the proof of the truth of the message he brings, or the proof of God Himself. The logic of the request is the logic that is worth stating in full: the request of the sign of Pharaoh is the request that is different from the request of the sign of the people of ʿĀd and the people of Thamūd, and the difference is the difference that is the request of the punishment rather than the request of the sign. The people of ʿĀd and the people of Thamūd asked the prophet to bring the punishment that the prophet threatened, and Pharaoh asks the prophet to bring the sign that confirms the message of the prophet. The two requests are the two forms of the disbelief, and the two forms of the disbelief are the two forms that are the disbelief that seeks the confirmation rather than the disbelief that seeks the destruction. The logic of the request of the sign of Pharaoh is the logic that is on the form of the teaching of the request of the sign in general: the one who requests the sign is the one who is the danger of the request, on the form of the teaching of the commentary of verse 73, and the one who requests the sign the arrogance is the one who is the one who is bound to the danger of the request. The verse, in its form, is the form of the request of the sign of Pharaoh, and the request of the sign of Pharaoh is the request that is in the form of the introduction of the statement of the first sign in the next verse.

## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:107

> **So he cast his staff and, behold, it was a serpent manifest.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Sign of the Serpent**

The verse states the first sign of Moses, and the sign is the sign that is the casting of the staff: *So he cast his staff and, behold, it was a serpent manifest*. The two evidentiary miracles that Moses produces in response to the request of Pharaoh are the two miracles that are on the form of the statement of the other sūrah (26:32–33): his thrown staff transforms into a serpent, and his hand turns white. The two acts of the miracles were the acts in which Moses had been previously instructed by God (20:18–22; 27:10–12; 28:30–32). The serpent produced from the staff of Moses was said to be of the supernaturally large size, and to have frightened Pharaoh from his throne. The logic of the sign of the serpent is the logic that is worth stating: the serpent is the serpent that is the fear of the people, and the serpent that is the fear of the people is the serpent that is the sign that demonstrates the power of God over the natural order, and the power of God over the natural order is the power that is the teaching that the natural order is the natural order that is the order of God.

**The Staff of Adam**

The tradition gives the detail of the staff of Moses, and the detail is the detail that is worth stating. According to a legendary account, the staff had been the staff of Adam, and was bequeathed to the successive prophets, including Shuʿayb, the father-in-law of Moses, until it reached Moses himself. The detail of the staff of Adam is the detail that is the connection of the staff of Moses with the staff of Shuʿayb, and the connection of the staff of Moses with the staff of Shuʿayb is the connection that is the family relationship of the two prophets, and the family relationship of the two prophets is the relationship that is the teaching that the prophets of the Qur'an are the prophets that are the chain of the prophets. When Moses speaks with God for the first time, God questions Moses about his staff, and Moses later uses the staff to miraculously produce the twelve streams of water from the rock, in the form of the teaching of the other verses (2:60; 7:160), and to part the sea, in the form of the teaching of the other sūrah (26:63). The logic of the staff is the logic that is worth stating: the staff of Moses is the staff that is the instrument of the signs of God, and the instrument of the signs of God is the instrument that is the teaching that the instrument of the sign is the instrument that is the sign in the hand of the prophet, and the sign in the hand of the prophet is the sign that is the power of God the staff. The verse, in its form, is the form of the statement of the first sign of Moses, and the statement of the first sign is the statement that is in the form of the introduction of the statement of the second sign in the next verse.

## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:108

> **And he drew forth his hand and, behold, it was white to the onlookers.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Sign of the White Hand**

The verse states the second sign of Moses, and the sign is the sign that is the drawing forth of the hand: *And he drew forth his hand and, behold, it was white to the onlookers*. Some of the commentators note that it was the request of Pharaoh for the second sign that prompted Moses to put his hand into the hollow or the sleeve of his cloak and then to draw it forth as white as snow. The phrase of the "white to the onlookers" of the verse is the phrase that is the whiteness of the hand that is seen by the onlookers, and the whiteness of the hand that is seen by the onlookers is the whiteness that is the light of the hand, and the light of the hand is the light that is the sign that demonstrates the purity and the light of the hand of the prophet.

The logic of the two signs is the logic that is worth stating in full: the two signs of Moses are the two signs that correspond to the two arts of the sorcerers of Egypt, and the two arts of the sorcerers of Egypt are the two arts that are the manipulation of the staffs and the ropes, and the manipulation of the staffs and the ropes. The sign of the serpent of Moses is the sign that answers the art of the staffs and the ropes of the sorcerers, and the sign of the white hand of Moses is the sign that answers the art of the sleight of hand of the sorcerers. The two signs of Moses are the two signs that are the signs that demonstrate the power of God over the two arts of the sorcerers, and the power of God over the two arts of the sorcerers is the power that is the teaching that the sign of God is the sign that is the sign that transcends the art of the sorcerers, and the sign that transcends the art of the sorcerers is the sign that is the proof of the prophethood of Moses. The verse, in its form, is the form of the statement of the second sign of Moses, and the statement of the second sign is the statement that is in the form of the introduction of the statement of the accusation of the notables in the next verse.

## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:109

> **The notables among Pharaoh’s people said, “Truly this is a knowledgeable sorcerer.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Accusation of the Sorcery**

The verse states the reaction of the notables of the people of Pharaoh to the signs of Moses, and the reaction is the reaction that is the accusation of the sorcery: *Truly this is a knowledgeable sorcerer*. The notables consider Moses to be the knowledgeable sorcerer, on the form of the statement of the other sūrah (26:34), in which it is Pharaoh himself who describes Moses as the knowledgeable sorcerer to his notables. The accusation of the sorcery of the notables is the accusation that is the deflection of the notables from the question of the truth of the signs to the question of the cause of the signs, and the deflection of the notables from the question of the truth to the question of the cause is the deflection that is the teaching that the sign that is the miracle is the sign that is deflected by the notables into the sorcery, and the deflection of the sign into the sorcery is the deflection that is the refusal of the notables to the prophethood of Moses. The logic of the accusation is the logic that is worth stating: the notables who accuse the prophet of the sorcery are the notables who are in the ones who are the experts in the art of the sorcery, and the notables who are the experts in the art of the sorcery are the notables who are in the ones who know the difference between the sorcery and the miracle, and the notables who know the difference between the sorcery and the miracle are the notables who are in the ones who accuse the prophet of the sorcery the refusal of the knowledge rather than the refusal of the truth. The accusation of the sorcery is the accusation that is repeated by the people of the prophet in the other verses of the Qur'an, including the accusation of the sorcery against the Prophet Muhammad ﷺ and against Jesus (26:34; 27:13; 28:36; 40:24; 51:39; 61:6; 74:24), and the repetition of the accusation is the repetition that is the teaching that the accusation of the sorcery is the accusation that is the constant response of the notables to the signs of the prophets.

## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:110

> **He desires to expel you from your land; so what do you command?”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Charge of the Expulsion**

The verse continues the statement of the notables, and the continuation is the continuation that is the charge of the expulsion: *He desires to expel you from your land; so what do you command?* The charge of the notables is the charge that is the accusation that Moses seeks to expel Pharaoh from his land, and the accusation of the expelling of Pharaoh from his land is the accusation that is the reversal of the teaching: Moses is the one who demands the release of the Children of Israel from the land of Egypt, and the notables reverse the teaching and accuse Moses of seeking to expel Pharaoh from his land. The logic of the reversal is the logic that is worth stating: the notables who accuse the prophet of seeking to expel the king are the notables who are in the ones who reverse the truth, and the notables who reverse the truth are the notables who are in the ones who make the demand of the release of the people the demand of the expulsion of the king, and the demand of the expulsion of the king is the demand that is the charge that unites the people of Pharaoh against Moses. The question of the notables, "so what do you command," is the question that is the seeking of the advice of the notables, and the seeking of the advice of the notables is the seeking that is the teaching that the king who consults the notables is the king who is the one who is the object of the advice of the notables. The verse, in its form, is the form of the statement of the charge of the notables, and the statement of the charge of the notables is the statement that is in the form of the introduction of the suggestion of the notables in the next verse.

## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:111

> **They said, “Put him and his brother off for a while, and send marshalers to the cities**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Suggestion of the Detainment**

The verse states the suggestion of the notables to Pharaoh, and the suggestion is the suggestion that is the two parts: the detaining of Moses and his brother, and the sending of the marshalers to the cities. The first part of the suggestion is the suggestion that Pharaoh put Moses and his brother Aaron off for a while, and the suggestion of the detaining of Moses and his brother is understood to be the suggestion that Pharaoh detain them by either delaying their departure or imprisoning them. The suggestion of the detaining of Moses and his brother is the suggestion that is the attempt of the notables to remove the prophet from the presence of Pharaoh, and the attempt of the notables to remove the prophet from the presence of Pharaoh is the attempt that is the attempt of the notables to weaken the position of the prophet.

**The Sending of the Marshalers**

The second part of the suggestion is the suggestion that Pharaoh send the marshalers to the cities, and the sending of the marshalers to the cities is the sending that is the seeking of the sorcerers of the cities, on the form of the continuation of the verse in the next verse. The reaction of the notables of the verse sets up the competition between Moses and the sorcerers of Egypt, and the competition between Moses and the sorcerers of Egypt is also recounted in the other sūrahs (20:58–70; 26:36–48). The Bible provides the very short account of the competition with the Egyptian sorcerers, in which it is Aaron who casts the staff. The story of Moses and the sorcerers of the Qur'an provides the meditation on the relationship between the prophethood, the miracles, and the sorcery, and the differences between the miracles and the sorcery with regard to their causality, power, and reality. The logic of the teaching is the logic that is worth stating: the miracles of the prophets are often dismissed as the mere sorcery by the detractors, and have no effect upon certain of the disbelievers, and it seems that the primary function of these miracles is to encourage the belief among those who are already inwardly disposed to it, like the sorcerers in this passage, as well as those who are wavering between the belief and the disbelief. The miracles, like the prophetic messages themselves, serve to distinguish the inherently good, but merely misguided, members of a disbelieving community from those, like Pharaoh, whose hearts were so hardened as to be impervious to any prophetic sign or message. The verse, in its form, is the form of the first part of the suggestion of the notables, and the first part of the suggestion is the part that is in the form of the introduction of the second part of the suggestion in the next verse.

## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:112

> **to bring you every knowledgeable sorcerer.”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Bringing of the Sorcerers**

The verse is the second part of the suggestion of the notables, and the second part is the second part that is the bringing of the sorcerers: *to bring you every knowledgeable sorcerer*. The call for the sorcerers to challenge Moses is also mentioned in the other verses (10:79; 26:36–67), and the call of the sorcerers is the call that is the response of the notables to the signs of Moses. According to one report, the marshaled sorcerers boast of their superior skill in the manipulation of the staffs, the ropes, and the snakes, and the other reports indicate that the marshalers sought out the sorcerers capable of the feats similar to those produced by Moses. The logic of the bringing of the sorcerers is the logic that is worth stating: the notables who bring the sorcerers are the notables who are in the ones who seek to defeat the prophet by the art of the sorcerers, and the notables who seek to defeat the prophet by the art of the sorcerers are the notables who are in the ones who know that the art of the sorcerers is the art that is the art that cannot defeat the miracle of God. The man who seeks to defeat the miracle of God by the art of the sorcerers is the man who is in the one who is defeated by the miracle of God, and the man who is defeated by the miracle of God is the man who is in the one who is the object of the wisdom of God. The verse, in its form, is the form of the second part of the suggestion of the notables, and the second part of the suggestion is the part that is in the form of the introduction of the statement of the arrival of the sorcerers in the next verse.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:113

> **And the sorcerers came unto Pharaoh. They said, “We shall surely have a reward if it is we who are victorious.”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Demand of the Reward**

The verse states the arrival of the sorcerers to Pharaoh, and the arrival is the arrival that is the response of the sorcerers to the call of the notables. The sorcerers seek the reward if they are victorious over Moses, and the seeking of the reward of the sorcerers is the seeking that is the worldly desire of the reward, and the worldly desire of the reward of the sorcerers is the worldly desire that is the seeking of the reward the victory over Moses. The logic of the seeking of the reward is the logic that is worth stating: the sorcerers who seek the reward are the sorcerers who are in the ones who condition the participation in the competition upon the reward, and the sorcerers who condition the participation upon the reward are the sorcerers who are in the ones who are the objects of the promise of Pharaoh in the next verse.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:114

> **He said, “Yes, and indeed you shall be among those brought nigh.”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Promise of the Bringing Nigh**

The verse states the reply of Pharaoh to the demand of the sorcerers, and the reply is the reply that is the promise of the reward: *Yes, and indeed you shall be among those brought nigh*. The word for "brought nigh" is the same word that is used elsewhere in the Qur'an for the prophets, the angels, and the most righteous who are rewarded with the highest Paradise (56:10–11, 88; 83:21, 28). The promise of Pharaoh that the sorcerers shall be among those brought nigh is the promise that is identical to the description used elsewhere in the Qur'an for the most righteous, and the identity of the promise is the identity that is the ambiguity of the language. Although the verse, on the literal level, articulates the worldly desire for the reward on the part of the sorcerers and Pharaoh's worldly manner of fulfilling it, the ambiguity of the language suggests that the sorcerers, and Pharaoh unwittingly but presciently, utter the truths that will come to be realized in a manner other than the one they envision. The sorcerers will be defeated by Moses, but their subsequent submission to God and to Moses can be understood as the spiritual "victory," for which they will surely have a reward with God. The verses 124 through 126 suggest that the sorcerers were the martyrs for their belief, and may therefore be among those brought nigh in the Hereafter. The logic of the teaching is the logic that is worth stating: the king who promises the bringing nigh the worldly honor is the king who is in the one who unwittingly promises the bringing nigh the spiritual reward, and the king who unwittingly promises the spiritual reward is the king who is in the one who is the object of the wisdom of God, and the wisdom of God is the wisdom that is the turning of the promise of the king into the promise of God. The verse, in its form, is the form of the promise of Pharaoh, and the promise of Pharaoh is the promise that is in the form of the introduction of the statement of the arrival of the day of the competition in the next verse.

---
## Sūrah al-Aʿrāf 7:115

> **They said, “O Moses! either you cast, or we will be the ones who cast.”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Choice of the Casting**

The verse states the demand of the sorcerers to Moses on the day of the competition, and the demand is the demand that is the choice of the casting: *O Moses! either you cast, or we will be the ones who cast*. Some of the commentators indicate that the sorcerers demonstrated the proper etiquette by allowing Moses to choose who should cast the staff first, even as they clearly preferred to cast first. The logic of the etiquette is the logic that is worth stating: the sorcerers who allow the prophet to choose who should cast first are the sorcerers who are in the ones who demonstrate the etiquette the preference of the casting first, and the sorcerers who demonstrate the etiquette in the form of the preference of the casting first are the sorcerers who are in the ones who possess the inner sincerity that allowed them to perceive Moses as the person of the noble character, deserving of respect. Some of the commentators propose that it is as the reward for their graciousness and proper demeanor toward Moses that God causes the faith to enter their hearts, so that they ultimately come to be the believers in God and in the prophethood of Moses. The verse, in its form, is the form of the demand of the sorcerers, and the demand of the sorcerers is the demand that is in the form of the introduction of the reply of Moses in the next verse.
